# Design/build deviation ledger — P04

Decision: 2026-09-13. User selected **Option A: intended design authority plus a small deviation ledger**.

## Authority and change control

The [GDD](../design-godot-v7.md) and its explicitly adopted decision contracts define **intended behavior**. More specific, explicitly adopted resolutions supersede the historical rule they identify. A silent discrepancy between two current design sources is unresolved documentation, not permission to pick either version; reconcile it with the accepted decision and update the mirrors.

Verified code, resource data and scene/configuration bindings at a **pinned game revision/build** describe current implementation. They are evidence about that build, not authority to reverse an approved target design. Reading a resource proves its stored value, not that the game loads or applies it correctly; implementation and runtime claims need corresponding evidence.

When target and verified build differ, record the discrepancy below, implement/validate the accepted behavior or obtain an explicit design change. An intentional deviation stays open or explicitly deferred with its reason and acceptance condition; it does not become the new target merely because it shipped. Unknown/unavailable implementation stays **unverified**, not automatically compliant or defective.

Where the target deliberately leaves a number provisional or unspecified, record that fact. Do not invent a requirement or silently promote an unverified resource into the intended design. Later approval updates the GDD, contracts, mirrors and relevant [consistency checks](../scripts/README.md) together.

## Confirmed current-build deviations

**Superseded 2026-09-13 by the Package 11 closeout.** When this section was written no pinned game source or build was available to this docs workspace. That is no longer true: Package 11 was implemented directly in the game repository, nine reconnaissance dossiers read the build in full at commit `31fed14`, and twenty workstreams verified their own areas against it. The confirmed entries are in the Package 11 table below; the historical-lead table that follows is dispositioned rather than deleted.

For each future confirmed entry, record:
- Stable deviation ID, affected system/hero/level/mode and current state: confirmed-open, explicitly deferred, or resolved.
- Intended behavior with accepted decision/date and document reference.
- Actual observed behavior/value with pinned game commit/build, file/scene/resource identifiers and dated reproduction/test evidence.
- Owner when assigned; otherwise **Unassigned**. Do not invent a person, date or delivery commitment.
- Resolution path and concrete acceptance criteria, including content coverage/migration/balance where required; link explicit approval for any target change.
- Closing build/evidence and remaining exclusions. Retain the record after closure rather than deleting its history.

A partial fix remains partial. A configuration edit alone does not close missing scene placement, save migration or runtime validation. Status promotion follows [P02's evidence rules](PRODUCTION_SCOPE.md#p02--current-status-and-evidence).

## Confirmed open deviations — Package 11

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **VERIFY-PAR-SECONDS** — F01 level par and F11 remaining-route budgets | V01a: shared-level `parSeconds` is the **slowest eligible hero's median Normal required-route time** with the normally unlocked kit and no purchased upgrades, rounded upward; each distinct Level 4A route gets its own benchmark. F11 additionally requires a **separately measured** `remainingRouteSeconds` per level/variant/checkpoint/difficulty, plus its depleted-resource baseline and a `budgetVersion`. | Package 11 A3 (branch `p11/A3`) authors **provisional** values, because no measurement pass has happened. Par is seeded as `authored room count x 90 s`, rounded up to the nearest 30 s: L02 360, L03 540, L04 360, L05 270, L06 360, L07 630, L08 360, L09 360, L10 360, L11 360, L12 540, L13 450, L14 540, L15 540. Remaining-route budgets are seeded as fractions of par in `StoryLevelControllerBase.RemainingRouteFractionFor`: **Entry 0.90, Middle 0.45, PreBoss 0.00** (PreBoss has no timed route, so it falls to the 25-point floor). A `BudgetVersion` field now exists on the attempt record (`StoryAttemptState`, value 1), so only the measurement half remains. **Amended 2026-09-29 (Package 13 W2, S27):** Level 4A is retired, so the "each distinct Level 4A route gets its own benchmark" clause lapses; the measurement set is the fourteen timed Levels 2–15. Level 5 now also carries the Eraser debut encounter on its boat deck, which its measured par must include; its seeded 270 s did not. | Unassigned | A measurement pass replaces every par with a median Normal required-route time and every route fraction with a per-anchor, per-difficulty measured value, adds `budgetVersion`, and re-validates that each enabled anchor reaches the pre-boss lock with positive Integrity. The table is pinned in `StoryLevelControllerBaseTests.EveryTimedCampaignLevelAuthorsAParSecondsAndTheClockArmsFromIt`, so the replacement is a single-file edit. **These numbers must never be quoted as measured.** |
| **VERIFY-BOSS-HP** — the fifteen-boss HP curve | The V7.6 2.E ruling re-states the GDD boss table. Package 11 §2.2 adopts **exactly four** of its rows (L1-L4) and rules the rest an open documentation conflict: the GDD table and the authored resources have never agreed, and the table's own preamble says "the authored `resources/Bosses/*.tres` files are canonical" while `docs/PACKAGE4_ROSTER_PLAN.md` §4.1 records that Package 4 deliberately superseded the table (e.g. "`apex_eraser` is 1200 HP, not 3000"). Two of the four adopted "cuts" are in fact increases against the build, which is what makes the curve irreconcilable without a ruling. | Applied by A7b at `p11/A7b` (baseline `31fed14`): L1 `borgia_inquisitor` 500 → **350**, L2 `siegemaster_duke` 540 → **520**, L3 `chronal_inventor` 560 → **700**, L4 `revolutionary_tribunal` 590 → **850**. **Unchanged and in conflict** (`resources/Bosses/<id>.tres` `MaxHP`, repo value vs. design-table value): L5 `tidal_eraser` 640 vs 1050; L6 `vulcan_decimator` 660 vs 1200; L7 `dread_admiral` 700 vs 1350; L8 `jackal_priest` 700 vs 1500; L9 `iron_chancellor` 780 vs 1700; L10 `tragedy_king` 800 vs 1850; L11 `siege_cannon` 880 vs 2000; L12 `gravity_overseer` 950 vs 2200; L14 `archive_prime` 1050 vs 2500; L15 `apex_eraser` 1200 vs 3000. L13 `mirror_paradox` is 1000 in both, per F20's 1,000 base. Secondary unreconciled metadata in the same table: `Sequential`/`Random` attack patterns that `BossAttackPattern` does not define, and a not-knockback-immune `revolutionary_tribunal` at `[0.66, 0.33]` against the authored `[0.5]`, `DistanceBased`, knockback-immune. **Package 12 (2026-09-27): held under plan D10 pending a user ruling and left untouched.** W9's M19 split the Tribunal's authored 850 into 2 × 425 squad members without changing the total. | Unassigned | An explicit user ruling on which curve is intended — the design table's ~2.5x scaling or the shipped Package 4 curve — applied to all fifteen rows at once, together with a re-tuned band in `tests/ContentValidation/BossRosterActITests.cs`. A7b **retired** that suite's old 500-anchor / 500-700 band and strictly-ascending sweep (350 fell below the floor, 850 above the ceiling, and 850 > 640 broke ascension across the Act I/II boundary); `ActIHealthPoolsPinTheAuthoredV76RowsAndAscendAcrossTheUnchangedTail` now pins the four V7.6 values literally and asserts ascension only over the untouched L5-L7 tail, so re-tuning the curve is a single-file test edit plus the `.tres` rows. Encounter balance for L3/L4 (+140 and +260 HP) has not been re-validated; the boss-arena width checks are unaffected because HP does not move `RangedRangeThreshold`. |
| **VERIFY-STORY-PITS** — F16 lethal openings | F16 defines lethal Story pits: crossing an authored kill boundary resolves one non-hit fall death that ignores HP, block, armor, one-hit shields and temporary invulnerability, and cannot be prevented by Defy History. | The machinery ships complete in Package 11 A3 — `PlayerController.KillPlayerNonHit()`, the `StoryKillBoundary` Area2D, and the `StoryLevelControllerBase.BuildKillBoundary` authoring helper with its edge cue — but **zero boundaries are authored**, because a sweep of all sixteen campaign controllers at `p11/A3` found no lethal opening to attach one to. Every gap is documented as deliberately non-lethal: L04's boss pit is "deliberately not a hazard" (`Level04Controller.cs:296`), L05's flood "never becomes a kill floor" (`Level05Controller.cs:435`), L07's sea is "not a rising water zone and not a kill plane" and rescues the player onto the deck they launched from (`Level07Controller.cs:437`), L06's lava front is "heavy damage and forward knockback, never a kill" (`Level06Controller.cs`), and L12/L13/L14/L15 each state they have no pits. L10's "Yard" is a recessed crowd area with a floor, not a void. **Package 12 (2026-09-27): held under plan D11 pending a user ruling and left untouched.** W9's Level 4 Tribunal pit is non-lethal by construction (ramped, no kill boundary). **Amended 2026-09-29 (Package 13 W2, S27):** Level 4A — whose 520 px movement gaps were the one other candidate the acceptance line below named — is retired and deleted, so the sweep set is the fifteen campaign levels 1–15 (sixteen with the Tutorial) and none authors a boundary. | Unassigned | Either a design pass authors genuine lethal openings (and calls `BuildKillBoundary` for each), or the campaign's non-lethal-gap stance is ratified and F16 is scoped to the Open Fighter stages (the former "Level 4A" scope option lapsed with S27). The chokepoint's behavior is already pinned by `StoryKillBoundaryTests` (5 cases), so authoring a boundary is content work, not code work. |
| **DEFER-CPU-SNAPSHOT** — F19 CPU admission rolls and rollback resimulation | `CPU_COMBAT_POLICY.md` (2026-09-12): "Roll admission once per opportunity using the seeded match PRNG… Snapshot the admission result, opportunity/attack IDs and PRNG state for deterministic resimulation." | Package 11 A9b (branch `p11/A9b`) implements the once-per-opportunity rule with `_grabOpportunityID` / `_attackExecutionID` inside `FighterCpuController`, whose xorshift `_randomState` is derived from the per-match world seed (`FighterSimulationDriver` line ~145, pinned by `FighterMatchSeedTests`) but is **deliberately outside the rollback snapshot** — the controller is an input source, exactly like a human pad, and Package 6 §2.5 states that determinism comes from the seed plus the observation sequence. `FighterCpuBehaviorTests.TheExpandedTableStaysBitIdenticalForTheSameSeedAndObservationStream` is the standing pin on that property. Produced input frames are recorded and replayed on rollback, so an admission decision is never re-sampled; the document's literal request (snapshotting AI state into the simulation) would make CPU frames simulation-derived rather than recorded, which is the architecture Package 6 rejected. `GrabOpportunityID` and `AttackExecutionID` are exposed as read-only controller properties. **Package 12 (2026-09-27): held under plan D15 pending a user ruling and left untouched.** | Unassigned | Either (a) an explicit ruling that the recorded-input model satisfies the contract's intent, closing this entry with no code change, or (b) a Package 7 netcode pass that moves CPU input generation into the snapshot — which requires a Klotho component ID (none is assigned; §2.7 forbids self-assignment), a protocol change for CPU-vs-human input provenance, and re-running the rollback readiness gate. **Do not "fix" this by re-sampling the RNG during resimulation**: that reintroduces the desync this architecture prevents. |

| **DEFER-ROSTER-ENUM** — `FighterCharacterID` is an enum, not roster data | V7.5's roster-agnostic mandate: never hardcode roster size, never enumerate the cast in load-bearing ways. Package 11 A6b routed every production consumer through the manifest-backed `FTT.Core.CharacterRoster`. | `FTT.FighterSim.FighterCharacterID` remains an enum, and is the one documented exception. Its ordinals serialize into deterministic snapshots and into the network protocol, and `zoneTypeID = (int)id * 10 + slot` derives from them, so it cannot become data without a rollback and protocol pass. The **append-only contract** is written into `CharacterRoster`'s doc comment and `AGENTS.md`: a new member takes the next ordinal, nothing is reordered, renumbered or reused. | Unassigned | A tenth character is the forcing event. Either (a) accept the enum permanently under the append-only contract, closing this entry, or (b) a Package 7 pass that moves character identity out of the snapshot, which requires a protocol version bump and a full re-run of the rollback readiness gate. Until then, adding a roster row means adding an enum member — never editing an existing one. **Amended 2026-09-29 (Package 13 W5, plan D3):** the roster swap exercised the contract for the first time — `Tubman = 9` appended, `Pocahontas = 8` kept as an `[Obsolete]` reserved ordinal, `RollbackInputPacket.ProtocolVersion` 3 → **4** (a v3 packet is refused), persistent-object type 4, zone type 83 and kit phases 19–20 retired, never reused. Rollback readiness passed across the nine mirrors with Tubman at 9. Option (a) is now the de-facto practice; the row stays open until it is ratified. |
| ~~**DEFER-SEALING-ANCHOR**~~ — N01's per-level sealing interaction | N01 generalises Level 15's `TemporalCoreAnchor` to every boss level: boss defeat → defeat dialogue → regain control beside the boss's physical pickup and a marked sealing anchor → a single Interact "Seal Timeline — Complete Level" → one atomic transaction → restoration vignette → results, with `AwaitingSeal` **persisted**. | Package 11 A10 implements the **once-only commit half** of the transaction; the anchor interaction itself is not built. **Closed 2026-09-27 (Package 12 W8, branch `p12/W8`), D12(a): AwaitingSeal is persisted.** Boss defeat commits `attemptState.sealReadiness` (boss-defeated, anchor ID, pending boss pickup) as an F10 critical event through `FTT.Environment.SealReadiness`; the defeat beats play; the generic sealing anchor (`TemporalCoreAnchor.CreateSealingAnchor`, prompt `seal_timeline_prompt`) arms beside the boss pickup; a single Interact commits the completion transaction **before** the restoration vignette (the exit beat) and the results; remaining Extractors take `ShutDownBySeal` (inert, no credit). A pre-seal load rebuilds the boss defeated (never respawned), re-arms the anchor and respawns an uncollected boss pickup once. Covers Levels 1–14 and all nine 4A variants (Florence hand-wired through the shared rules; the Level 13 Mirror through `CommitNonStandardBossDefeat`); Level 15 keeps its scene Prime Anchor but persists readiness the same way. Refused during Time Freeze, the Post-Landing Hold and death. Deviations: the prompt is static text, not a live C01c binding glyph; the vignette is the existing exit dialogue plus a `seal_timeline_sealed` notice (placeholder); Level 14's local-objective copy and the Act III destination wording are unchanged. See `docs/handoffs/P12_W8.md`. | Package 12 W8 | Closed on headless evidence: `SealingAnchorTests` (6), `SecretCachePlacementTests` (anchor sweep). Nobody has played a seal; reopen if a playthrough contradicts it. |
| **DEFER-STEAM-RPT** — Steam Remote Play Together | M01 asks for a Remote Play Together entry, hidden when Steam integration is unavailable. | Steamworks is not installed and `NetworkManager.cs`'s `SteamTransportAvailable` is a hard `false`, so "hide when unavailable" would hide it permanently. Remote Play Together needs **no netcode** — it is host-side streamed local multiplayer, so the existing local shared-screen 1v1 path *is* the feature. Package 11 A6 therefore ships **only** the localized notice (`fighter_remote_play_notice`, a focusable label in the Character Select footer), not an entry point. | Unassigned | Steam SDK integration (Package 7 scope), after which the notice can become a real entry gated on `SteamTransportAvailable`. No netcode work is required for the feature itself. |
| ~~**VERIFY-SECRET-CACHE**~~ — the optional dust pool has no physical source | F05 budgets an optional pool per level, split between Extractors and a discoverable secret, and states that a thorough run pays **1,000 base dust**. | **No campaign level authors a `SecretCache`.** The allocator, the manifest rows and the `{level}.secret` source IDs all exist and allocate correctly, but nothing in any scene issues them, so the discovery half of every optional pool is unreachable and a thorough run cannot reach 1,000 today. Level 4A is the sharpest case: `LegacyLevelControllerBase` builds neither a secret cache nor an Extractor, so its whole 10-dust optional pool has no physical source; each variant's manifest reserves `level_04a_<hero>.secret` for one to be attached to. **Closed 2026-09-27 (Package 12 W8, branch `p12/W8`).** `SecretCache` now issues its reserved `{level}.secret` award once as a physical pickup (`DustAwardSource.Secret`), paid and attributed at collection, and a resume that finds a secret found but uncollected respawns the pickup once. `StoryLevelControllerBase` places the cache after `BuildLevel` (so the sealed 4A `BuildLevel` gets it too) at a per-level `SecretCachePosition`, abstract on `LegacyLevelControllerBase`. Caches are authored in Levels 2–15 and all nine 4A variants (Level 1 reserves none by design), off the critical path and reachable with the worst character's double jump. | Package 12 W8 | Closed on headless evidence: `SecretCacheAwardTests` (3), `SecretCachePlacementTests` (3), and `ThoroughRunCollectionWalkTests`, which loads every level a run visits, completes every physically placed Extractor/hold node and secret through its real path, and collects exactly 1,000 base dust for every hero. Nobody has walked to a secret in a playthrough. |
| **DEFER-SAVE-VERSION-SPLIT** — one schema counter for two payloads | Separate story and global payloads would ideally version independently, so a global-only change does not invalidate story saves. | `SaveSchemaMigrator.CurrentVersion` is **shared by both payloads**, so Package 11's global-side v6 work (the binding rename, the match-settings normalization, the seen-dialogue union) also bumped every story payload. Package 11 §6 explicitly **accepted the coupled bump**: splitting the counters is a larger change than an alignment package should take. **Package 12 Phase C (2026-09-27) bumped the shared counter again, to v7**: the step composes one global derivation (W5's hazard toggle) and two story derivations (W2's completion reconciliation, W6's lowest-difficulty seed), so both payloads genuinely needed it this time. **Package 13 Phase C (2026-09-29) bumped it once more, to v8**: two story derivations (W2's Level 4A retirement, then W5's roster swap) and one global (W5's Tubman unlock); again both payloads needed it. | Unassigned | A future package splits the two counters, with independent migration chains and a test that a global-only bump leaves story payloads at their own version. Not urgent — the coupling costs a redundant story migration pass, nothing more. |

R03: `ChronalRewindManager.PostLandingHoldFrames` (60) ends the death rewind, the Level 0 scripted rewind, the in-session Collapse resume and the Anchor Snap; the frozen set is the union of the Time Freeze and rewind sweeps (plan D3(a)); `ChronalRewindManager.IsWorldHeld` gates `Hitbox.OnAreaEntered` and `Hurtbox.TakeHit`, so frozen actors take no damage and pay no meter, Rally, checkpoint or extractor credit; Time Freeze activation and `InteractionArea` wait for the thaw; Integrity, the Time Freeze cooldown and hero timers run; protection is armed by `PlayerController.EndRecoveryHold` at the thaw; `EventBus.OnRecoveryLanded` / `OnRecoveryWorldThawed` bracket the hold; the reused thaw warning plays through `AudioManager.PlayCriticalCue` 30 frames before the thaw and survives Reduced Temporal Effects; the music duck ends on `RewindPresentationPhase.Thawed`; a pending boss T01b beat resumes after the thaw; the hold is armed in memory only and a save load never grants one. Residuals tracked elsewhere: the sealing anchor does not exist (`DEFER-SEALING-ANCHOR`); `PressurePlate` is not a freezable (as under Time Freeze); the thaw cue stream is null under the silent placeholder audio kit (Package 10); `PostLandingThawWarningFrames` = 30 is provisional. See `docs/handoffs/P12_W1.md`. | Package 12 W1 | Closed on headless evidence: `PostLandingHoldTests` (11), `BossHistoricalRecoverySuspensionTests`, `DeathTriggeredRewindTests`, `ScriptedRewindTests`, `StoryCombatRulesTests`, `StoryDropsAndRewindTests`. Nothing reads it yet. **Package 12 W3b (2026-09-27): the flag now has semantics in both modes** (only a launching hit grants DI, tumble and landing tech; a non-launcher on a grounded victim is grounded knockback; a missed tech is the M05 knockdown), and **W3b applied W3's proposal unchanged** — no `.tres` value was edited, because the GDD still does not enumerate the launching Specials/Ultimates and no design review has ruled. Sim consumers of the authored flag: the generic melee Special/Ultimate intents and projectiles (through `FighterHitContractTable`). The bespoke per-kit sim pulses (zone finales, expiry launches, the coil chain) still launch by their own knockback, which agrees with the proposal because every one of those abilities is authored `Launches = true`. **Non-player sources author no launch flag**: enemies, bosses and Story hazards keep the legacy "any knockback launches" rule on the Story player (`PlayerController.ResolveHitLaunches`), and sim stage hazards launch by default. Story kit scripts that build their own `HitPayload` (W4's area) do not yet stamp `Launches`, so those hits do not tumble a `PlayerController` victim (the Mirror Paradox clone). | Package 12 W3b → design review | A design ruling names the launching Specials/Ultimates (and whether enemy/boss/hazard hits launch); the `.tres` values and `AbilityHitContractTests.EveryAbilityAuthorsTheDerivedHitContract` are updated to the ruled table; kit-script payloads stamp `Launches` from their `AbilityData`. **Amended 2026-09-29 (Package 13):** the character review ruled some rows. W7b: Righteous Smite (J02) and Rail Charge (LN02) are authored **non-launching**; Splitting Strike launches (spikes) an **airborne** target only and shoves a grounded one. Consuming the loadout inside the tick needs either a non-snapshot loadout table reachable from the systems or component space. **W3b:** `FighterHitContractTable` — static match configuration built from the two loadouts and handed to `FighterCombatSystem` / `FighterProjectileSystem` at construction exactly like the stage geometry, never a component, never snapshot or hash state — now feeds the generic melee Special/Ultimate intents and every Special projectile their authored `HitstunFrames` and `Launches` (12 frames on every shipped Special; the old fixed 18/30 remain only as the fallback for a hand-built loadout with no projected contract). **Still open:** the bespoke per-kit sim hitstun constants — the zone pulses and finales (`EmancipatorHitstunFrames` 18, Tempest 10, Grand Crusade 24, Union 30, Tidewater 24, Symphony 30, Stage Finale 30), the expiry launches (Spiral 12, Cosmological 30, Matrix 18), the coil chain (12), the construct/fence/nest ticks — are kit dispatch code (W4's area) and still disagree with the authored 12. **Amended 2026-09-29 (Package 13):** most of that list is gone. The rows below were consolidated at the Phase C closeout (2026-09-27): the four held items that had no row, and the adopted recommendations that §9 recorded but the ledger did not. All were observed at `main` after the Phase C commits; none has been played.

The anchor is single-press, so Phase C plays the `seal_charge` hum when the anchor **arms** and `seal_lock` when the seal is accepted (`EventBus.OnSealingAnchorChanged`); both streams are silent. | Unassigned | Package 10 presentation, plus a ruling on whether the seal should be a hold (which would move the charge cue onto it). |

The build's binding sense (`InputBindings.SetUnbound`, `controls_binding_unbound`) is correct and untouched. | Unassigned (docs) | The master and its contract copy are corrected the same way; the mirror is then re-synced byte-identical.
