## Confirmed open deviations — playtest feel pass 2026-10-04, workstream ARENA

The 2026-10-04 playtest-driven feel / anti-spam / pathing pass (`docs/PLAYTEST_FEEL_2026-10-04_PLAN.md`, items P1, P2, P3 lead, P5 and F8), as amended by its fix round (fix-arena A1–A5 and G5; fix-feel M1, the player-side line of sight). First observed in the ARENA worktree on top of `main` `5d3ce25`; the rows below describe `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-07 after both rounds were merged (still uncommitted on `5d3ce25`). **Nobody has played or looked at any of it**; the evidence is the Story playtest bots and the GdUnit suites (`BossArenaLeashTests`, `ArenaGeometryContentTests`, `ArenaEnemyAttackGeometryTests`, `StoryCameraRigTests`).

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ARENA-FLORENCE-DOORWAY** — Level 1's boss arena entrance and edge | Design §17 (`design-godot.md:3517`): the boss engages when the player enters the arena. The P5 brief: no layout changes beyond the gate fixes. | Florence's arena west wall ran the full height (y 0–1080) over the floor at 900 since the first commit, so no hero could enter (Level 1 has no movement ability) and the old radius reveal started the fight through the wall. The wall now stops at `Level01Controller.ArenaDoorwayTopY` (700), a 200 px ground-level doorway. The arena rect (reveal and leash) starts at the Phase 2 west barricade's inner face (`Room4OffsetX + 180`), so the boss-room checkpoint (`florence_checkpoint_2`, +100) stands in a 160 px antechamber and is struck before the reveal. A hero still west of the arena when the barricades close is moved inside. **Fix round:** the workshop door joins `StoryGateRules.DoorGroup` (A1), and the arena ward (A5, `ARENA-LEASH-EDGE-ANSWER`) can hold against a hero at the checkpoint only while the engaged Borgia Inquisitor is still beyond its 420 px reach; once it has closed to its edge (it does so on its own), no point from the doorway's inner face (`ArenaInnerLeftX`) to the edge is warded (`BossArenaLeashTests.FlorencesAntechamberIsNeverWardedAgainstABossHoldingItsEdge`). The `play` bot cannot reach the arena: it stops at the gear-puzzle workshop door (x 8600), a bot limitation. | Unassigned | A human walkthrough of the doorway and the antechamber, or an authored arena entrance that replaces them. `EveryBossArenaCanBeEnteredOverItsWestThreshold` and `ThePreBossBreathersAndTheirBeatsStandOutsideTheArenaReveal` stay green. |
| **ARENA-LEASH-EDGE-ANSWER** — what a leashed boss does about a target outside its arena | `design-godot.md:1624`: bosses are bounded to stage platforms. The design does not say how a leashed boss treats a target outside the arena. | *Provisional.* **The answer:** a boss pinned at its leash edge answers an outside target only inside its engagement range (`max(MeleeRangeThreshold, RangedRangeThreshold)` × 60 px — 420 px for the Borgia Inquisitor up to 720 px for the Siege Cannon), and then **never** with `SummonMinions` or `Teleport`, not even through the no-deadlock fallback (`BossController.SelectAbilityIndex(distance, targetOutsideArena)`); with nothing else ready it rests. Beyond that range it holds the edge, idle and facing the target. The first cut answered at any distance, which let the RangeClass.Any summon kits (Tribunal, Forge Sentinel, Tragedy King, First Severed) raise a wave every cooldown while the hero stood outside. **The arena ward (fix round A5, provisional)** closes the plink spot that rule left (a hero outside the arena and beyond the boss's range attacking it with long-range kit unanswered): `BossController.IsArenaWarded` holds while the boss is engaged and leashed and its target stands both more than `ArenaWardMarginPixels` (60, one world unit) outside the arena's x-range **and** beyond its engagement range (`IsWithinEngagementRange`, the same test chase uses to commit an attack). While warded, `OnHurtboxHit` refuses every hit first (no damage, knockback, status, combo mark, victim hitstop or phase/interrupt work, and with 0 dealt no meter or Rally), `ApplyStatusEffect` refuses a status applied without a hit (the Relativity Rift's linger; a rewind slot restore bypasses it), and a Venom tick deals nothing while the tick cadence and the status clock keep running. A refused hit reads as a `UIPalette.UnboundCold` flash for 0.12 s and, for a player-dealt hit, a `UIPalette.Slate` "0" damage number (honouring the damage-number setting). Never warded: a dormant boss (the waking hit from outside lands — `ARENA-WAKE-REVEAL`), an unleashed boss, a hero inside the engagement range (so melee spacing against a boss holding its edge, about 58–103 px outside, lands normally) and scripted `TakeDamage(int)`. It is derived state (engagement, leash, target), so pool and rewind need no new reset beyond the `WardedHitsRefused` counter. The fix round's first cut used the 60 px edge margin alone, which warded that melee spacing while the boss kept swinging; review made it relative to the boss's range and added the status and DoT guards. The Level 13 Mirror has no ward (`ARENA-MIRROR-NO-WARD`). No `play`-bot run (Easy `fxward2` on Levels 2/4/5/7/11, Normal `fxwardn` on 2/5/8/9) was ever outside an arena after a reveal, so the re-approach from outside is covered only by unit cases; the runner has no start-position option. | Unassigned | A user ruling: keep the ward (ratifying its 60 px margin and its read), answer an outside target at any distance with a non-summon ranged kit instead, or move the leash and reveal to the room boundary so the outside spot disappears. `BossArenaLeashTests` pins today's rule (`AnEngagedBossRefusesEveryHitWhileItsHeroStandsOutsideTheArenaBeyondItsReach`, `ABossPinnedAtItsEdgeTakesEveryHitFromAnOutsideHeroWithinItsReach`, `AVenomTakenBeforeTheHeroStepsOutDealsNothingWhileTheBossIsWarded`, `AnUnleashedOrDormantBossIsNeverWardedAndAPoolCycleClearsTheWard`). |
| **ARENA-WAKE-REVEAL** — a dormant boss hit from outside its arena | Design §17: the intro (name card, establishing beat, free telegraph) plays as the player enters the arena. The design does not cover the boss being struck first from outside. | *Provisional.* The struck boss wakes into a revealed fight: HUD bar, `BossRevealed` (objective, boss-intro dialogue, boss music), still leashed, with the name card, the 1.5 s freeze and the free telegraph skipped. The intro is not recorded as seen, so a later attempt still gets it. Entering the arena afterwards never runs the intro freeze over a fighting boss. | Unassigned | A ruling on whether a woken boss should still show the name card (without the freeze). |
| **ARENA-WEST-EDGE-AT-OLD-REVEAL-LINE** — where each arena starts | Design §17: the reveal fires on entering the boss arena. | *Provisional.* For Levels 2, 3, 5–12, 14 and 15 each arena rect's west edge is the old reveal-radius line, not the room transition into the boss room, so the pre-boss anchors, absence beats and `PreBossTrigger`s stay outside it and the boss is leashed to that sub-range of the room. Since fix-round A4 the edge is one derived value per level: `BossArenaWestX = BossSpawnX − BossArenaApproachPixels`, where the approach is the old radius (900 px on Levels 2, 5–8, 10 and 11; 800 on 3 and 9; 700 on 12 and 14; 750 on 15), and the encounter is built at `BossSpawnX` through `StoryLevelControllerBase.BuildBossEncounter`'s arena overload, which takes no reveal distance — the rect is the reveal (the radius overload survives for the `SealingAnchorTests` fixture). Level 4's edge is its courtyard room boundary (`Room4StartX` 8640, 40 px east of the old 9400 − 800 line); Florence's is its barricade face (row above). `SecretCachePlacementTests` and `AbsenceBeatContentTests` now read each level's `ArenaBounds` instead of hand-copied radii (the latter's Level 4 literal was already 40 px off). | Unassigned | A ruling that the edge should be the physical room boundary (then the pre-boss beats move west of it), or acceptance of the old reveal line. `ArenaGeometryContentTests.EveryDerivedArenaEdgeStandsItsApproachInFrontOfTheSpawnTheEncounterUses` pins the derivation on all twelve levels. |
| **ARENA-PROVISIONAL-CONSTANTS** — numbers the design does not give | `design-godot.md:3119-3132` gives the Story camera table but no lead cap or cut threshold; the P3 brief asks for a clamped lead. | Single named constants, none measured: `StoryCameraRules.MaxLookAheadPixels` (3 units) and `SnapDistancePixels` (900); `EnemyAttackGeometryRules.MaxLeadSeconds` (0.75), `ResampleRadiusPixels` (480), `BodyAimHeightPixels` (32), `WallClearancePixels` (2), `WallProbeRays` (3); `StoryGateRules.ColumnTopY` (−2400) and `ColumnMarginPixels` (8). Fix round: `BossController.ArenaWardMarginPixels` (60) and the ward's flash (`ArenaWardFlashSeconds`, 0.12 s) from A5; each level's `BossArenaApproachPixels` from A4 (values under `ARENA-WEST-EDGE-AT-OLD-REVEAL-LINE`). | Unassigned | Package 9 playtests ratify or replace each value in its one rulebook. |
| **ARENA-MIRROR-NO-WARD** — Level 13's Mirror and the arena ward | `ARENA-LEASH-EDGE-ANSWER`'s ward: a leashed boss cannot be plinked from outside its arena beyond its reach. | The clone is clamp-leashed to `Level13Controller.MirrorArenaBounds` (x 7200 to the east wall's inner face; its reveal is still the 700 px `MirrorRevealDistance` radius). Fix-round A2 moved the clamp after the clone's own physics step (`MirrorParadoxController.LeashPhysicsPriority` = 100), so no move, Echo Step or warp ends a frame outside (`BossArenaLeashTests.TheMirrorCloneEndsEveryPhysicsFrameInsideItsArena`). It has **no ward**: the clone is a `PlayerController` driven by the Fighter CPU decision table, so there is no boss engagement range to measure against, and its hit intake is the shared Story player path. A hero west of the arena can still hit the clone with ranged kit; whether the clone answers is the CPU's own range policy, which nobody has measured. | Unassigned | A ruling: a Mirror-only refusal keyed to the clone's longest kit reach, a west wall that closes when the Mirror is revealed, or acceptance that the clone answers with the hero's own ranged kit. |
| **F8-LEAD-AXIS** — the Story camera's look-ahead axis | `design-godot.md:3130` (Story camera table): "Lookahead Time `0.3s` — Slight camera lead in movement direction". | *Provisional.* The follow rig leads on the **horizontal** axis only: `StoryCameraRules.LookAheadTarget(velocityX)` is `LookAheadSeconds` (0.3) × horizontal velocity, clamped to ±`MaxLookAheadPixels` (3 units = 180 px), and eased into the confiner's lead. Vertical tracking is the dead/soft zone, Y damping 0.3 and the one-unit-up offset, with no lead, because a jump's vertical velocity reverses mid-arc and a vertical lead would bob the view on every hop. With the design's X damping (0.5) a steady runner still trails the view centre slightly, so the lead mostly cancels the follow lag rather than putting the view ahead. Raised by the round-1 ARENA review; nobody has looked at it. | Unassigned | A user ruling: accept horizontal-only (amend the table to "horizontal movement direction"), or add a vertical lead with a rule for jump arcs (for example falls only) plus a `StoryCameraRigTests` case. |
| **P3-HORIZONTAL-LEAD** — how enemy shots lead a moving target | Plan P3: "Enemy projectiles lead a moving target". The design gives no lead model. | *Provisional.* At fire time a shot re-acquires the living player nearest the telegraphed point (within `ResampleRadiusPixels`, 480), aims at body height (`BodyAimHeightPixels`, 32 px above the feet) and leads through `EnemyAttackGeometryRules.LeadPoint`: the target's **horizontal** velocity only, a first-order intercept iterated twice, flight time capped at `MaxLeadSeconds` (0.75 s). Vertical velocity is ignored (a linear vertical lead on a jumping hero aims into the sky or the floor), so a well-timed hop still dodges a led shot. A target that crossed to the shooter's other side keeps the telegraphed aim. Raised by the round-1 ARENA review. | Unassigned | A ruling to keep horizontal-only or add a gravity-aware vertical lead, measured with the `play` bot's projectile share of `damage_by_source`. |
| **PROVISIONAL-PLAYER-MELEE-LINE-OF-SIGHT** — the player's swings need Environment line of sight (fix-round M1) | P2's rationale — a swing does not reach through a wall — applied to the player; the design is silent on the player side. | *Provisional.* Story only. `Hitbox.RequiresEnvironmentLineOfSight` is set by `CharacterFactory` on the player's `MeleeHitbox` alone (the basic string and both directional strikes). A new contact with an `EnemyController` or `BossController` hurtbox is dropped unless an Environment ray from the hurtbox's `CollisionShape2D` centre back to the swinger's `PlayerController.BodyCentreGlobalPosition` (it follows the crouch) is clear: `EnvironmentProbe.IsClear(..., ignoreBodiesAtOrigin: true)`, one-way platforms never block. Casting from the target and skipping every body that contains the target's centre means a wall-phasing Rift Phantom caught mid-wall — inside a multi-shape wall too — is not sheltered by the wall it is passing through, matching its own P2 clip, while one whose centre is past the wall's far face is blocked both ways (the review found the first cut, cast from the swinger, made a mid-wall phantom immune to basics while it could still hit back). One centre-to-centre ray per contact, tested once when the contact begins (a wall that clears later in the same active window is not re-tested); a low wall between the two centres blocks even where the hitbox rect clears it. Not gated: strike surfaces (fractures, extractors), the Mirror clone (a `PlayerController`), projectiles, zones, constructs and kit shape queries. The sim never reads it. The enemy-side clip still uses the default `ignoreBodiesAtOrigin: false`, so a phantom's own swing from inside a multi-shape wall is clipped by its other shapes (Level 13's geometry is all single-rect bodies). | Unassigned | A human review of swings at ledges and low walls, and a ruling on whether Specials' shape queries take the same rule. `ArenaEnemyAttackGeometryTests.APlayerSwingNeverLandsOnAnEnemyBehindAWall`, `AWallPhasingEnemyInsideAWallIsNotShelteredByIt` and `AWallPhasingEnemyInsideAMultiShapeWallIsNotShelteredByIt` pin it. |
| **VERIFY-AREAPULSE-WALLS** — enemy AreaPulse hitboxes ignore walls | P2: enemy hitboxes do not reach through walls. | P2 clips only forward (mirrored) `MeleeStrike` and `ChargeDash` hitboxes; since fix-round A3 a strike probes once at activation and a charge re-probes only on an active frame its owner moved, through one reused ray query per executor (`EnvironmentProbe.CreateReusableQuery`). `AreaPulse` hitboxes (radial, unmirrored: the `shell_burst`, `electric_nova` and `cold_gas_vent` mob pulses and seven boss pulses, e.g. the Chronal Inventor's `resonator_pulse` at 170 px) are not occluded, so a caster pressed against a thin wall can hit through it. No reported door case is affected: fix-round G8 found the bots' "hit through the Level 6 rockfall / Level 3 court door" reports were Collapse Tremor debris (`env`) and pre-P1 boss roaming, and `TheRockfallAndTheCourtDoorStopForwardSwingsFromEitherSide` pins the forward clip on both real doors. | Unassigned | Either clip a pulse's reach against Environment, or rule that radial pulses are area denial that ignores walls; a pin either way. |
| **P5-GATE-COLUMN-SWEEP** — every gate stands under a column no hero can clear | `PACKAGE5_CAMPAIGN_PLAN.md` L02: gates cannot be jumped; plan P5. | Round 1 added columns rising to `StoryGateRules.ColumnTopY` over Florence's workshop door, Level 2's Gates A and B, Level 3's Court of Honor door, Level 6's rockfall slab, Level 9's RadarGate and Level 12's curtain. The round-1 review found the audit incomplete (Level 4's CourtyardGate, top y 140 with nothing above it, clearable by Einstein's double jump plus an upward Warp; Level 11's breastwork earth stopping at y 0). Fix round A1 added columns over Level 4's CourtyardGate, Level 8's VaultDoor (to the tomb ceiling) and Level 11's breastwork and both lane-roof masses (Cleopatra could double-jump the roofs from the array top), and replaced the hand list with enumeration: every `BuildDoor` door (and Florence's own) joins `StoryGateRules.DoorGroup` (`story_gate_door`), and `ArenaGeometryContentTests.EveryPuzzleGateStandsUnderAColumnNoHeroCanClear` requires every door and `ForcefieldBarrier` (at least 11) to stand under a registered column covering its width, foot within 40 px of the gate top, out of reach — best double jump × 1.25 at the level's lowest gravity plus a 240 px movement-ability allowance — from every standable within 1000 px. Columns per level: L1 1, L2 2, L3 1, L4 1, L6 1, L8 1, L9 1, L11 3, L12 1. Level 8's shows as a 32 px strip at Room 3's east edge above the tomb ceiling and a 12 px sliver in the chamber's top-left pocket. | Unassigned | A visual review of the column strips in the editor (Level 8's most visibly); the sweep stays green. |
| **LAYOUT-CRAWLSPACE-MOVES** — no standable surface wedged under a ceiling (fix-round G5) | Bot game bug 11 (plan §3): Level 4's top tower step left 62 px under the walkway, less than the 64 px hero. The P5 brief said "no layout changes beyond the gate fixes". | Level 4's Bastille tower top step (one-way 5200/380, top 370, under the walkway's 308 underside) moved to `Level04Controller.TowerTopStepX/Y` = 4880/380, west of the walkway's end at 5000; the climb is 100 px then 78 px, inside Lincoln's single jump (`TheParisTowerTopStepClearsTheWalkwayWithinTheWeakestSingleJump`). The new sweep `ArenaGeometryContentTests.NoStandableSurfaceIsWedgedUnderASolidCeiling` (headroom ≥ 64 + 4 px wherever a hero can get onto the surface: from 52 px for a one-way, whose 12 px landing margin lifts a rising hero onto it, from 64 px for a solid) found two more and moved them: Level 6's west roof one-way 1000/700 → 1000/720 (82 px under the 1120 stump) and Level 11's battery-ridge step 2680/730 → 2680/750 (78 px under the ridge Extractor; it is not a route onto the ridge, which is climbed from the sandbags). Left as authored: unenterable solid pockets (Level 1's gear platforms under the print-shop shelves, stacked Level 7/10 ledges) and Level 14's top scaffold rung, 68 px under `level_14_extractor_0` (it passes the margin; the extractor table is authored data). The bots' frame-simulated nav graph (Lincoln) still climbs Level 4 (ground → 750 → 610 → 470 → 370 → walkway) and Level 6 (ground → roof → stump); no bot route crosses the three platforms. | Unassigned | A human look at the three moved platforms and the Level 14 rung in the editor; the sweep stays green. |

## Confirmed open deviations — playtest feel pass 2026-10-04, workstream ENEMY

The same pass's Story enemy-combat items (`docs/PLAYTEST_FEEL_2026-10-04_PLAN.md` §2.2 S1–S5, F5 victim half, F9, P4, the AbilityOrigin mirror), as amended by its fix round (fix-enemy E1–E4; E5, the pool/rewind/kill-throw reset of the grab, throw and slam-bounce latches through `EnemyController.ResetGrabAndSlamState`, is a build fix with no row). Every number is a single constant in `FTT.Combat.StoryEnemyCombatRules` (`scripts/Combat/StoryEnemyCombatRules.cs`), Story-only; nothing in `scripts/FighterSim/` references it. Observed in `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-07 (uncommitted on `5d3ce25`). Evidence: `StoryEnemyCombatRulesTests`, `StoryEnemyCombatTests`, `StoryEnemyCombatReviewTests`, `EnemyControllerTests` and the Story playtest bots. **Nobody has played it.**

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PROVISIONAL-STORY-CONFIRM-PRORATION** — string + Special burst on Story mobs | User complaint 2026-10-04: string + both Specials is "basically an insta-kill". The design has no PvE proration (`design-godot.md:1606` and `:1490` reject juggle decay for Fighter PvP only). | *Provisional.* **Amended 2026-10-11 by USER-FIRST-SPECIAL-UNTAXED (D4):** the chain's **first** Special lands untaxed — no ×0.6 and no chain decay on any of its hits — though its hits still advance the chain counter; everything below now applies to a **second or later** Special. A Special-class hit whose **first** hit lands on a mob already in hitstun (a confirm) deals ×0.6 on every hit of that Special in the chain; inside one uninterrupted stun chain each hit after the 3rd decays ×0.85, floored at ×0.5 (a late confirm can reach ×0.3). The chain spans the Armored Recovery its own poise trip opens and resets on natural stun expiry, the end of that window, death, spawn/despawn and rewind. Ultimates, Tick deliveries and hazards are exempt and do not advance it. Bosses are not prorated (no hitstun). | Unassigned | A user ruling on the rule and its numbers, informed by the bot matrix (`summary_*_after*.csv`): string + S1 + S2 should no longer kill a full-HP standard mob in one cycle while a Special thrown from neutral keeps full value. |
| **PROVISIONAL-STANDARD-POISE** — standard mobs gain the V7.4 stagger budget | V7.4 Enemy Stagger Discipline scopes the budget to elites and bosses (`design-godot.md:1445`). | *Provisional.* **Amended 2026-10-11 by USER-FIRST-SPECIAL-UNTAXED (D4):** the budget is **2.325 s** (139.5 frames; it was 1.9 s) and drains **4.0 s/s** (it was 3 s/s); the ×1.5 cost now applies only to a second or later Special in the chain (the chain's first Special charges ×1.0); derivation and residuals in `PROVISIONAL-FIRST-SPECIAL-TAX-RULES`. Standard-tier mobs get a 2.325 s budget (a full string under the S5 floors costs 1.70 s, plus the chain's first Special, so it completes); a taxed Special-sourced stun costs ×1.5; a trip opens a 0.6 s Armored Recovery with the committed counterattack; the standard budget drains 4 s/s while unstunned (elites keep 1 s/s). Throws and a bowled standard's knockdown charge nothing; a bowled **elite** keeps the V7.4 budget charge it took before the pass (the applied stun, no floor — elites are grab-immune but not bowling-immune; the first cut had silently dropped it, fix round E3). Fix round E2: Ultimate-sourced stuns — the Ultimate attack class or an authored Ultimate origin, `StoryEnemyCombatRules.ChargesStandardPoise` — charge no standard poise (the stun still applies), as S2 and V7.4's diminishing special stun already exempt Ultimates; otherwise a meter-paid multi-hit Ultimate (Lincoln's five 0.5 s smashes, 2.5 s) tripped the budget (then 1.9 s; still under 2.325 s) mid-cast and had its own last hits refused by the counterattack. The elite budget keeps charging Ultimate stuns. No poise accrues while the mob is frozen or `DrillInvulnerable`. | Unassigned | A ruling to keep or reverse; if kept, amend design §4 Enemy Stagger Discipline. |
| **PROVISIONAL-BASICS-PACING** — basic string vs standard mobs | 2026-08-10 feel batch accepted "Story encounters take ~2× longer on basics" with no compensating retune. | *Provisional.* The universal basic set (`combo_1/2/3`, up-attack, down-air) deals ×1.25 to Standard-tier mobs on Story intake only (half away from zero, never below 1). Constructs, throws, elites and every shared `.tres` damage are untouched. | Unassigned | A ruling; the bot `basics` median frames-to-kill is the measure. |
| **S1-STUNNED-KNOCKBACK-FRICTION** — knockback on stunned Story mobs | Plan S1 (build bug): knockback replaces velocity, so the string and finisher should move mobs; the per-frame zeroing of a stunned mob's velocity predated that rule. The plan gives no numbers. | Build fix with agent-chosen numbers: a stunned mob's horizontal velocity decays at 1800 px/s² grounded and 600 px/s² airborne (`StoryEnemyCombatRules.StunnedGroundFrictionPixelsPerSecondSquared` / `StunnedAirDragPixelsPerSecondSquared`; flying mobs keep their stunned hover), and a ground mob's knockback heading over a drop is cancelled at the edge (probe depth `LedgeProbeDepthPixels`, 64 px, grounded; `KnockbackAirborneLedgeProbeDepthPixels`, 480 px, airborne). **Fix round E1:** a stun *opened* by a hit or call that writes no knockback — an impulse-free construct bite or coil arc, a zero-knockback Special, a standalone direct `ApplyStun` such as a Static Charge or a Kinetic Splitting bounce that finds the mob neither stunned nor armored — zeroes the mob's horizontal speed (and a flyer's vertical) instead, so the friction never slides chase, patrol or ChargeDash speed (the first cut let a charging mob stunned by a turret bolt slide about 100–225 px). A *refresh* of a running stun keeps the slide an earlier knockback wrote, and a throw's flight and a bowled victim's knockback count as knockback (`EnemyController.TryApplyKnockback` reports whether knockback was written). **Since 2026-10-11** a Standard or Elite mob's **finisher** flight decelerates at `max(S1, the D6a carry deceleration)` until touchdown (`USER-FINISHER-CARRY`, `PROVISIONAL-SPACING-WALLS-RULES` 1). | Unassigned | None beyond the user's review. `StoryEnemyCombatReviewTests` pins the open and refresh paths. |
| **S5-HIT-INDEXED-STUN-FLOOR** — basic-string stun floor on Story mobs | The shared `BasicComboRules.EnemyBasicStunFloorFrames` (24) claimed to cover the string's connect gaps but did not (31 / 41–43 ticks). | Build fix: string hits 1 and 2 floor the applied stun at 34 / 44 frames (`StoryEnemyCombatRules.BasicStringStunFloorFrames`); the finisher, up-attack and down-air keep 24. Non-resistant mobs therefore stun 4 frames longer than their authored 30/40. The V7.4 **elite** budget is charged at the pre-S5 24-frame floor, so elite poise is unchanged. | Unassigned | None beyond the user's review. |
| **F9-DEALT-HIT-FEEDBACK** — camera shake and damage numbers on hits the player deals to mobs/bosses | Design §8 shake table: shake comes from `AbilityData.ScreenShakeIntensity`. | Ability payloads with a positive authored intensity shake at ×12 for their authored duration (matching the player-hit path); basics and unauthored payloads use `min(6, 1 + 0.15 × dmg)` px (+2 for a launch), 0.08 s (+0.06). Floating damage numbers float for every player-dealt damaging hit and honour the damage-number setting. No haptics are added on the mob side. | Unassigned | A visual review in the editor. |
| **PROVISIONAL-ENEMY-CHASE-LEASH** — chase persistence and ledge/wall awareness | `design-godot.md:2986`: a mob returns to its waypoints when the player leaves `deAggroRadius`; mobs are slower than the player and encounters may be skipped. User complaint 2026-10-04: "too easy to bypass everything / pathing is not very good". | *Provisional.* An aggroed mob keeps chasing while its target is inside the mob's room x-bounds (the first room trigger containing its spawn x) or within 1.5× `DeAggroRadius`. In Chase, ground mobs stop at a drop deeper than 64 px and at Environment walls instead of walking off or pushing, and hold position under a target ≥ 48 px directly above. No jumping (design forbids it for ground mobs). Patrol and Returning are unchanged. Consequence noted by the fix round and left for this ruling: a mob in Chase idles at a ledge or wall for as long as its target stays in its room. | Unassigned | A ruling, measured with the `bypass` bot's hits-after-pass. |
| **KILL-FREEZE-VICTIM** — hitstop on a lethal hit on a Story mob | `BasicComboRules`: lethal hits skip hitstop (written for Fighter KOs; the design is silent for PvE). | A lethal direct hit holds the corpse in its flashed hitstun pose for the hit's own launch-aware victim hitstop (`StoryEnemyCombatRules.VictimHitstopFrames` = `BasicComboRules.HitstopFrames(dealt, launches)`, the attacker's rule, F6's +3 launch bonus included — fix round E4) before the death animation; the kill event, dust and collision removal stay immediate. Lethal ticks get no freeze. Bosses keep the skip. | Unassigned | None beyond the user's review. |

## Confirmed open deviations — 2026-10-04 playtest feel pass (workstream FEEL)

The user asked the 2026-10-04 pass (`docs/PLAYTEST_FEEL_2026-10-04_PLAN.md`) to iterate on what improves feel; these rows are the **provisional rulings** its player-side workstream made under that request, each reversible in one place, plus the design deviations the round-1 review found unrecorded and the fix round's (fix-feel G2, G7, R11, R12; M1 is under workstream ARENA). Observed in `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-07 (uncommitted on `5d3ce25`). Nobody has played them; the feel probe (Joan, Level 2) is the only measurement.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PROVISIONAL-HITSTOP-WEIGHT** — the shared hitstop curve (plan F6) | `design-godot.md` "Hitstop / Hitlag (V7)": every landed direct hit freezes both parties for **3–8 frames** scaled by damage (3 at ≤5, linear to 8 at ≥25), blocked hits 2. | `BasicComboRules.HitstopFrames(damage, launches)`: the floor is **4** (`HitstopMinFrames`), an authored launcher (the finisher, Up-Attack, Down-Air, the throws, Lincoln's hit 2, launching Specials/Ultimates) adds **+3** (`LaunchHitstopBonusFrames`), capped at **11** (`HitstopCeilingFrames`); blocked hits stay 2. Both modes call the one rule — Story's attacker (`OnMeleeHitConfirmed`, `ApplyAbilityCasterHitstop`) and player victim with the payload's launch flag, the sim's `ApplyFighterHit` with the hit's `launches`, and (fix round E4) the Story mob and boss victim and a mob's kill freeze through `StoryEnemyCombatRules.VictimHitstopFrames(dealt, launches)`, which is `BasicComboRules.HitstopFrames(dealt, launches)` with the same dealt damage the attacker uses — so both parties of a launching hit freeze for the same frames (round 1 left the enemy side on the one-argument rule, so a launching hit that froze the attacker 7 frames froze the mob 4). A killing blow on a Story mob still freezes the attacker. Every Fighter hash moves. Probe: Joan's basics freeze 4 frames per hit (was 3). | Unassigned (user ruling) | The user keeps or reverses it. Reverting is `HitstopMinFrames = 3` and `LaunchHitstopBonusFrames = 0` plus the pins in `FeelPassRulesTests` and `StoryEnemyCombatRulesTests.TheVictimHitstopIsTheAttackersLaunchAwareRule`; `StoryEnemyCombatReviewTests.ALaunchingHitFreezesAMobABossAndACorpseForTheAttackersLaunchAwareFrames` pins the victim side. |
| **PROVISIONAL-STANDARD-MOB-NO-LAUNCH** — enemy hits on the Story player (plan F7) | `VERIFY-ABILITY-LAUNCHES` left the enemy/boss/hazard half open: M05 says only authored launchers launch, but non-player sources author no launch flag and kept "any knockback launches", so every mob poke tumbled the hero into a 30-frame knockdown and 10-frame get-up. | `HitPayload.SourceIsStandardMob` (stamped by `EnemyController` on its attack hitbox for `EnemyTier.Standard` and by `EnemyAbilityExecutor` on the pooled shots a standard mob fires; reset by `EnemyProjectile.Setup`). `PlayerController.ResolveHitLaunches` never launches the Story player for such a hit — grounded hitstun with a horizontal slide, no tumble, no DI, no knockdown — **unless the payload authors `Launches`**. Elites, bosses and hazards keep the legacy rule. Story-only; the sim never reads the field. | Unassigned (user ruling) | A ruling on which enemy/boss/hazard hits launch (amending `VERIFY-ABILITY-LAUNCHES`); if per-ability, author an `EnemyAbilityData` launch flag and retire the tier stamp. |
| **PROVISIONAL-QUEUED-CHAIN-PRESS** — the basic-string input buffer (plan F3) | `design-godot.md` Basic Attack & 3-Hit Combo String: subsequent attacks chain through the 24-frame chain-hold/buffer window; holding attack through the window continues the chain. The plan adds: active-frame and hitstop presses buffer, and a press made after the next swing started counts for the following swing. | Both modes: a press in startup, **active frames** or the **hitstop freeze** buffers the next hit (the old active-frame exclusion is gone), holding BasicAttack at the recovery end or through the hold continues the chain (`BasicComboRules.ChainContinues`), and — beyond the plan's wording — a **second** press made while the next hit is already buffered is queued for the hit after it (Story `_queuedAttackBuffered`; sim `FighterBasicAttackRules.FlagQueuedBuffered`, a spare `AttackFlags` bit). Without the queue, the probe's "three presses on a 12-frame rhythm" still gave two hits (the 2nd and 3rd presses both fall inside hit 1). One deep, so the authored string pace is unchanged — N presses give at most N swings, never a faster one. Time Freeze discards, never buffers. | Unassigned (user ruling) | The user keeps the one-deep queue or reverts it to a single buffer (drop the queue flag in both modes and the two `ThreePresses…` pins in `FeelPassStoryTests` / `FeelPassSimTests`). |
| **F2-SINGLE-FRAME-CLOCK** — what times a player hitbox (plan F2) | `design-godot.md:1018`: hitboxes and hurtboxes are "driven by **AnimatedSprite2D frame callbacks** for frame-perfect accuracy"; `:1035`: "An `AnimatedSprite2D` frame callback activates the hitbox `Area2D` at the authored active frames" (also `:1248`, and `:2517` for Joan). | No sprite or `AnimationPlayer` callback drives any player hitbox. Every Story swing (template-timed or authored-profile) and every `BaseSpecial` phase runs on `PlayerController`'s physics frame clock from the `BasicComboRules` / `AbilityData` frame tables — the one clock hitstop suspends. `CharacterFactory` no longer builds a `CombatAnimationPlayer`, and `resources/Animations/placeholder_combat_animation_library.tres` (a second copy of the frame tables, on an idle clock hitstop never paused) is deleted. Sprites are posed from the windows (`AttackPoseRules`); the sim's phase machine was already frame-clocked. Raised by the round-1 FEEL review. | Unassigned | Amend `design-godot.md` §Combat Mechanics (`:1018`, `:1035`, `:1248`) to name the physics frame clock as the hitbox authority with the sprite posed from it; a production animation library must be driven from that clock, never drive it. `FeelPassStoryTests.HitstopExtendsTemplateAndAuthoredProfileSwingsByExactlyTheFreeze` and `StoryCombatRulesTests.NoSecondAttackClockDuplicatesTheFrameTables` stay green. |
| **SIM-SPECIAL-POSE-THIRDS** — Fighter presentation of on-press Specials (plan F1) | `design-godot.md:3878` (the retro 3-frame contract): each animation's three key poses are stretched across the authored frame windows; plan F1 poses Specials from their authored startup/active/recovery. | The sim resolves Specials on press (`DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`), so `FighterSimulationDriver` poses a Special's (and the generic `ultimate` sheet's) cosmetic hold in equal thirds of its 18 `AbilityPresentationFrames` (`AttackPoseRules.PoseForHold`), not the authored windows; the same Special shows its strike pose at different times in the two modes. Posed from real sim phases: basic swings (`AttackPhase`), Tubman's Foresight and — since fix round R11 — the activation strike for its whole duration (`FighterUltimateActivationRules.IsCasterBusy`: wind-up, strike, the cinematic held on the strike pose as Story poses the Mirror clone's Ultimate, the 45-frame whiff recovery on the follow-through, with no cosmetic hold trailing it; round 1 left the whiff-recovery branch unreachable). The movement ability is never posed in either mode: it plays as a reel (`FighterSimulationDriver.PosesAbilityHold`, R11; round 1 posed it in thirds in Fighter only). Presentation only, read-only over sim state. Raised by the round-1 FEEL review. | Unassigned | Closes with `DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`: when sim Specials gain phases the driver poses them from those phases and the thirds path is deleted. `FeelPassSimTests.ThePresentationPosesFollowTheSimPhases`, `TheWholeActivationStrikeIsPosedFromItsPhase` and `TheFighterMovementAbilityPlaysItsReelRatherThanAPose` pin today's mapping. |
| **ZONE-RESOLVED-HIT-FREEZE** — a Story/Fighter difference in which ability hits freeze | `design-godot.md:1020-1023` ("Hitstop / Hitlag"): every landed direct player-authored hit — specials and ultimates included — freezes both parties; zone and DoT ticks are exempt. The design does not say which multi-pulse abilities are impacts and which are ticks. | Story authors Leonardo's Golden Ratio (`leonardo/special_1.tres`), Mozart's Requiem Chord (`mozart/special_1.tres`, whose burst pulses inherit it) and all nine Ultimates `Delivery = 0` (`HitDelivery.DirectHit`), so since F5 their first landed hit per execution freezes the caster (`PlayerController.AbilityHitFreezesCaster`) and each landed hit freezes a mob or boss victim. The sim delivers the same hits as zone pulses with `appliesHitstop: false` — Golden Ratio as zone type 21, the Requiem burst as `FighterReachKitRules.RequiemBurstZone`, and every Ultimate's cinematic as one ultimate-slot zone (type `CharacterID × 10 + 3`, `FighterUltimateRules.SpawnCinematicZone`) — so in Fighter Mode neither fighter freezes on them. `PlayerController.ApplyAbilityCasterHitstop`'s doc comment still says "The Fighter simulation already freezes both parties of every direct hit", which is not true for these. Raised by the round-1 FEEL review. | Unassigned | A ruling on whether these hits are impacts (the sim's zone pulses for them pass `appliesHitstop: true` with a once-per-execution caster freeze) or ticks (Story authors them `Tick`); then one parity test pins both modes and the comment is corrected. |
| **F5-CASTER-FREEZE-ATTRIBUTION** — which cast a late hit's caster freeze belongs to (fix round R12) | `design-godot.md:1020` freezes both parties of each landed direct hit; plan F5 limits the caster's freeze to once per execution. The design does not cover a hit that lands after the next cast began. | A pooled shot carries the `BaseSpecial.ExecutionSerial` of the cast that fired it (`PlaceholderProjectile.SourceExecutionSerial` → `Hitbox.SourceExecutionSerial` → `HitPayload.SourceExecutionSerial`, stamped by `BaseSpecial.SpawnPlaceholderProjectile`), and so do the deferred second stages (the E=mc² burst, Yorick's sonic wave, the Requiem Chord's burst pulses); 0 means the current execution. `BaseSpecial.TryClaimCasterHitstop(serial)` claims **monotonically** (a serial at or below the last claimed one is refused), so a late shot from cast N never spends cast N+1's freeze. Residuals: a late shot from a cast that never froze does not freeze once a newer cast already has (a rare under-freeze, never a double one), and a late shot that does claim still freezes the caster in whatever it is doing now. Before R12 the freeze went to whichever execution was current when the hit landed (round-1 FEEL review). `MozartRequiemChord` tracks one chord and one burst at a time, so two chords in flight would cross-attribute; that is unreachable at the authored 7 s cooldown against a 3 s chord lifetime. | Unassigned | A ruling that the monotonic rule is the intent, or a per-execution claim set. `FeelPassStoryTests.ALateShotFromAnEarlierCastNeverSpendsTheCurrentCastsFreeze` and the three deferred-stage cases (`ADeferredEmc2Burst…`, `ADeferredYorickWave…`, `ARequiemBurstIsChargedToTheCastThatFiredTheChord`) pin today's rule. |

## Confirmed open deviations — playtest feel pass 2026-10-04, fix round (level bugs)

The fix round's level items (fix-level G1, G3, G4, G6; G8 was not a bug — see `VERIFY-AREAPULSE-WALLS`), each from a game bug the Story playtest bots surfaced (`docs/PLAYTEST_FEEL_2026-10-04_PLAN.md` §3). Observed in `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-07 (uncommitted on `5d3ce25`). Closed rows record build fixes on headless evidence and are kept for their history. Nobody has played any of it; `play`-bot runs on the fix tree (Joan, Easy) completed Levels 3, 9 and 12.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**FIX-L06-WINCH-DETECTION**~~ — Pompeii's rockfall winch (G1) | `PACKAGE5_CAMPAIGN_PLAN.md` L06: the rockfall winch puzzle is solvable; Package 12 W8 (M02) moved the movable weights onto Environment. | **Closed (fix round G1).** `PressurePlateTemplate.tscn`'s `collision_mask` was 513 (Player + PersistentObject, the props' old layer), so no `WeightedObject` entered a plate by physics — only the west pan's authored load was registered, by hand — and Godot does not wake a sleeping `RigidBody2D` when the static shape under it is disabled, so the pumice stayed asleep at (4260, 320) after its wedge broke and the rockfall at x ≈ 5400 never opened. Now the mask is 65 (Player + Environment; `PressurePlate.RegisterBody` still weighs only `WeightedObject`s and the hero, so floors and walls weigh nothing), and `DamageableEnvironmentObject.DestroyObject` wakes every `movable_weight` body within `RestingWeightWakeMarginPixels` (64) of the broken shape, deferred behind the deferred shape write inside a physics flush. Level 6 is the only campaign user of plates and weights. **Still open, bot side:** the `play` bot does not know to stand on the east pan as the missing counterweight once the pumice lands there (RightPan 2 of 3), idles at the rockfall (≈5352, 900) and collapses on the timer. | Fix round G1 | Closed on headless evidence: `Level06RockfallPhysicsTests` (2: the template sees the player and the weights; on the real Level 6 scene, breaking the wedge drops the pumice onto the east pan, the hero balances the winch and the rockfall opens). A `PlaytestRouteHints` step for the east pan would let the bots measure Level 6. |
| **LIFT-STOP-RULE** — moving-platform endpoints (G3) | Bot game bug 7 (plan §3): the Level 12 spire lift descended onto a hero standing in its footprint and drove the hero through the floor and out of the world. The design gives no endpoint rule. | Build fix with a content contract: every `PathMovingPlatform` stop and `TrapdoorPlatform` leaf in Levels 1–15 stands clear of static geometry or sinks into it as a **flush walk-on stop** — top on the solid's top (±0.5 px) and no deeper than the solid (`ArenaGeometryContentTests.NoMovingPlatformEndpointOverlapsStaticGeometry`, at least 29 endpoints, through the new `PathMovingPlatform.AuthoredOrigin`). The refused shape is the half-sunk stop: the spire lift's 240 × 32 deck stopped centred at 1900, its top 16 px proud of the 1900 regolith and its bottom 16 px inside it. It now stops flush — scene position 7050/1916, waypoint −1516 (the top stop is unchanged at 400) — and `Level12Controller.Lifts` reads `SpireLiftBottomY` (`SurfaceY + 16`), pinned to the scene. Level 14's cargo lift keeps its existing flush stop on the 1700 deck (an interim fix-round change that made it a 32 px step on the critical path, which standard mobs cannot climb, was not kept). Level 7's SkiffA west stop moved −140 → −80, because its hull slid 60 px into the gun pier (`Level07Controller.Skiffs` changed with it). | Unassigned | None beyond the user's review. `TheEndpointContractRefusesAHalfSunkStopAndAllowsAFlushOrClearOne`, `Level12ContentTests.TheLiftTableIsTheAuthoredSceneAndTheSpireLiftBoardsWithoutAStep` and `Level14ContentTests.TheCargoLiftBoardsWithoutAStepAtBothDecks` stay green. |
| **PROVISIONAL-LIFT-HOLD-OVER-HERO** — a descending deck and a hero beneath it (G3) | The same bug; the design is silent on elevator safety. | *Provisional.* A descending `PathMovingPlatform` whose next step would overlap a body on `CrushGuardMask` (the Player layer only) whose origin lies more than `RiderFeetTolerancePixels` (2) below the deck top **holds** (`IsHeldByBodyBelow`, zero `PlatformVelocity`) and resumes once the hero steps out. Riders are carried as before; sideways and upward moves are untouched; one shape query per platform is reused. Enemies are deliberately not guarded, so a mob patrolling a bottom stop, or holding under a hero riding down (P4), cannot park a lift in mid-air. The alternative — the deck carries or pushes the hero out — was not built. | Unassigned | A user ruling (hold above the hero, or push/carry). `LiftDescentPhysicsTests` (3: a lift holds above a hero and lands once the hero steps out; a rider is carried all the way down; a flush walk-on stop holds, then lands level and is walked across) pins today's rule. |
| **PREBOSS-BEAT-AFTER-DEFEAT** — the post-boss chain and a late pre-boss trigger (G4) | N01: boss defeat → defeat dialogue → the sealing anchor arms → a single Interact seals → vignette → results; the level's pre-boss beat (`level_NN.preboss`) leads into the fight. | Bot game bug 8 (plan §3): on Level 5 a bot fought the Tidal Overseer short of its arena, then struck the PreBoss fracture beside the pre-boss trigger; once the sealing anchor never armed, once `level_05.preboss` played after `level_05.postboss`. Root cause: `DialogueManager.StartSequence(id)` drops a sequence requested over an active one but still returns true, so the post-boss chain waited for a completion that never came. Fix round G4, confined to `StoryLevelControllerBase` (`DialogueManager` is unchanged; an interim change to its return contract broke `Level15ContentTests`, `EpilogueFlowTests` and `NearCaptureBeatTests` and was reverted): the base's post-boss and exit beats go through `RunBeatWhenDialogueFree`, queued while `IsDialogueSequenceActive` and released from the completion event after the usual beat delay, and the pre-boss triggers of Levels 5, 12 and 15 go through `StartPreBossDialogue`, which plays nothing once `IsBossDefeated`, `SealAccepted` or `LevelComplete`. *Provisional:* a pre-boss beat whose trigger is first crossed after the boss fell is skipped for good, not deferred; a PreBoss fracture struck after the defeat leaves AwaitingSeal and the seal chain alone. Every other dialogue call is unchanged. Not changed: a checkpoint struck after level completion still runs `SaveManager.SaveCheckpoint` (reachable only with a fracture beside a sealing anchor). | Unassigned | A ruling only if the skipped lead-in should play somewhere else. `PostBossChainTests` (6, including the real Titanic scene where the Overseer falls first) pins today's chain. |
| ~~**FIX-L09-EXTRACTOR-SLIVERS**~~ — Extractors that leave a sliver of deck (G6) | Bot game bug 10 (plan §3): Level 9's watchtower Extractor left about 14 px of its walkway to stand on. | **Closed (fix round G6).** `Level09Controller.ExtractorPlacements` (IDs unchanged): `level_09.extractor_watchtower` (5620, 480) → (6194, 440), flush on the east end of the walkway deck (5550–6250 at 512); `level_09.extractor_radar_mast` (7130, 580) → (7404, 540), the same fault on the 7040–7460 gantry at 612. The scene's `FeedRelayTower` block moved x 6150 → 6020 to make room (ID and condition unchanged). | Fix round G6 | Closed on headless evidence: `ArenaGeometryContentTests.NoExtractorLeavesASliverOfTheDeckItStandsOn` (no Extractor leaves less than a 40 px hero width of a deck it stands on, pedestals exempt, over every level). A `play` run on Level 9 Easy completed. |

## Confirmed open deviations — the ability lockout (special global cooldown), 2026-10-08

A user-directed combat rule, built in both modes on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-08 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`). The rule and its 30 frames live once in `FTT.Combat.BasicComboRules` (`AbilityLockoutFrames`, `IsAbilityLocked`, `TickAbilityLockout`); Story keeps the counter on `PlayerController`, the sim in `FighterRuntimeComponent.AbilityLockoutFrames` (component 305's first int, the unread `BasicCooldownFrames` reused, so 305 stays exactly 128 B and no component ID was allocated). Suites: `AbilityLockoutRulesTests`, `StoryAbilityLockoutTests`, `FighterAbilityLockoutTests`. Nobody has played it.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-ABILITY-LOCKOUT** — a special global cooldown | **User direction 2026-10-08:** "half a second for using specials after using other abilities — the normal string attack should continue to behave as expected", preventing specials "at the same time as normal attacks or other specials/ultimates". Rulings asked and given: a basic swing arms it; the Ultimate is locked too; the movement ability is not involved; both modes. This supersedes `design-godot.md` :782 (Attacking → UsingSpecial "if special off cooldown"), :1034 and :1487 ("a special or the ultimate cancels a swing at any point" / "at any time"), the Cooldown System bullets :1094–1103 (per-slot cooldowns are the only input block), :3900 ("legal basic-to-Special/Ultimate cancels") and the CPU rows :3994 / :4009 (specials chained straight off a basic). **Amended by the user's 2026-10-11 rulings (`DECISIONS.md` D1, D1a, D10 and its addendum):** a basic swing that **lands** a hit may cancel straight into Special 1 or Special 2 (hit-confirm Specials only); whiffs still lock; a Special accepted within the last 30 frames keeps blocking Specials; **the Ultimate keeps the 30-frame lockout after every swing, landed or not, in both modes** (no hit-confirm for the Ultimate). **Landed** (D1a) = damage was applied, or a shield or barrier layer absorbed it; not a block-stance block, an invulnerability / arena-ward / Time Freeze / world-hold refusal, a whiff, or a hit on a construct, prop or strike surface. A Special 1/2 press during the hitstop freeze of a landed hit fires on the first tick after the freeze if it is legal then (**the freeze-only Special buffer**, both modes; not a general lockout buffer; the Ultimate is not buffered). A Special 1, Special 2 or Ultimate press the lockout refuses **flashes that slot's HUD icon** (D10, both modes, visual only); refused presses are still dropped. | Starting any basic swing (each chain hit, buffered and held continuations included, the up-attack, the down-air), casting Special 1 or 2, or having the Ultimate accepted sets a 30-frame lockout; Special 1, Special 2 and the Ultimate are refused while it runs (silently, spending no cooldown or meter, arming nothing). Basics are never refused; the movement ability, roll, air dodge, grab and Echo Step neither arm nor read it. Armed on tick T, the next special or Ultimate is legal on T + 30 in both modes. A special or the Ultimate still cancels a swing once the lockout has run out (late in grounded hit 3, in the chain hold). **Amended 2026-10-11 by `USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL` (D1):** a swing that lands releases Special 1/2 from its own lockout. Whiffs still lock. Special → Special and every swing → Ultimate keep the full 30 frames. **Completed at the 2026-10-11 finalize step:** "lands" is the D1a predicate (Story: the receiver's report, `Hurtbox.ReportLanded` → `HitPayload.ContactLanded`; sim: `FighterDamageRules.ResolveFighterHit` = `Landed` or `Absorbed`; a zero-damage payload never lands, the orchestrator's ruling). Specials read `BasicComboRules.SpecialGateFrames(A, S, C)` and the Ultimate reads A alone (`USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL`). A refusal is no longer silent: a press the lockout alone refused flashes its slot (`USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`). A Special press inside the attacker freeze of the fighter's own landed swing is held one deep and re-presented on the thaw tick (`USER-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`, `PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`); every other refused press is dropped. Per-hit Momentum (`USER-PER-HIT-MOMENTUM`) refunds both Special cooldowns on every landed swing. | User (design amendment) | The GDD master (`design-godot-v7.md`) amended at the lines cited and re-synced to the mirror; a hands-on session confirms 0.5 s reads right in both modes. The 2026-10-11 amendments are accepted through `USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL`, `USER-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER` and `USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`. |
| **PROVISIONAL-ABILITY-LOCKOUT-RULES** — details the direction left open | The direction gives the duration, the arming and the gated actions only. | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* (1) armed at **acceptance**, not at the cast's end. The sim fires specials on press (`DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`), and every Story Special runs 30+ frames, so in Story the lockout mostly bites through a block-cancelled Special recovery or a triggered Foresight. (2) It pauses in the fighter's own hitstop in both modes, and under Time Freeze in Story, like the ability cooldowns; it is never scaled by Story cooldown perks and never persisted. (3) Cleared by a Story death rewind, a checkpoint restore and the F22 Sudden Death reset; left to expire on a stock loss. (4) **Same-tick order, both modes:** a basic swing (including a chain continuation starting that tick: Story's `ChainSwingStartsThisTick` mirrors the sim, which starts swings in `FighterMovementSystem` before any ability is polled) → an Ultimate the meter pays for (`FighterCombatSystem.UltimatePressAccepted`; in the sim it now also takes the tick from the **movement ability**, as Story always polled it first) → Special 1 → Special 2. In the sim an accepted entity-system special also takes the tick from the movement ability, as Story polls specials first. The generic Special 1 block resolves before Tubman's Foresight and Joan's Divine Piercing. Lincoln's Splitting Strike no longer yields to an unpaid Ultimate press. A fighter that an activation strike captures this tick takes no further action that tick; without that, the ability system's early Ultimate claim let a held-back special reach `BuildIntent` as a phantom melee hit. (5) Refusal is silent: no cue, no input buffer, no HUD display of the lockout. (6) The CPU reads `CpuDecisionObservation.AbilityLockoutFrames` as it will stand at delivery (≤ the band's minimum reaction delay). Its Echo Step meter valuation ignores the lockout. While a viable Ultimate waits on the CPU's own lockout, it stops pressing BasicAttack, so a buffered chain hit cannot re-arm the lockout past the string. **Amended 2026-10-11 (D1, `PROVISIONAL-HIT-CONFIRM-RULES`):** item (3) — the clears extend to the D1 Special-sourced lockout S, the swing-landed latch C and the freeze-only Special buffer (Story `ClearAbilityLockout`, sim `FighterAbilityLockoutRules.Clear`); a stock loss still lets A and S expire on the respawn platform and leaves C, but drops a held freeze-buffer press. Item (6) — the CPU's Special rungs (close S1, close S2, ranged S1) and its mobility-Special check read the D1 Special gate at delivery (`SpecialLockoutClearAtDelivery`); the Ultimate commit and the wait rule keep the lockout. **Amended 2026-10-11 (D10, agent A2):** item (5) "Refusal is silent … no HUD display" is superseded by `USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`: a refusal the lockout alone caused now flashes that slot's HUD icon. There is still no lockout timer on screen, and no buffer beyond the freeze-only Special buffer (`PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`). **Amended 2026-10-11 (the user's rulings, recorded at the finalize step):** item (1) — beside A, every Special 1/2 and Ultimate acceptance arms S, the Special-sourced lockout, which ticks with A; a landed swing then gates Specials on S alone, while the Ultimate always reads A (`USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL`). Item (4) — the same-tick order gains one rule: a meter-paid Ultimate held back only by the lockout claims the tick from Special 1/2, never from the movement ability (`PROVISIONAL-HIT-CONFIRM-RULES` (4)). Item (5) — "no input buffer" is amended by the user's freeze-only Special buffer (`USER-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`): a Special 1/2 press inside the attacker freeze of the fighter's own landed swing is held and re-presented on the thaw tick, where the ordinary gates judge it; nothing else is buffered and the Ultimate never is. | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a playtest that leaves them alone. A HUD display or an input buffer for a refused press, if wanted, is new work with a localization and HUD-contract pass. |
| **VERIFY-SAME-TICK-VERB-PARITY** — pre-existing Story/sim same-tick differences the lockout reviews surfaced | One input, one outcome in both modes. The design authors no same-tick verb priority beyond `BasicComboRules.SelectRecoveryVerb`. | None of these were introduced by the lockout, and none were changed. (1) The sim accepts the Ultimate from the block stance, shieldstun and the 12-frame landing-tech lock; Story never polls it there. The sim's tech branch also leaves input live for specials. (2) In a swing's recovery or the chain hold, Story's special or Ultimate beats a same-tick Roll, Block or Echo Step chord, while the sim lets those win. With Jump, the sim does both the jump and the special. (3) On the tick an aerial string lands, Story returns to Idle and drops a same-tick special press that the sim casts. (4) From neutral, a same-tick Jump or Roll + BasicAttack jumps or rolls in Story but swings in the sim. (5) A melee-intent special (Lincoln's Splitting Strike) still loses to a same-tick movement ability in the sim, because it resolves in `BuildIntent` after the movement ability starts. (6) On a tick the opponent grabs or captures the fighter, a same-tick Ultimate + special press yields nothing. **(7) Added 2026-10-11** — two pre-existing offsets that D1 makes player-facing: (a) Story's basic-swing box opens one tick after the sim's for the same press (Story's press tick runs no swing clock, so elapsed 6 = T+7 against the sim's T+6), and its contact reaches the attacker one physics flush later; (b) the sim reads S1/S2/Ultimate presses on a fighter's last hitstop tick (the movement system decrements 1 → 0 and continues; the entity and combat systems read 0), while Story drops every frozen tick. Net: a confirm through a freeze is legal 2 ticks earlier in the sim (T+10 against T+12 after the template opener), 1 tick earlier with no freeze. The freeze-only Special buffer (`PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`) makes (b) moot for presses made inside the freeze: each mode fires them on its own thaw tick (the sim's last freeze tick, Story's first unfrozen tick). | Unassigned | A ruling on each, then one shared same-tick table (in the manner of `SelectRecoveryVerb`) read by both modes, with parity pins. |
| **VERIFY-GAMEPAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD** — pre-existing input defects surfaced by the lockout | `COMFORT_SETTINGS.md` C01c: the gamepad Ultimate is the Movement Ability + Special 2 preset chord ("LB+RB at defaults"). | (a) Nothing suppresses the chord's component presses (`InputManager.SampleGodotDevice`). Pressed on the same tick, the Ultimate now wins in both modes (above). Pressed a frame apart, the first button casts its own verb: a Special 2 then arms the lockout and the chord's Ultimate is refused. Story's `UsingSpecial` state already refused it before the lockout; the sim used to fire both. (b) **Fixed 2026-10-11** (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`). `project.godot` bound Special 2 to joypad button 5, the movement ability to 4 and the chord to 4 + 5. In Godot 4's `JoyButton` enum those are **Back** and **Guide**, not the shoulders (9 / 10); "LB+RB" was the Godot 3 numbering. Special 2 therefore sat on the Guide button, which the OS or Steam usually claims, and the movement ability shared Back with Time Freeze, so a gamepad movement press in Story also froze time (`TimeFreezeController` polls `Input.IsActionJustPressed`). The defaults are now the GDD Controls table's: Movement Ability `LeftShoulder` (9), Special 2 `RightShoulder` (10), the chord 9 + 10. Time Freeze moved to L3 (7) by user direction on 2026-10-11 (`USER-GAMEPAD-TIME-FREEZE-L3`). `GamepadDefaultBindingTests` (4 cases, read from `ProjectSettings`, all four failing on the old numbers) pins both shoulders, the chord equal to its recipe's component defaults, no authored action on Guide, and no default button shared by two actions outside the chord. Saved overrides get no schema migration; a load-time repair rewrites the stale rows the Controls tab could not have made on purpose (`PROVISIONAL-GAMEPAD-OVERRIDE-NO-MIGRATION`). (c) Found while fixing (b), not changed: at runtime the gamepad chord is not the C01c recipe. It is the Ultimate action's own two joypad events, which `InputManager.ReadUltimatePressed` reads as a conjunction. So the gamepad direct slot is not "initially Unbound" (the chord fills it, and capturing a direct gamepad button replaces the chord instead of adding to it); the Controls tab's Ultimate-chord On/Off toggle changes nothing at runtime (only Grab and Echo Step consult `IsShortcutEnabled`); and remapping the Movement Ability or Special 2 changes the chord's label, which renders the components, but not the chord. **2026-10-11 (D1, §1.7 of spec A):** a meter-paid Ultimate held back only by the lockout claims the tick from Special 1 and Special 2 in both modes (`PROVISIONAL-HIT-CONFIRM-RULES` (4)), so the chord pressed inside a hit-confirm cannot cast its Special 2; its movement-ability component is unchanged and fires as today. The freeze-only Special buffer holds nothing from a tick that presses the Ultimate, so a same-tick chord's Special 2 is never held either; a split chord whose Special 2 half lands first inside the freeze is held and cast alone at the thaw, as (a) already does outside a freeze (`PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`). | Unassigned | (a) A ruling on chord recognition (a short chord window, or masking the component edges when the chord forms). (b) Met 2026-10-11. (c) The gamepad Ultimate recognized from the recipe's live component bindings behind its shortcut flag, with the direct gamepad slot authored Unbound (C01c), or a ruling that the build's shape stands. |
| **PROVISIONAL-GAMEPAD-OVERRIDE-NO-MIGRATION** — saved binding rows that still carry the Godot 3 shoulder numbers | C01c: preserve independent device overrides; no binding is silently stolen. The task that moved the defaults (2026-10-11) ruled out a save-schema bump. | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* no schema migration, and a load-time repair instead. `InputBindingService.BuildOverrides` saves only actions that differ from the project defaults, so a profile that never touched the Movement Ability, Special 2 or the Ultimate inherits the new shoulders at once. A profile that rebound any of them, even on the keyboard only, saved that action's whole event list, so its row still holds the old gamepad half (4, 5, or 4 + 5). Most such rows are not ambiguous: a Controls-tab capture replaces a device half with exactly one event (there is no chord editor), and `InputBindingConflicts.FindConflictingAction` refuses an event any action already holds, so under the old defaults Back and Guide could not be captured at all. `InputBindingSet.RepairGodot3ShoulderRows(projectDefaults)` (pure data, idempotent, no stamp) therefore runs from `SaveManager.ApplySavedInputBindings` just before `InputBindingService.Apply` on every load, rewriting a stale gamepad half onto the action's current project default and keeping the rest of the row: (1) an Ultimate half of exactly Back + Guide, always — only the old default has that shape; (2) a Movement Ability half of exactly Back, or a Special 2 half of exactly Guide, only while another action's effective gamepad half (its row, nothing if its gamepad slot is Unbound, else its default; judged on the payload as loaded) also holds that button — until 2026-10-11 the typical case was the Movement Ability sharing Back with Time Freeze's default (the double trigger of `VERIFY-GAMEPAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD` (b)). Since D11 moved Time Freeze to L3, a stale Movement-on-Back row is repaired only while another loaded row holds Back: a saved Time Freeze row that kept Back, a stale Ultimate chord of Back + Guide (judged as loaded, before its own repair), or any other action saved on Back. The commonest stale profile — a pre-fix keyboard-only Movement rebind with no Time Freeze row — is therefore no longer repaired: it keeps the Movement Ability on Back (it works; nothing double-fires), LB does nothing alone for that profile, and the Controls chord label, which renders the recipe's components, reads "Pad Button 4 + Pad Button 10" while the runtime chord stays LB + RB (`VERIFY-GAMEPAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD` (c)). A profile that captured L3 for another action while L3 was free now shares it with Time Freeze's default, the same undetected class as the pre-fix LB/RB captures. The repaired rows persist with the next global save. Left alone, because after the fix they may be deliberate: a lone Special 2 on Guide (Guide is now free to capture), and a Movement Ability on Back that no other loaded row shares (the commonest case since Time Freeze moved off Back, above). Not detected either: a profile that captured LB (9) or RB (10) for another action before the fix, when the shoulders were bound to nothing, now shares that button with the moved Movement Ability or Special 2 default, so one press fires two verbs; `FindConflictingAction` runs only at capture and nothing scans for a duplicate at load, and no automatic choice between the player's bind and the moved default is safe. The build is unreleased, so only development profiles are affected; the Controls tab's per-action reset or Reset All clears every case. `GamepadOverrideRepairTests` (5, pure C#) pins the rule and `InputBindingSchemaTests.SaveManagerRepairsAStaleGodot3UltimateChordBeforeApplyingIt` the load path. | Unassigned | Either accept it, or (i) a load-time scan for a joypad button shared by two effective actions outside the Ultimate chord, with a Controls notice naming both, for the pre-fix shoulder captures; (ii) a ruling on whether the lone Special 2 on Guide **and the lone Movement Ability on Back (unrepaired since D11)** should be repaired too (a one-shot flag on the global payload, which belongs to the next package's single schema bump). |

## Confirmed open deviations — movement feel (snappier run, jump arc, ramped fast-fall), 2026-10-08

A user-directed movement change, built in both modes on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-08 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`, beside the ability lockout above). The shared numbers live once in `FTT.Core.UniversalMovementRules` (`RunAccelerationFrames`, `RunDecelerationFrames`, `ApexHangSpeedBand`, `ApexHangGravityMultiplier`, `FallGravityMultiplier`, `TerminalFallSpeed`, `LaunchTerminalFallSpeed`, `FastFallSpeed`, `FastFallGravityMultiplier`). Story's own air constants and its per-tick step live in the new `FTT.Core.StoryAirPhysics` (`StepVertical`, called by `PlayerController.ApplyGravity`; plain C#, which also integrates jump arcs for the level code, the bots and the content tests), the sim's in the new `FTT.FighterSim.FighterAirPhysics` (`StepMovement`, `StepLaunch`, `RampStep`, `HasJumpedThisAirtime`). No component, snapshot field or protocol change; every Fighter hash moved. Suites: `StoryAirPhysicsTests`, `StoryMovementFeelTests`, `FighterMovementFeelTests`, plus one pin each in `Level09ContentTests`, `Level10ContentTests`, `TutorialCalibrationContentTests` and `FighterCpuBehaviorTests`. The rope-bar fix that `VERIFY-MOVEMENT-FEEL-FALLOUT` (a) asked for followed on 2026-10-09, also uncommitted (`PendulumAnchorTemplate.tscn`'s rope body on layer 0, `PlaytestNav`'s rope model, `PendulumRopeBarTests`). The Level 10 recess walls followed the same day, also uncommitted (`VERIFY-L10-ARENA-RECESS-EXIT`, the hero half), and then the relocation rule and the arena fall net that closed its boss half (`FTT.Combat.RelocationRules` / `SafeRelocation`; suites `RelocationRulesTests`, `SafeRelocationTests`, `BossArenaFallNetTests`, `Level10BossRelocationTests`), with their agent-chosen details in `PROVISIONAL-RELOCATION-CLEARANCE` and `PROVISIONAL-ARENA-FALL-NET`. A review of that fix found the net misfiring in Level 4's walkable Phase 2 pit and the rule reading a body on a ledge as already over nothing; both were fixed the same day, also uncommitted (`VERIFY-L04-PIT-FALL-NET`, closed: a 240 px margin under every arena's edge, and the origin's support read across the body's width; one new case each in `RelocationRulesTests`, `BossArenaFallNetTests`, `ArenaGeometryContentTests` and `Level04ContentTests`). A second review closed two of the rule's three remaining limits the same day, also uncommitted (`PROVISIONAL-RELOCATION-CLEARANCE`: the body's own box is swept along the travel beside the three wall rays, and a summon emerges from the part of its summoner that stands on something; two new cases each in `RelocationRulesTests` and `SafeRelocationTests`). Nobody has played it.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-MOVEMENT-FEEL** — snappier run, a hanging jump, a heavier fall, a slower ramped fast-fall | **User direction 2026-10-08:** "I want to make movement more snappy, currently the acceleration/decelleration is too slow and the player feels sluggish when moving. I also want to make updates to jumping. I want to increase the hang time, so reduce the gravity at the apex of the jump, and then increase the gravity as the player is falling to increase falling speed. I want to change the down ability or the down movement while jumping. Currently it's way too high, and the player falls almost instantly to the ground that should be lower. We should put a lower maximum fall speed." Rulings asked and given: everything in **both modes**; fast-fall works any time in the air but **ramps in** instead of snapping; the run ramp is **6 frames up / 4 frames to stop**. | **PROVISIONAL-MOVEMENT-FEEL-RULES** — details the direction left open | The direction gives the shape (snappier ramps, more hang, a heavier fall, a slower fast-fall under a lower cap); the rulings give both modes, the ramp-in and 6 / 4 frames. Nothing else is specified. | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* (1) The numbers: a 2 u/s hang band (120 px/s) at ×0.5; the fall at ×2.2 (Story's was 1.8, the sim had none); a shared 12 u/s terminal (Story 10 → 12, sim 20 → 12; the sim's float glide now caps at 12 too); the fast-fall cap 20 → 15 at ×3. (2) **The ease rule:** a descent already above its cap (a released fast-fall, a launch that has just ended) eases down by one base-gravity step per tick instead of snapping; one that reaches the cap on this tick stops on it. (3) **The hang's discriminator is "jumped this airtime".** The sim reads `RemainingJumps < MaxJumpCount` (its ledge grab refills jumps and its climb jump sets `MaxJumpCount − 1`). Story reads a latch, `PlayerController.JumpedThisAirtime`, set by every jump (ground, coyote, buffered, air, and a ledge or swinging-rope jump-off) and cleared by floor contact in any state, a ledge or rope grab and the Tempest Apex jump refresh, but not by a launch (the sim leaves `RemainingJumps` alone in hitstun). Story's jump budget is unchanged by ruling, which is why the count alone could not say it. So a walk-off, a drop-through or a ledge drop gets no hang, and a ledge or rope jump-off (Story) or a climb jump (sim) does. (4) **Launch states unchanged:** Story ×1.8 with a hard 600 px/s clamp; the sim flat −30 clamped at 20 (`LaunchTerminalFallSpeed`, M01's old number). (5) **Fast-fall scope:** the sim on every normal-control airborne tick (gravity-suspending kit phases excepted); Story through `Airborne`, aerial swings (the down-air keeps stacking into a falling strike) and the air dodge, **not aerial Specials**, whose startup and active frames run half-time gravity with no sim counterpart (`DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`). (6) **Story push and release ticks:** the Idle → Running push tick takes the first ramp step and the Running → Idle release tick the first stop step. Both were dead state-change ticks, so Story read 15 / 13 against the sim's 14 / 12. The Idle push tick restores the tick's starting speed before its one step, so a re-press after a one-tick release takes one step, as in the sim. (7) **Standstill turnaround:** a push against the facing turns at once unless it reverses real travel (`PushReversesTravel`: the body moves the other way faster than one 4-frame stop step). From a standstill the hero faces the push and reaches full speed on frame 6 with no skid; a real reversal at speed keeps the skid, whose first tick now takes a stop step, so a full-speed reversal takes 10 frames (4 + 6), as in the sim. A reversal begun while still sliding from Idle still pays the 3-tick skid timer and can trail the sim by up to 2 ticks. A push with Down held still crouches. (8) **FP64 round-up:** `FighterAirPhysics.RampStep` rounds the per-frame step up by one raw unit when the division is inexact; truncation (7 / 6 at the default speed) left the ramp a frame late. (9) **CPU recovery-jump revalidation:** `FighterCpuController` flags a recovery jump (`ScheduledDecision.RecoveryJump`) and strips its Jump bit at delivery when the CPU's own `RemainingJumps` is 0, keeping the steer, per `CPU_RECOVERY.md`'s "revalidate legality immediately before each planned action". No new RNG draw, no snapshot state. It stops jumps queued before the first press was observed from overwriting Medium's one pending movement-ability edge. Point-mass harness, Medium, start vy −4, 400 seeds: old physics 344 survive, new physics 319, new physics with the fix 377; Easy from a standing start below the floor 36 → 0 (not asserted, matching the contract). The CPU's pit-risk probe now predicts the hang (`Observe(..., maxJumpCount)`). Still open: jumps queued inside one reaction window can spend the second jump mid-rise (Hard too), two edges inside one 3-tick pulse still overwrite each other through `_pendingEdges`, and every recovery input still carries the full reaction delay. (10) **Level geometry:** Level 10 room 3's yard wall (x 8280) rises to the bridging span's underside (`Level10Controller.YardWallTopY` = 628, was 700), closing a hang-enabled jump over it; Level 9 room 4's east boundary wall now runs from y 0 to `GroundY` (it stopped at 440, 352 px over the east castle deck, which five heroes' double jumps already cleared and the hang added Tesla and Leonardo); Level 0's gated double-jump step moved to (1900, 540), 240 px above the first step (`Level00Controller.DoubleJumpGatedStepX/Y`), because Lincoln's single jump (~133 px) had cleared the old 128 / 130 px steps. | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a playtest that leaves them alone. `StoryMovementFeelTests`, `FighterMovementFeelTests`, `Level09ContentTests.TheEastBoundaryWallRunsTheFullHeightAndOutreachesEveryJumpFromTheCastleDeck`, `Level10ContentTests.TheYardWallLeavesNoGapUnderTheBridgeAndTheBridgeIsBeyondEveryJump`, `TutorialCalibrationContentTests.TheDoubleJumpGateNeedsTheSecondJumpFromEveryHero` and `FighterCpuBehaviorTests.ARecoveryJumpArrivingAfterTheJumpsAreSpentIsDroppedAndTheMovementAbilityStillFires` pin today's rules. |

None of the seven placed ropes (Level 7 three, Level 10 four) overrides it, no script writes a pendulum's layer or mask, and `PendulumAnchor.cs` changed in comments only. The grab point (`LedgeGrabPoint`, Trigger layer / Player mask), the carry, the release launch, the swing, Time Freeze, rewind, the `puzzle_object` group and Level 10's audience throw never read the bar. Side effects, all intended: the bar no longer stops enemy projectiles, enemy wall and edge probes, the player's melee line-of-sight check, kit ground-snaps or the Echo Step / Warp destination checks, and no hero can stand on it. `PendulumRopeBarTests` (2 cases, real physics frames) pins layer 0 / mask 0 on the template and every placed rope, with the grab box still Trigger / Player and the carry on, and sends an all-bits ray and a dropped hero through a hanging bar, the hero into its grab box; both cases failed against the old template. The bot nav model (`PlaytestNav`) lost its solid bar (bar collisions, standing on a bar, waiting for a swinging bar's gap before a jump, and the "release only while the rope swings toward the target" rule, which existed for the bar). It now follows the controller's catch rule — first entry only, not rising faster than 150 px/s, inside the 3-per-airtime grab budget it reads from a private `PlayerController` field by reflection, a grab box met on the way counting as a catch of that rope — and its release timing: the jump one frame later from the rope's next hang position, the rope's push the frame after (the old model was about 10 % out, which is why Joan kept missing rope B). It refuses a release that would only re-catch the rope just let go of, sets aside after one full swing any release that never reached its own target and re-plans from the rope, and keeps the tapped short-hop release (Joan's Level 7 B → C hop takes it). Bots, Easy `play`, Joan and Lincoln, Levels 7 and 10, seeds 0–3 (`ropefix`): all 16 runs got through the rope channels, against 2 of 16 before. Completions before → after: L7 Joan 0 → 4 of 4; L7 Lincoln 0 → 0, all four crossing and then dying around the skiffs (x 6700–7900) — seed 0's first death was in the channel's sea mid-crossing (x 5587), the one rope-area death in the 16; L10 Joan 0 → 0, all four reaching the boss (a timeout, a timer collapse, two falls out of the world); L10 Lincoln 2 → 2 (seeds 0 and 2 both times; after, seed 1 beat the boss and collapsed on the timer and seed 3 fell out of the world). Channel crossings: Level 7 Joan 20–27 s, Lincoln 68–87 s; Level 10 Joan 12–29 s, Lincoln 32–152 s. **Read the comparison with care:** it mixes the scene change with the planner change (no A/B run separates them); the four seeds are not independent (L7 Joan seeds 0 and 2 both took 99.62 s, L7 Lincoln seeds 1 and 2 died on identical frames); the "before" seed 0 is the `fixfinal` run; and an earlier batch on the same rope code before the last planner fix (`ropefixC`) scored L7 Lincoln 1/4, L10 Joan 2/4 and L10 Lincoln 3/4, so nothing shows that last fix helped. **Still open:** the human pass over both rope chains; the bot tests the 56 × 56 grab box as axis-aligned although it rotates with the rope (up to 52° on Level 7, about ±39 px), so it under-predicts stray catches on ropes it passes. **Closed since:** the Level 10 boss-arena recess that three of the six failed Level 10 runs fell out of (`VERIFY-L10-ARENA-RECESS-EXIT`, below) — walls for the hero on 2026-10-09 (`recessfix`: no run fell out, Level 10 completions 2 → 7 of 8), then the relocation rule the same day for the Tragedy King, whose teleport past the east wall, not a boss fight lost on time, was the Joan seed 0 timeout in both `ropefix` and `recessfix` (`tpfix`: Level 10 8 of 8). The remaining failures come after the ropes: Level 7 Lincoln to the mortars, Extractor discharge and the sea around the skiffs. (b) Story's aerial Specials do not fast-fall (5 above). (c) Story's jump budget is unchanged by ruling: a Story ledge grab refills no jump and the jump-off spends none, so a double jump to a ledge leaves 0 air jumps where the sim leaves 1. A King that has dropped into the recess through a Phase 2 arena trap can therefore land in x 9690–10220 at recess height, under the east board beyond the new wall, and fall forever; the level cannot then be won. `L10_joan_play_easy_recessfix.json` (seed 0): the King in the recess at (9025, 1264) on frame 5461; at (9776, 1295), Attacking, on frame 6481 with Joan in the recess at (9507, 1260); at (9704, 2218) on frame 6541; at y ≈ 2.19e8 with 249 HP at the end, while the bot idles on the board above — the 780 s timeout.
