## Confirmed deviations — reach-aware CPU, Mirror and bots, and the finisher-separation bar, 2026-10-11

Found by the 2026-10-11 code research and fixed on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`), on top of the one string-hitbox table above. The CPU is an input source, so no component, snapshot field, save field or protocol changed, but **CPU-driven Fighter hashes move** for a CPU Lincoln or Joan, and the Level 13 Mirror clone's decisions move for every hero whose band below is under 2.0. No suite pins an absolute hash. The playtest bots' fixed 92 px strike range is now each hero's own opener reach against the target's hurtbox (`StoryBot.StrikeRangeFor`), so bot batches from before and after this change are not directly comparable. Suites: `CpuMeleeBandTests` (5), `PlaytestBotStrikeRangeTests` (1), and in-place additions to `MirrorParadoxTests` and `FighterKnockbackScalingTests`. **A review fix-up the same day** made a string continuation read the band of the hit it would start instead of the close range, re-reads that step when the press is delivered, kept the plan's 2.5-unit separation floor under the per-kit bar, and cached the hero's hurtbox width in the Mirror adapter: `CpuMeleeBandTests` 5 -> 8 (one case rewritten in place), `MirrorParadoxTests` +1, `FighterKnockbackScalingTests` in place. A CPU Tesla or Tubman (and every profile of 100 % reach or more) is still bit-identical, because every one of their hit bands is at least the 2.0 close range; only CPU Lincoln and Joan hashes move.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**FIX-CPU-REACH-BLIND-OPENER**~~ — the CPU pressed its string opener from beyond its reach | `design-godot.md`, CPU Fighter AI Specifications (`:3981`): the utility engine scores actions on "distance to opponent"; Easy "strictly uses basic ground attacks" (`:3990`) and Hard "executes frame-perfect basic attack strings" (`:4020`). An opener pressed from outside its own hitbox is neither. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0` `FighterCpuController.CloseRange` (2.0 units) gated the neutral BasicAttack press for every fighter. The sim opener reaches hit 1's box far edge plus the 0.5 victim half-width: Lincoln 1.86 and Joan 1.94 units, so from there to 2.0 their opener whiffed. The Level 13 Mirror clone used the same 2.0 against the hero's 40 px hurtbox, where even the template opener reaches only 1.93. Now only the close-range branch's BasicAttack fallback reads a per-fighter melee band (`FighterCpuController.MeleeBand`): hit 1's far edge from the one string table scaled by the fighter's `ReachWidthPercent` (`FighterCombatSystem.StringBoxFarEdge`, FP64), plus the target's hurtbox half-width (`CpuDecisionObservation.TargetHurtboxHalfWidthRaw`: the sim's live victim half-width, or the hero's real hurtbox from the Mirror adapter), less 0.1 unit. Inside the close range but beyond the band, the CPU walks in instead of pressing. A press that would continue the CPU's own string reads the band of the hit it would start (`CpuDecisionObservation.SelfNextStringStep`), and delivery re-reads that step on self-state and drops a press whose string has ended (fix-up, same day; see `PROVISIONAL-CPU-MELEE-BAND` (2) and (7)). Sim opener bands: Lincoln 1.76, Joan 1.84, Tesla and Tubman exactly 2.0 (the old range, so their neutral is bit-identical), Mozart and Shakespeare 2.08, Einstein 2.16, Leonardo 2.24, Cleopatra 2.32. Mirror bands against the 40 px hero: Lincoln 1.59, Joan 1.67, template 1.83, Mozart and Shakespeare 1.91, Einstein 1.99, Leonardo 2.07, Cleopatra 2.15. | Unassigned | Met: `CpuMeleeBandTests` (a CPU Lincoln or Joan 2.0 units from a standing dummy lands its first opener, where the reach-blind press whiffed; the Ultimate commit and the close Special ladder still fire at 1.9; Lincoln does not press from 1.9 during his finisher or before a hit 2 that would whiff; a press decided mid-string and delivered after the string ended is dropped; the template band equals the close range to the raw unit) and `MirrorParadoxTests.AShortReachCloneWalksInFromBeyondItsOpenerBandAgainstTheHerosRealHurtbox` (a Lincoln clone 1.8 units from the hero, past Story's real hit-1 reach, walks in; from 1.5 it swings). |
| **PROVISIONAL-CPU-MELEE-BAND** — the band's agent-chosen rules | The design names no CPU spacing number. The task that shipped the fix (2026-10-11) set the band per string hit: an opener reads hit 1's reach and a continuation the reach of the hit it would start. | *Provisional, agent-chosen.* (1) The margin is 0.1 unit (`MeleeBandMarginUnits`, also the bots' 6 px). (2) A press reads the band of the string hit it would start, `CpuDecisionObservation.SelfNextStringStep` (sim `FighterBasicAttackRules.NextChainStep`, Story `PlayerController.NextChainStepForPress`): the next hit (1 or 2) while a chain swing of hit 1 or 2 runs, any phase, the chain hold included; otherwise an opener, which covers rest, the finisher (it cannot continue) and a directional swing (it stands outside the chain). The first version exempted every press made during any swing and kept the close range for it. That was wrong twice: a press during the finisher or a directional swing can only become an opener, and Lincoln's hit 2 band is 1.845 units and Joan's 1.93, short of the 2.0 close range. Every hit band of a profile at 100 % reach or more is at least 2.0, so their behaviour did not move. (3) The close range stays 2.0 for everything else in its branch: the threat roll and block, the A02 Ultimate commit (`UltimateActivationCanConnect`), the lockout wait, the grab and the close Special ladder, and the Echo Step punish threat. (4) The approach walk-in happens only where the BasicAttack fallback would have pressed, so a fighter whose band exceeds 2.0 still closes to 2.0 before swinging and does not use its extra reach in neutral (Cleopatra, Leonardo, Einstein, Mozart, Shakespeare). (5) The Mirror reads the clone's base profile reach, not the Story AttackRange minor a Hard clone may mirror, so it walks slightly closer than it needs to. (6) A hand-built observation with no reach (0) keeps the legacy 2.0 press. (7) Delivery revalidation: a decision is delivered 4-8 (Hard), 15-20 (Normal) or 30-45 (Easy) frames after it is made, so a press admitted on a continuation band can arrive after the string has ended (the finisher ran out, the chain hold lapsed, an aerial string landed) and swing an opener. At delivery the CPU re-reads the step from its own current state, keeps the decision-time gap (the opponent read stays delayed, as `CPU_COMBAT_POLICY.md` requires), and drops the press if that step's band no longer covers the gap, walking toward the target instead. Like the recovery-jump revalidation it draws no RNG and touches no snapshot. (8) The Mirror adapter reads the hero's hurtbox width once per bound target, not every tick. | Unassigned | A user ruling or a hand-played pass against CPU Lincoln, Joan and the Mirror confirms or replaces these rules. Closing `VERIFY-SIM-HURTBOX-WIDTH` moves every sim band with the half-width (the template band then drops below 2.0, so Tesla and Tubman CPUs walk in too, and `CpuMeleeBandTests.ATemplateReachBandIsExactlyTheCloseRangeSoItsNeutralIsUnchanged` fails by design); re-run `CpuMeleeBandTests` then. |
| ~~**FIX-FINISHER-SEPARATION-BAR**~~ — the finisher-separation guarantee was pegged to a retired range | Gameplay-feel plan §2.5 (2026-08-10): the 4.5× finisher must leave the victim out of melee range when its hitstun ends, so the string ends in separation rather than a loop, with "≥ 2.5 units horizontal separation". That 2.5-unit floor is kept. Because the opener is what restarts the loop, a second bar is added on top of it (agent-derived, 2026-10-11): the kit's own hit-1 pivot reach plus 0.5 unit. The test asserts the larger of the two. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** `FighterKnockbackScalingTests` pinned a flat 2.5 units, "the 2-unit melee range plus a margin", and `BasicComboRules.KnockbackMultipliers` cited a nonexistent `FighterFinisherSeparationTests`. The V7.1 reach profiles had already retired the 2-unit range: Cleopatra's opener reaches 2.42. Measured 2026-10-11 with the suite's own protocol (full victim HP, mirror match), separation against the new per-kit bar: Cleopatra 3.19 / 2.92, Leonardo 3.71 / 2.84, Tesla 3.81 / 2.60, Einstein 3.95 / 2.76, Tubman 4.00 / 2.60, Shakespeare 4.22 / 2.68, Joan 4.33 / 2.44, Mozart 4.42 / 2.68, Lincoln 5.11 / 2.36. Every kit clears it. The test now asserts the larger of the 2.5 floor and the per-kit bar, read from the live box and hurtbox. The first version of this fix asserted the per-kit bar alone, which for Lincoln (2.36) and Joan (2.44) is below §2.5's locked 2.5 units, so a later change could have cut their separation to 2.4 and stayed green; the review fix-up the same day restored the floor. The comment now names the real test. No multiplier or width changed. | Unassigned | Met: `FighterKnockbackScalingTests.TheFinisherSeparatesEveryKitBeyondMeleeRangeAtFullVictimHP` passes against the larger of the 2.5 floor and the per-kit bar. If a later reach or knockback change fails it, bring it to the user (a width % or the finisher multiplier) rather than loosening the bar. |

## Confirmed deviations — the Story damage buff and ability-damage routing, 2026-10-11

Found by the 2026-10-11 code research and fixed on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`). **Story-only:** nothing under `scripts/FighterSim/` was touched, and no component, snapshot field, save field, schema version or protocol changed; no Fighter hash moves. Suites: the new `StoryAbilityDamageRoutingTests` (5), `ChronalOrbItemTests` (+2), `KitAlignmentContractTests` (+1, a source scan over `scripts/Characters/Abilities/` and a repo-wide check that nothing outside `PlayerController` applies the buff), and `ResonanceReaderCoverageTests.EveryAuthoredAbilityScopeHasAProductionReader` updated in place to accept the composition as the shared `AbilityDamage` reader. With the buff left out of the composition and a pooled projectile's Hitbox carrying the whole composition, all five routing cases fail.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**FIX-STORY-DAMAGE-BUFF-KIT-ROUTING**~~ — the item-drop damage buff skipped most Specials and Ultimates | `design-godot.md`, Items & Power-Up System, Story drop system (`:3267`–`:3269`): a temporary buff item "Boosts character Damage or Speed stats by +50% for 15 seconds" on Easy and "+25% for 10 seconds" on Normal; Hard spawns none. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0`, `PlayerController.StoryTemporaryDamageMultiplier` was read only in `Hitbox.CreatePayload` and the throw. Every payload a kit resolved itself (shape queries, ground waves, bursts, the Lorentz chain, every Ultimate, construct bites, arcs and the fence, zone ticks) multiplied only `StorySpecialDamageMultiplier` (plus the scoped lane for Joan's Smite, Lincoln's Emancipator and Mozart's Fortissimo), so a damage pickup changed nothing for most of the kit. Now the three Story attacker multipliers compose once, in `PlayerController`: `StoryAbilityDamage(base, abilityID)` / `StoryAbilityDamageScale(abilityID)` = SpecialDamage × AbilityDamage(abilityID) × the buff, split into a build-time half, `StoryAbilityHitboxDamage(base)` (SpecialDamage, for a value a Hitbox carries), and a contact-time half, `StoryHitboxContactDamageScale(abilityID)` (the buff × the lane), which `Hitbox.CreatePayload` alone applies. All 29 kit damage sites in 13 kit files, `PlaceholderZone` ticks, `CharacterFactory`'s kit hitboxes and `BaseSpecial.SpawnPlaceholderProjectile` go through it, so each factor applies once on every path. The throw keeps its own read of the buff. | damage-buff-routing step (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `StoryAbilityDamageRoutingTests` (a buffed Righteous Smite and E=mc² burst deal ×1.5 once; a buffed Cosmological Constant deals 117 where it deals 78; a zone tick carries the buff once; Lincoln's factory-built Splitting Strike hitbox and E=mc²'s pooled contact land ×1.25, never ×1.5625) and `KitAlignmentContractTests.KitScriptsReadStoryAbilityDamageOnlyThroughTheOneComposition`. Nobody has played it. |
| ~~**FIX-STORY-ORB-BUFF-CASES**~~ — `ChronalOrbItem` ignored its two buff effects | `OrbEffect` authors `SpeedBuff` and `DamageBoost`; the Story buff values are the drop system's (`:3267`–`:3269`). The design also says Story campaign pickups are not orbs (`:3278`), and nothing spawns a Story orb. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0`, `ChronalOrbItem.OnPickedUp` had no case for either effect, so the pickup was consumed with no effect. Now both grant the Story drop buff through `ApplyStoryDamageBuff` / `ApplyStorySpeedBuff` at `StoryDropTable`'s profile for the current Story difficulty (`StoryDifficultyTuning.CurrentStoryDifficulty`, the drop system's own source). No orb was placed anywhere. | damage-buff-routing step (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `ChronalOrbItemTests.BuffOrbsGrantTheStoryDropBuffAtTheCurrentDifficulty` and `OnHardABuffOrbIsConsumedWithoutCancellingARunningBuff`. |
| **PROVISIONAL-STORY-ABILITY-DAMAGE-ROUTING** — the composition's agent-chosen rules | The design says only that the buff boosts "character Damage". It does not say which delivery paths count, when the buff is sampled, or what a Story buff orb grants. | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* (a) the buff applies to every hit the hero's kit deals (Specials, Ultimates, movement-ability hits, construct bites, arcs and bolts, zone ticks, Tesla's coil detonation), as it already did to basics and throws; the Resonant Overdrive coil's direct Extractor arc (`TakeEnvironmentDamage`) keeps its unscaled value. (b) It is sampled when each hit lands (a Hitbox at contact, a kit hit at resolution), so a buff picked up mid-cast counts for that cast's later hits and one that expires mid-cast stops counting; Lincoln's Emancipator now resolves its damage per contact instead of once at cast. (c) Each ability's `AbilityDamage` lane now applies to every hit the ability authors, keyed by its ability ID, as `Hitbox.CreatePayload` already did. As a result, Mozart's Minor Chord Damage ("The Requiem Chord deals +10%") now also scales the 3 × 4 burst pulses; before, only the 4-damage contact took it. Joan's and Lincoln's lanes are unchanged. (d) The SpecialDamage lane has no grid node and carries only the Level 13 Mirror clone's F20 outgoing campaign multiplier (0.5 / 1.0 / 1.5). It now also reaches the pooled-projectile contacts (E=mc², Requiem Chord, Yorick), Tesla's coil arc, fence and detonation, and Leonardo's turret bolts, which ignored it. So on Easy and Hard the clone's projectile contacts and constructs now scale with F20; Normal is unchanged. (e) Mozart's Fortissimo rounds its authored base before the multipliers rather than base × SpecialDamage. That is identical for its whole-number base at every SpecialDamage value in use. (f) A speed or damage orb grants the drop profile's buff (Easy ×1.5 for 15 s, Normal ×1.25 for 10 s) and ignores its own `Value` and `Duration`. On Hard, which authors no buff, it is consumed with no effect and never overwrites a running buff. | Unassigned | A human playtest on Easy and Normal confirms the buff reads on Specials and Ultimates and is not out of proportion. A user ruling on (a) constructs and zones, (c) the Requiem burst, (d) the Mirror's projectiles and constructs and (f) the orb values ratifies or replaces each. Until then none is a design number. |

## Confirmed open deviations — combat flow (hit-confirm, per-hit Momentum, not-yet flash), 2026-10-11

User-directed combat-flow rules (`DECISIONS.md` D1, D1a, D2 and the 2026-10-11 addendum), built in both modes on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`) by spec A's agent A1, and the D10 "not yet" flash (`USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`) by its agent A2 the same day. The rules live once in `FTT.Combat.BasicComboRules` (`SpecialGateFrames`, `IsSpecialLocked`, `MomentumRefundFramesFor`, `MomentumRefundFramesForHitbox`, `MomentumGrant`, the 20 / 20 / 60 / 120 constants). Story keeps S, C and the freeze buffer on `PlayerController`; the sim packs them into one int of component 305 (the unread `AegisHits` slot, reused — 305 stays exactly 128 B, no component ID allocated, no protocol or save change). Every Fighter hash moves (per-hit Momentum, CPU Specials mid-string); no suite pins an absolute hash. Suites: `AbilityLockoutRulesTests` (+2), `FighterHitConfirmTests` (18), `StoryHitConfirmTests` (15); for the flash, `FighterLockoutRefusalRulesTests` (4), `StoryLockoutRefusalEventTests` (2) and one case each in `StoryHudPresentationTests`, `HudAbilityIndicatorModelTests` and `FighterHudSceneTests`. Nobody has played it, and nobody has looked at the flash.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL** — a landed basic swing cancels into Special 1/2 (D1, D1a) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D1):** "A basic swing that **lands** a hit may cancel straight into **Special 1 or Special 2**. Whiffs still lock. … A Special accepted within the last 30 frames keeps blocking Specials, whatever lands afterwards. … No hit-confirm for the Ultimate, in either mode." **D1a:** a hit counts when "damage was applied, or it was absorbed by a shield or barrier layer"; it does not count when blocked by the block stance, refused by invulnerability, the arena ward, Time Freeze or the world hold, whiffed, or struck on a construct, prop or environment strike surface. **Orchestrator ruling on spec B §0.2:** spec A's mechanism is the single D1a predicate, and a payload with zero authored damage never counts as landed. Supersedes part of `USER-ABILITY-LOCKOUT`, and amends `design-godot.md` :782, :816, :1499, :3912 and the Cooldown System block from :1106 again. | Specials are refused while `BasicComboRules.SpecialGateFrames(A, S, C) = C ? S : A` is positive; the Ultimate reads A alone. **S** (Special-sourced lockout) is armed to 30 with A by every Special 1/2 and Ultimate acceptance and ticks with A (paused by the fighter's hitstop, and by Story Time Freeze); **C** is cleared by every swing start and set by the swing's first landed contact. **D1a, Story:** the receiver reports — `Hurtbox.ReportLanded` / `DidLand`, read back by `Hitbox.OnAreaEntered` into `HitPayload.ContactLanded` — from `EnemyController` (alive, not `DrillInvulnerable`, rounded payload damage > 0; a shield or armor taking it to 0 still lands), `BossController` (damage applied, after the ward and every invulnerability window), `PlayerController.ResolveIncomingHit` (damage applied, or a full Aegis / shield absorption on a body that is not post-rewind, tech or knockdown invulnerable) and `TrainingDummy` (alive, damage > 0); strike surfaces, props and constructs never report, and Time Freeze / the world hold / the M1 wall discard the contact before any receiver. **Sim:** `FighterDamageRules.ResolveFighterHit` returns `Refused` / `Blocked` / `Absorbed` / `Landed` (a partial barrier absorb followed by a block is `Blocked`; `ApplyFighterHit` is `== Landed`, hash-neutral for its callers); `Landed` or `Absorbed` with damage > 0 sets C. A Special's own hit never sets C, and a throw is not a swing. Measured first legal confirm after the template opener: sim T+s+F = T+10, Story T+s+2+F = T+12 (both previously T+30+F = T+34); the Ultimate is unchanged at T+30+F; a Special followed by a landed swing reopens Specials on the 30th unfrozen tick after the Special (previously after the swing). **Fighter consequence:** in PvP, hit 1 → any reaching Special is a guaranteed confirm from the last freeze tick (hit 1's hitstun is never block-cancelable and Specials resolve on the press tick, `DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`); D1 is ruled for both modes and no escape or proration was added. | User (design amendment) | The GDD master (`design-godot-v7.md`) states the D1 cancel and the D1a definition at the cited lines and is re-synced to the mirror; a hands-on session in both modes; a Fighter session judges the hit-1 confirm against the No Juggling pillar (F09). |

Cap: 120 frames refunded per slot per cooldown cycle. Once per swing, not once per target." **Addendum ruling (2026-10-11):** "D2 applies to every landed basic hit on any enemy, boss included." Replaces the V7.1 finisher-only refund (60 frames, at most twice per cycle); supersedes `design-godot.md` :1108, :1352 and the snapshot struct :1766–1767 (`MomentumRefundsS1/S2` now count frames). | `BasicComboRules.MomentumGrant(nominal, refundedThisCycle, cooldownRemaining) = max(0, min(nominal, 120 − refunded, remaining))` on each slot, paid on the C 0 → 1 edge (so once per swing however many bodies a Story swing cleaves, and for an absorbed landing exactly as for a damaging one), bosses included. Counters: sim `FighterVerbComponent.MomentumRefundedFramesSlotOne/Two` (component 310, renamed in place from `MomentumRefundsSlot*`, same layout, now frames), Story `PlayerController._momentumRefundedFramesSlot*`; reset when that slot's cooldown is armed, zeroed by the F22 Sudden Death reset, kept through death, a death rewind, a checkpoint restore and a stock loss. Story refunds timer-seconds and sets a timer the grant fully covers to exactly 0. | **PROVISIONAL-HIT-CONFIRM-RULES** — the details D1/D2 left open | D1/D1a/D2 give the gate, the landed definition and the refunds only. | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* (1) S is armed by Special 1/2 **and an accepted Ultimate**. (2) The landed table above: absorbed = Aegis / Story shield / sim barrier / a mob's frontal or bubble reduction; a block taking any part wins; refusals are Foresight, projectile immunity, the Defy window, tech / knockdown / post-rewind invulnerability (including a Story full absorption during them, `PlayerController.IsConfirmInvulnerable`), the boss ward, transition, T01b, guarded and soliloquy; the `TrainingDummy` counts; a zero-damage payload never lands (orchestrator ruling). (3) Timing: S pauses in hitstop like A, so the landing's freeze delays the first confirm and a Special → landed swing → Special chain reopens on the 30th unfrozen tick; presses inside the freeze are held by the freeze-only buffer (`PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`) rather than dropped. (4) A meter-paid Ultimate held back only by the lockout claims the tick from Special 1/2 (never from the movement ability): Story `_ultimateHeldBackThisTick`, set in `CheckUltimateInput` (every Story site polls it before `CheckSpecialInput`); sim `FighterCombatSystem.UltimatePressHeldBackByLockout`, read by the entity system's four Special branches and `BuildIntent`. (5) Storage: the reused `AegisHits` int of component 305 (an unread presentation mirror since D02e moved Aegis to component 312), packed as S (bits 0–15), C (16), freeze-buffer armed (17) and held presses (18–19); the four dead `AegisHits` writes are gone; the fallback, if the reuse is rejected, is growing component 320 by two ints. (7) The CPU reads the Special gate at delivery for its S1/S2 rungs and the mobility Special (new `CpuDecisionObservation.SpecialLockoutFrames` / `SwingLanded`, copied from 305; the Mirror adapter copies them from the clone); the Ultimate wait is unchanged, so a full-meter Hard CPU never hit-confirms against a grounded target in hitstun (it waits for its Ultimate); no hit-confirm preference tuning and no delivery-time revalidation. (8) Momentum: the grant counts frames removed; Story refunds timer-seconds and zeroes a timer the grant fully covers; counters are not persisted (a reload mid-cycle reopens the cap); an absorbed landing pays like a damaging one. (10) Test seams `FighterSimulation.SeedFighterInvulnerabilityForTest` and `SeedFighterSpecialCooldownsForTest` (both internal, both move the hash). (11) **Orchestrator default (`DECISIONS.md` addendum A):** hit 1 → Special confirms in Fighter PvP are accepted as D1 rules them ("both modes"); no escape, proration or hitstun change was added for them (the Fighter consequence in `USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL`), and a Fighter session judges them against F09. Also: the playtest telemetry's group-A seams (`PlaytestWorld`: `HasHitConfirmGate`, `SpecialGateFrames`, `IsLandedContact` = `ContactLanded`, `ConnectBasis` "d1a", `SwingLanded`) now read the A1 seams, so the `play` / `spam` bots gate Specials on the D1 gate; the refusal-event half (`HasLockoutRefusedEvent`, `SubscribeLockoutRefused`) waited for A2 and now counts the hero's `EventBus.OnAbilityLockoutRefused` events (`lockout_events` in the per-slot refusal stats). **Appended by agent A2 (2026-10-11):** (6) The "not yet" flash (`USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`): a 0.25 s `UIPalette.BossRed` `SelfModulate` tint back to white plus a hollow 2 px frame that appears at 1.35× and contracts to 1× as it fades (`FTT.UI.NotYetFlash`; the frame is the non-colour channel, and `Modulate` is left to the readiness writers, so a same-tick ready flash and a refusal flash never fight). It fires only when the lockout is the sole refusal: the slot unlocked and off cooldown (the Ultimate: the meter full), in a state that reads the press, and no Special or Ultimate accepted that tick. Under §1.7 the Ultimate alone flashes; Special 2 flashes beside a movement-ability acceptance (the chord at a non-full meter), because the movement ability is outside the lockout family. A press inside a landed swing's freeze is held and never flashes; if the gate refuses it on the thaw tick it flashes there (the addendum: "presses refused by the lockout are still dropped and get the D10 flash"). The Fighter classifier (`FighterLockoutRefusalRules.Evaluate`) reads the post-tick `PressedButtons`, which every input-discard path zeroes, with read-time cooldowns (one frame lower on an unfrozen tick, untouched on the last freeze tick, which the systems read) and the pre-tick meter, and flashes nothing for a fighter that is captured, knocked out or on the respawn platform after the tick. None in LAN. Known misses, never false flashes: a press on the tick the fighter is hit or its own swing lands with damage, on the tick an absorbed landing sets C, on the tick the meter fills inside `TickCounters`, and a sim Ultimate pressed from the block stance (`VERIFY-SAME-TICK-VERB-PARITY` (1)). Known differences: a same-tick BasicAttack + Special from neutral starts the swing in both modes, but only the sim flashes the Special — the A2 review classed this as a missing Story report site (a gap to close), not an intended mode difference; left open (the sim reads it after the swing armed the lockout; Story polls the swing first and never reads the Special); and a sim melee-intent Special pressed under the lockout while its target is out of reach flashes, although with the gate open its range gate would refuse it too (`DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`; Story casts at any range). The duration, scale, colour and border are provisional. (9) The Story HUD's three cooldown radials are written from the hero's own timers every frame (`StoryHUD.SyncCooldownAuthority` → `HudAbilityIndicatorModel.SyncCooldownRemaining`, after the fallback tick, so the arc is the hero's value that frame, not one frame ahead), keeping the denominator the cast armed and raising it only if the authority exceeds it (a cooldown restored on load raises no start event). A per-hit Momentum refund, a Time Freeze pause and `StoryCooldownMultiplier` now reach the radial; with no hero in the tree the old local countdown remains the fallback. | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a playtest that leaves them alone. |

A Special 1/2 press during the hitstop freeze of a *landed* hit fires on the first tick after the freeze ends, provided it is legal then. This is not a general lockout buffer. Presses refused by the lockout are still dropped and get the D10 flash. The Ultimate is not buffered. This amends D10's 'No input buffer' for exactly this case." It answers spec A §7 item 1. Pinned by `FighterHitConfirmTests` and `StoryHitConfirmTests` (two cases each). Bot evidence (`absolum_research/flow/bots_after.md`): under the default press policy the `play` and `spam` bots' Special refusals labelled `hitstop` rose from 7 to 84 and from 5 to 70 per 24 Normal runs; the D6d telemetry resolves a press on the next tick, so it books a press the buffer fires after the freeze as refused. | User (design amendment) | The GDD master states the buffer beside the D1 cancel and is re-synced; `HUD_CONTRACT.md`'s D10 paragraph (`USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`) names it; a hands-on session in both modes judges the timing. |
| **PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER** — a Special pressed in a landed hit's freeze fires after it | **User ruling 2026-10-11 (addendum):** "Freeze-only Special buffer: YES, both modes. A Special 1/2 press during the hitstop freeze of a *landed* hit fires on the first tick after the freeze ends, provided it is legal then. This is not a general lockout buffer. Presses refused by the lockout are still dropped … The Ultimate is not buffered." Amends D10's "No input buffer" for exactly this case. | *Provisional, agent-chosen details:* **Which freeze:** only the attacker freeze of the fighter's own **landed basic swing** (D1a landed, with damage > 0, so the swing froze its attacker) arms the buffer; a victim freeze, a blocked or shatter freeze, a strike surface's freeze, a landing that does not freeze its attacker (an absorbed one; a lethal one in the sim, whose KO skips the freeze — Story's kill freeze does arm it) and a Special's own caster freeze buffer nothing (the S lockout would refuse those at the thaw in all but a slow projectile's late landing). **What is held:** one deep — the Special 1/2 bits of the latest frozen tick that pressed either (both if pressed on one tick; the ordinary same-tick order then casts Special 1). A frozen tick that also presses the **Ultimate** holds nothing new and leaves an earlier held press in place, so a same-tick LB+RB chord's Special 2 is never held. **Edge, recorded at the finalize step (the A1 review), unchanged:** a chord whose RB half (Special 2) arrives on an earlier frozen tick than its LB half holds that Special 2 on the earlier tick, and the chord's Ultimate press is not buffered, so the thaw casts Special 2 alone if its gate allows — the same split-chord outcome as outside a freeze (`VERIFY-GAMEPAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD` (a)). **When it fires:** Story re-presents the held press on its first unfrozen tick (`PlayerController.ReleaseFreezeSpecialBuffer`, OR-ed into that tick's `CurrentInputFrame.Pressed` before any state reads it); the sim re-presents it on the fighter's thaw tick — the tick the movement system takes `HitstopFrames` 1 → 0, whose presses the ability and combat systems read — by OR-ing it into `FighterRuntimeComponent.PressedButtons` (`FighterAbilityLockoutRules.ReleaseFreezeBuffer`). There the ordinary gates decide it: a press the D1 gate, a cooldown, Suppression, Time Freeze, hitstun or the block stance refuses is dropped, never retried. The buffer is emptied at that thaw tick, by every swing start, and by a death rewind, a checkpoint restore, Sudden Death and a stock loss. **Sim storage:** bit 17 (armed) and bits 18–19 (held S1 / S2) of the packed hit-confirm int on component 305 (`FreezeBufferArmed`, `FreezeBufferedSpecials`) — snapshotted and hashed, no ID, no protocol change. Story keeps `_freezeBufferArmed` / `_freezeBufferedSpecials` (never persisted; `FreezeBufferedSpecials` is a test seam). Pinned by `FighterHitConfirmTests` (2 cases, plus the corrected-press convergence case, whose boundary is now the landing tick against the freeze's first tick) and `StoryHitConfirmTests` (2 cases). | Unassigned | A hands-on session in both modes judges the timing (a freeze-time press against a release-tick press) and the chord rule; a ruling on whether a Special's own landing freeze should buffer too, and on the split-chord edge (clear a held press on a tick that presses the Ultimate, or keep it). |

No input buffer." **Addendum (2026-10-11):** the freeze-only Special buffer amends "No input buffer" for exactly that case; "Presses refused by the lockout are still dropped and get the D10 flash." **Owed amendments:** `docs-3/docs/HUD_CONTRACT.md` and its repo copy `docs/design-contracts/HUD_CONTRACT.md` gain a "Lockout refusal (D10)" paragraph in the M25 section and a Story equivalent, both naming the shape channel (the contracting frame) beside the tint, per the contract's "colour alone" rule; `design-godot.md` :3514 (HUD Micro-Behaviours) and the sprite-table rows :4916 (Ultimate meter ring), :4918 (Story cooldown icons) and :4919 (Fighter cooldown icons) each gain a "not-yet flash" state. | **Story:** `PlayerController` raises `EventBus.OnAbilityLockoutRefused` (`AbilityLockoutRefusedPayload { PlayerIndex, Slot }`) at the exact refusal site: `CheckSpecialInput`'s D1 gate (one event per pressed Special that is Legacy-unlocked, has its slot node and a timer at or below 0), `CheckUltimateInput`'s held-back point (meter full and unlocked: the Ultimate alone, §1.7), and `ProcessAttacking` on a tick a chain swing starts (the swing wins and re-arms the lockout, as in the sim; §1.7 first). Time Freeze, Suppression, a Dormant slot, a cooldown and an unpaid meter refuse first and raise nothing. `StoryHUD` flashes player 0's `Cooldown1_Icon` / `Cooldown2_Icon`, or the meter ring (the frame on the plain `UltimateMeter_BG`, the tint on the drawn `UltimateMeter_Fill`); the Level 13 Mirror clone raises the event as index 1 and the HUD ignores it. **Fighter:** `FighterSimulationDriver` captures a `LockoutRefusalBaseline` per fighter (hitstop, both Special cooldowns, meter, Ultimate busy) before each local `Simulation.Advance` and classifies the tick after it with the pure, engine-free `FighterLockoutRefusalRules.Evaluate` (presentation only, never called by an ISystem, ints and `FP64` only); `FighterHUD.FlashLockoutRefusal` then flashes that player's `S1_Cooldown` / `S2_Cooldown` label or `MeterBar`. The look lives once in `FTT.UI.NotYetFlash` (`PROVISIONAL-HIT-CONFIRM-RULES` (6)). | **VERIFY-STORY-INVULN-LAYER-ORDER** — Story spends a shield on a hit its body was invulnerable to | `DEFENSIVE_EFFECTS.md` D01: "hit eligibility / invulnerability → Defy → projectile immunity → Aegis → barrier → block → HP". | Story's `PlayerController.ResolveIncomingHit` checks only roll invulnerability, Defy and Foresight before `ResolveProtectionLayers`; post-rewind, landing-tech and knockdown invulnerability are checked later, inside `ApplyDamage`. So a Story Aegis or shield is spent on a hit the body was invulnerable to, while the sim refuses first (`FighterDamageRules.ResolveFighterHit`'s invulnerability gate). ## Confirmed open deviations — combat flow (Story Special flow: the first Special untaxed, the Punish window, the on-hit Special recovery cancel), 2026-10-11

User-directed Story combat-flow rules from `DECISIONS.md` D4 and the 2026-10-11 addendum ("No count rule for poise"), built on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`) by spec B's sub-step B1. Spec B's later sub-steps (D8, the Punish window; D3, the on-hit Special recovery cancel) append their rows here. Every number is one constant in `FTT.Combat.StoryEnemyCombatRules` (`ChainSpecialRole`, `ClassifyChainSpecial`, `ChainHitScale`, `StandardBudgetCost(…, taxedSpecial)`, `StandardStaggerBudgetSeconds`, `StandardStaggerDecayPerSecond`, `FirstSpecialStunAllowanceFrames`); the behaviour lives in `EnemyController.ResolveIntakeScale` and its private `ApplyStun`. Story only: nothing in `scripts/FighterSim/` reads any of it, and no Klotho component, snapshot, hash, protocol, save field or `.tres` value changed. Suites: `StoryFirstSpecialTaxTests` (8), `StoryEnemyCombatRulesTests` (+1, 2 rewritten), `StoryRiftCollapseTests` (+1), `StoryEnemyCombatTests` (2 rewritten). Nobody has played it.

Spec B's sub-step B2 (2026-10-11, the same working tree) added D8, the Punish window: the two rows after D4's below. Its numbers are constants in the same rulebook (`PunishWindowFrames`, `PunishDamageScale`, `PunishWhiffNearMarginPixels`, `PunishWhiffNearVerticalPixels`, `PunishDamageNumberScale`, `IsPunishEligible`, `PunishWhiffIsNear`), plus the one shared mob melee-reach helper `StoryEnemyCombatRules.MeleePivotReachPixels` and the named Story hero hurtbox `StoryCombatRules.HeroHurtboxWidthPixels` / `HeroHurtboxHeightPixels` (40 x 64, now read by `CharacterFactory.BuildHurtbox`, no value change), which spec D's D6c reuses. The behaviour lives in `EnemyController` (the window, `ResolveIntakeScale`, `PresentDealtHit`, and the whiff rule on the executor's new `ActiveWindowEnded` event), `PlayerController.ResolveIncomingHit` (`ReportEnemyAttack`) and `EnemyAbilityExecutor` (the blocked Siphon Snare, the shooter's ID on its shots). Story only: nothing in `scripts/FighterSim/` reads any of it, and no Klotho component, snapshot, hash, protocol, save field, `.tres` value or translation key changed. Suites: `StoryPunishWindowTests` (8), `StoryEnemyCombatRulesTests` (+2). Nobody has played it.

Spec B's sub-step B3 (2026-10-11, the same working tree) added D3, the on-hit Special recovery cancel: the last two rows below. It reads the D1a predicate spec A's agent A1 had already built (`HitPayload.ContactLanded`, `Hurtbox.ReportLanded` / `DidLand`; the four receivers carry the orchestrator's 0-damage amendment, verified, so no D1a row of its own). The numbers are three constants and three predicates in `FTT.Combat.StoryCombatRules` (`SpecialRecoveryEvadeCancelFrame` 0, `SpecialRecoveryActionCancelFrame` 6, `SpecialCancelBufferFrames` 6; `SpecialRecoveryAllowsEvade`, `SpecialRecoveryAllowsAction`, `SpecialCancelBufferHolds`). The latch and the recovery frame index live on `BaseSpecial` (`LandedDirectHitThisExecution`, `RecoveryFramesElapsed`, set by `ConfirmAbilityHit`'s new first statement); kit shape-query hits are stamped by `BaseSpecial.Credit(Hurtbox struck, ...)`, whose hurtbox-less overload is deleted (all 25 kit call sites pass the struck hurtbox); `Hitbox.OnAreaEntered` now calls `ConfirmAbilityHitboxHit` for every contact, after the D1a stamp, so a fully absorbed (0-damage) ability contact reaches the latch (the feedback inside stays damage-gated). The cancel and its one-slot buffer live in `PlayerController.ProcessUsingSpecial` (`BufferSpecialCancelPress`, `TryFireSpecialCancel`, `TryCancelIntoJump`, and the pure extractions `TryStartRollOrAirDodge`, `StartBasicSwing`, `TryStartMovementAbilityCast`). `TubmanForesight.OnInterrupted` now ends the sidestep. Story only: nothing in `scripts/FighterSim/` reads any of it, and no Klotho component, snapshot, hash, protocol, save field, `.tres` value or translation key changed. Suites: `StorySpecialRecoveryCancelTests` (11), `StoryCombatRulesTests` (+1). Nobody has played it.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-FIRST-SPECIAL-UNTAXED** — the first Special in a stun chain is not taxed (D4) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D4):** "The first Special in a stun chain lands at **full damage**: no ×0.6 confirm proration, and no chain-decay tax on its hits; charges standard poise at **×1.0**. A second or later Special in the same chain keeps the ×0.6 proration and the ×1.5 poise cost. **The Smite → string route must work.** … Righteous Smite → hit 1 → hit 2 → hit 3 must not trip standard poise. Re-derive the S3 budget so that one full string plus the largest authored first-Special stun fits, and so that a second Special still trips it." **Addendum (2026-10-11):** "No 'count rule' for poise. A second Special trips standard poise only through D4's stun arithmetic, not automatically." Amends `PROVISIONAL-STORY-CONFIRM-PRORATION` (S2) and `PROVISIONAL-STANDARD-POISE` (S3); new design content (the GDD has no PvE proration or standard poise). | The intake classifies each hit against the mob's stun chain (`StoryEnemyCombatRules.ClassifyChainSpecial`): the chain's first Special-class, non-Ultimate hit records its `AttackID` (`EnemyController.ChainFirstSpecialID`), and every hit with that ID is `FirstSpecial` — scaled ×1.0 at any chain index (`ChainHitScale`) and charging standard poise ×1.0 (`StandardBudgetCost(stun, taxedSpecial: false)`). Any other Special is `OtherSpecial`: the pre-D4 ×0.6 confirm cut times the chain decay, and ×1.5 poise. V7.4's diminishing special stun is unchanged (it still halves the stun of a Special-class hit inside 4 s of another, the first Special's later hits included); elites keep V7.4 poise; bosses were never prorated or poised. Standard budget **2.325 s** (was 1.9), drain **4.0 s/s** (was 3). Measured on a resistance-0 standard: Smite → hit 1 → hit 2 → hit 3 charges 132 frames and fits (it tripped at hit 2, 123 > 114); a second Special after it charges ≥ 9 frames and trips (141 > 139.5); a first Special fired into a string at chain hit 4 lands in full (an 8-damage hit: 8, was 4). | User (design amendment) | A bot or hand check of the Smite route and of "string + both Specials" on Normal; the design master gains the rule beside Enemy Stagger Discipline. |
| **PROVISIONAL-FIRST-SPECIAL-TAX-RULES** — the details D4 left open | D4 names the first Special, its full damage and ×1.0 poise, the ×0.6 / ×1.5 for later Specials, and the Smite route; nothing else. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (spec B §1.3, the orchestrator's defaults for its §7):* (1) **Identity by `AttackID`.** The first Special-class, non-Ultimate hit on a chain claims the slot; every hit of that Special shares the role (Divine Piercing's thrusts, E=mc²'s contact and burst, Requiem's pulses, Lorentz and its arcs). A Special-class **tick** is classified too (it stays proration-exempt: no scale, no chain step), so the Sandstorm Vortex's launching tick can be the first Special and charges ×1.0; a tick's claim on a mob it does not stun is cleared by the next classified hit. **The Rift collapse joins its E=mc² execution** (design E01: "a 0-damage, Special-class hit of the same E=mc² execution"): `EinsteinRelativityRift.TryCollapse(burstPoint, sourceAttackID)` carries E=mc²'s ability ID into the collapse payload, so the E=mc² + collapse route charges 12 + 6 + 15 = 33 frames, not 40.5. (2) **The first Special's hits are not scaled but do advance the chain counter** (the minimal reading; orchestrator default "the first Special's hits still count toward the chain's free-hit count"), so Smite → hit 1 → hit 2 → hit 3 lands its finisher at ×0.85 exactly as before D4. (3) **Movement-ability hits never claim the slot** (`HitPayload.SourceIsMovementAbility`, stamped by `BaseSpecial.WithAbilityContract` and `Hitbox.ApplyAbilityHitContract` from `AbilityData.Slot`; movement abilities author `Origin = Special`) and keep the pre-D4 S2/S3 treatment, as does a Special with no `AttackID`. (4) **The budget:** 2.325 s = 139.5 frames = the S5 string's 102 frames + `FirstSpecialStunAllowanceFrames` 36 (Requiem Chord with the Requiem Crescendo perk, 12 + 4 × 6, the worst-case total first-Special charge with kit and perk extras: E=mc² + collapse 33, Splitting Strike + Kinetic Splitting's 20-frame bounce 32, Smite 30, Requiem 30) + a 1.5-frame margin; the same budget trips the ruled route plus any second Special (132 + 9 = 141 frames, the smallest taxed second charge being one 12-frame hit × 0.5 × 1.5) with a 1.5-frame margin. Drain 4.0 s/s, so the 0.6 s getup armor (2.4 s) clears a full budget and each chain that did not trip starts the next empty. `StoryFirstSpecialTaxTests.NoFirstSpecialChargesMoreStandardPoiseThanTheAllowance` recomputes every authored Special from a per-kit hit model reading only authored data and named kit constants (`LeonardoGoldenRatio.ChurnTickHitstunFrames`, `TeslaLorentzPulse.ChainArcHitstunFrames`, `EinsteinRelativityRift.CollapseLaunchHitstunFrames`, `LincolnSplittingStrike.GroundBounceWindowFrames`, all named in this change with no value change). (5) **Residuals, no count rule** (addendum): a string plus both Specials trips only where first + second > 37.5 frames — always for Joan and Mozart, for Lincoln only with the Kinetic Splitting bounce (Emancipator → string → Splitting Strike charges 123 frames), never for Tubman (123 frames); Einstein, Leonardo, Cleopatra, Tesla and Shakespeare have one stunning Special each (the other is a zone, a construct or the Tempest's windbox, which never trips); `shock_shield_legionnaire` (resistance 0.15) does not trip on the ruled route (135.15 frames). **String → Special → string trips** at the restarted hit 1 (first Special > 27.5 frames) or hit 2 (any) — orchestrator default "poise trips on a restarted chain are accepted", so a chain allows one string plus one Special on a standard. (6) **A recast of the same Special inside one chain matches the recorded first Special by `AttackID`** and lands untaxed too; the orchestrator default "Recasting the same Special does not reset 'first'" is read as "the chain's first-Special record is not reset or re-keyed by a recast" (execution keying would need a serial on every Special payload, child hitboxes included; rejected). Practically elite-only: the cooldown must come back inside one chain. | Unassigned | A ruling on each (especially (5) and (6)), or a bot before/after and a playtest that leave them alone. |
| **USER-PUNISH-WINDOW** — a blocked, countered or whiffed mob attack opens a ×1.2 window on that mob (D8) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D8):** "**Triggers** (the window opens on that enemy): a non-shatter block of that enemy's attack; a Foresight counter; an enemy whiff, meaning its melee attack's active window ended without hitting the hero while the hero was near. **Effect:** the hero's hits on that enemy deal **×1.2 for 60 frames**, shown with tinted damage numbers. **Scope:** mobs and elites, not bosses, because `VERIFY-BOSS-HP` is held." Orchestrator default (DECISIONS addendum): "The Punish window runs on world frames." New design content: the GDD has no PvE punish. | Every `EnemyController` (standard and elite tiers, summons included) carries a window (`IsPunishWindowOpen`, `PunishFramesRemaining`, `OpenPunishWindow()`) set to `StoryEnemyCombatRules.PunishWindowFrames` (60) by: the hero's chokepoint `PlayerController.ResolveIncomingHit` (`ReportEnemyAttack`) on a `BlockResult.Blocked` of a payload whose `SourceActorId` names that mob, and on a successful `TubmanForesight.TryCounter` of it; `EnemyAbilityExecutor` on a non-shatter block of the Eraser's Siphon Snare; and the mob's own whiff rule (`EnemyAbilityExecutor.ActiveWindowEnded`; `PROVISIONAL-PUNISH-WINDOW-RULES` (5)). The mob is named on its payloads through the new `Hitbox.SourceActorIdOverride` (stamped on the mob's attack hitbox by `ApplyScaledHitboxDamage`) and `EnemyProjectile.SourceActorId` (stamped by the executor after every `Setup`, pool-reset to 0). An eligible hit (`IsPunishEligible`: player-dealt, `DirectHit`, not a hazard: basics, directional strikes, Specials and Ultimates) on an open window multiplies `PunishDamageScale` (1.2) into `ResolveIntakeScale` after S2/D4 and S4, with the one `ScaleIntakeDamage` rounding, and its floating number is drawn in `UIPalette.GoldBright` at `PunishDamageNumberScale` (1.25×; `FloatingDamageNumber.Show(..., tint, scale)`). Measured in `StoryPunishWindowTests`: Joan's 6-damage hit 1 on a punished standard deals 9 (8 unpunished); Tubman's 20-damage Foresight answer on the countered mob deals 24. Bosses (`BossController`) and the Level 13 Mirror clone have no window, and a blocked boss payload changes nothing. | User (design amendment) | The design master amended (Section 4, beside Enemy Stagger Discipline) and re-synced; a hands-on session confirms the window reads and pays off. |
| **PROVISIONAL-PUNISH-WINDOW-RULES** — the details D8 left open | D8 names the three triggers, ×1.2, 60 frames, tinted numbers, and mobs and elites only; nothing else. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (spec B §1.4 and the orchestrator's defaults for its §7):* (1) **World clock:** 60 of the mob's own unfrozen ticks, decremented at the **end** of `EnemyController._PhysicsProcess` (the old body is now `ProcessMobTick`), after every trigger that tick could fire, so a window opened by a block, a counter or a whiff during tick T is open for every hit resolved through tick T + 59. It pauses only while the mob's body is frozen (`_rewindFrozen`: Time Freeze, the rewind freeze, the Post-Landing Hold) and keeps running through the mob's victim hitstop, a grab hold and a thrown flight, so a punish string's own freezes spend it (roughly 20 of the 60 frames on a three-hit string). A re-trigger refreshes to 60 and never stacks. It clears on death, pool spawn and despawn, and a rewind restore, and is never persisted. (2) **Eligible hits:** player-dealt `DirectHit` payloads that are not hazards; ticks, construct hits and hazards never punish, and Story throws and bowling never reach the intake path (`TakeDamage(int)`). (3) **Composition:** ×1.2 multiplies into the intake scale after S2/D4 and S4, with one rounding (half away from zero, at least 1). (4) **Block** means `BlockResult.Blocked` only: projectiles, Guard-Crush and a blocked Siphon Snare count; a shatter (`GuardBroken`), a full protection-layer absorption and every refusal open nothing. (5) **Whiff:** a MeleeStrike or ChargeDash active window that ends naturally (`ActiveWindowEnded`, raised first in `EnterRecovery`; never on a telegraph interruption or a hard stop), with no contact of that ability (matched by the active ability's `AttackID`) having reached the hero (HP damage, a full absorption by a body that was not invulnerable to it, a block of either result, or a counter), the swing not swallowed whole by a wall (`LastHitboxWallBlocked`), and the mob's target near at that moment: within `MeleePivotReachPixels(HitboxOffset.X, HitboxSize.X, 0, 20)` + `PunishWhiffNearMarginPixels` (40) horizontally and `PunishWhiffNearVerticalPixels` (96) vertically, feet to feet, using the authored box and no dash travel (positions are read where the swing ended; the legacy scalar melee is near within 110 px). Roll, air-dodge, tech and post-rewind invulnerability, Defy protection, a Foresight sidestep and projectile immunity never report a reach, so **rolling through a swing opens the window**; a swing that strikes a decoy or a construct instead of the hero is a whiff too. AreaPulse, Projectile, Shockwave and the other archetypes never whiff. (6) **Presentation:** `UIPalette.GoldBright` plus a 1.25× number scale (the non-colour channel); every other player-dealt number stays white at 1×. No HUD element, `EventBus` payload or string. (7) Bosses and the Mirror clone are excluded. (8) **Pooled owners:** a pooled mob keeps its Godot instance ID across a recycle, so a shot fired before the recycle and blocked after it opens the new spawn's window (rare, accepted); a blocked shot from a distant shooter opens that shooter's window even when the hero cannot reach it within 60 frames (the ruling's "that enemy's attack"). (9) **The shared reach:** `StoryEnemyCombatRules.MeleePivotReachPixels(offsetX, width, dashTravel, targetHalfWidth)` = abs(offset) + width / 2 + travel + half-width, with the named hero hurtbox `StoryCombatRules.HeroHurtboxWidthPixels` (40), is the one statement of a mob swing's reach; spec D's D6c commit cap reads the same helper. | Unassigned | A ruling on each (especially (1), the world clock, and (5), the whiff's "near" and the roll-through), or a bot before/after and a playtest that leave them alone. |
| **USER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL** — a Special that landed a direct hit cancels its recovery (D3) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D3):** "Applies to a Special whose execution has **landed a direct hit** (the D1a definition; ticks, constructs and zones do not count). … Roll or jump: from recovery frame 0. Basic hit 1, or the movement ability: from recovery frame 6. … Whiffs keep their full recovery. No Echo Step out of a Special (A06). Special → Special is still gated by the lockout. … Fighter: Specials resolve on the press tick there (`DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`, not being closed now), so this is Story-only for now." **Addendum (user, 2026-10-11):** "Out of a landed Special's recovery (D3), every basic attack variant is allowed from recovery frame 6: Up-Attack, Down-Air, or hit 1, according to the held direction. A held direction never eats the press." **Orchestrator default:** the Mirror clone inherits D3. **Supersedes,** for Story: `design-godot.md` :784–785 (`UsingSpecial` leaves only when the ability completes) and :783 / :1500 (only Block cancels a Special's recovery). | A Special execution whose own direct hit LANDED (`BaseSpecial.LandedDirectHitThisExecution`: the payload's `ContactLanded` — the receiver's D1a report — on a `DirectHit` payload whose `SourceExecutionSerial` is 0 or this execution's) cancels in the Recovery branch of `ProcessUsingSpecial`, after the unchanged grounded Block cancel: a roll (the air dodge when airborne) or a jump from recovery frame 0; a basic attack — hit 1, the Up-Attack or the Down-Air by the direction held at the press, through the same `BasicComboRules.SelectAttackVariant` as Idle — or the movement ability from frame 6. Recovery frames count the cast clock (`RecoveryFramesElapsed`, 0 on the owner's first Recovery tick, paused by hitstop). The verb starts, then the cast is interrupted: its world objects, cooldown and lockout stand, and a basic swing arms the swing lockout as any swing does. A whiff keeps its whole recovery; neither a Special nor the Ultimate is polled there, and Echo Step never is. What arms it, per hero: Einstein E=mc² (not the Rift); Joan both; Leonardo Golden Ratio (not the turret); Lincoln both; Tesla Lorentz Pulse and its arcs (not the coil); Shakespeare Yorick (the Tempest builds no payload); Mozart both; Tubman both (Foresight through its lantern answer); **Cleopatra never** (the nest is a construct, the Vortex ticks). The Mirror clone is a `PlayerController` and inherits it; its Specials arm only on the hero's damage or a full absorption. **Fighter:** none until `DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES` closes; the sim must then adopt the same table. | User (design amendment) | The master amended (the FSM table :783–785 and :1500) and re-synced; a hands-on pad session confirms the feel. |
| **PROVISIONAL-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL-RULES** — the details D3 left open | D3 and the addendum give the verbs, the two frame gates, the landed definition and the exclusions only. | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* (1) "Landed" is spec A's D1a predicate (a 0-damage payload never lands), on a `DirectHit` payload only, never a late pooled shot from an earlier cast (the R12 serial); a shape-query hit is stamped from the struck hurtbox by `Credit(struck, ...)`. A hit that lands during recovery is seen on the controller's next tick; the latch is read only in Recovery, so a landed hit never shortens startup or active. (2) Recovery frames count the cast clock and pause in hitstop (the caster freeze of the landed hit included). (3) Same-tick order: Block (existing, grounded), then the buffered verb; the verb starts, then the cast is interrupted. (4) A one-slot buffer of 6 unfrozen controller ticks (`SpecialCancelBufferFrames`): fed on every `UsingSpecial` tick including hitstop ticks, aged only on unfrozen ones, the latest press wins (priority Roll, Jump, Attack, Movement within one tick), cleared on entering and leaving `UsingSpecial`; a verb whose gate is open but which cannot start yet (no air jump, the air dodge spent, Root, a cooldown) stays buffered until it ages out; a whiff never fires it. Roll or Jump pressed in the caster freeze or up to 6 ticks before frame 0 fires at frame 0; an attack or movement press at frames 0–5 fires at frame 6. **Feel risk:** a press made just before a landing can fire a cancel the player no longer wants; without the buffer, presses in the freeze or at frames 0–5 would be silently dropped. (5) **The basic attack's variant is read from the direction held at the press** (Up = the Up-Attack, airborne Down = the Down-Air, otherwise hit 1; grounded Down is the string, as from Idle) and from whether the hero is airborne when it fires; the grab chord (Block held, grounded) is not a basic attack and is neither buffered nor fired (a grounded held Block takes the Block cancel first anyway). (6) Chord guards: a tick whose Ultimate bit is pressed (the gamepad LB+RB chord, recognized from its two buttons whatever the meter) neither fires nor buffers the movement cancel and clears a buffered one, and a Roll on a tick whose Echo Step bit is held (the Block+Roll chord, or a direct bind) never rolls (A06). A same-tick LB+RB chord out of a Special's recovery therefore does nothing. **Corrected at the finalize step (the B3 review; behaviour unchanged):** the guard reads only the tick's Ultimate bit, so (a) a split chord whose LB half arrives a tick before RB is caught only while that LB press is still buffered — before recovery frame 6 it is buffered and then cleared on the RB tick, but at frame 6 or later the LB press fires the movement cancel on its own tick, before RB arrives; and (b) an LB press while Special 2's button (RB) is still held forms the chord and is never taken as a movement cancel, at any meter. (7) The jump cancel refills the jump budget when grounded (as Idle does), spends an air jump when airborne, refreshes the held-Jump read before the jump (so a held Jump rises like an Idle jump; measured equal to within 1 px) and arms no jump buffer. (8) The movement cancel refuses under Time Freeze, Root, the Legacy lock and its cooldown (its bypass included); under Suppression it plays the refusal null-tone once and drops the press. (9) Foresight: its counter enters Recovery itself and the lantern answer lands on the ability's tick, after the controller has read recovery frame 0, so its earliest cancel is frame 1 (a press at frame 0 is buffered); `TubmanForesight.OnInterrupted` now ends the post-counter sidestep, so a cancel out of it — and Package 12 W3's Block cancel, which used to keep the invulnerability by accident — ends the invulnerability. (10) The Mirror clone inherits D3 (orchestrator default). (11) Cleopatra (nest, Vortex) and the Tempest (no payload) never qualify. | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a playtest that leaves them alone. |

## Confirmed open deviations — combat flow (the Story meter scale), 2026-10-11

User-directed Story meter rule from `DECISIONS.md` D5 and the orchestrator's default C for its open question (a Hard Mirror clone keeps the Mozart lane it mirrors), built on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`) by spec C's package C2, on top of the meter-rulebook step's `StoryMeterGainFactor` seam (`FIX-MOZART-BUILD-RATE-UNREAD`). The scale is one Story-only constant, `FTT.Combat.StoryCombatRules.MeterGainScale`; nothing under `scripts/FighterSim/`, nor the shared `InfluenceMeterRules`, names it (source-scanned). No component, snapshot, hash, protocol, save-schema or localization change.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-STORY-METER-SCALE** — the Story Ultimate is harder to get: meter gain ×0.35 (D5) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D5):** "The Ultimate is harder to get: Story meter gain ×0.35. **Scope:** Story only. The scale applies to all meter **gain**: damage dealt, damage taken, and drained Rally echo. It multiplies with Mozart's build-rate node. **Unchanged:** flat grants and spends. **Fighter is unchanged.** The Level 13 Mirror clone is exempt, because it plays Fighter rules. **Accepted side effect:** Defy History and Echo Step come around less often." **What it supersedes, for Story only:** `design-godot.md` :1268 ("Each **1 HP** of damage dealt … increases the meter by **1.0 point**"), :1269 ("**0.25 points**" per HP taken, and the drained-echo accrual) and the build-rate reading of :2793 (Minor Resonance, now 0.35 × 1.1 on the campaign hero). :1267 (cap 100, start at 0) and every spend are untouched. The design master states 1.0 per HP, so this is a user-directed amendment. | **Built 2026-10-11** (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`). `StoryCombatRules.MeterGainScale = 0.35f` and `MeterGainFactor(storyScaled, lane) = (storyScaled ? MeterGainScale : 1) × max(0, lane)`; `PlayerController.AppliesStoryMeterGainScale` (never persisted), set true only by `CharacterFactory`'s campaign-hero branch (`applyStoryProgression && applyLegacyUnlockLocks`: the hub, Levels 0–15, rebuilt heroes, slotless dev and bot launches) and written false on the Level 13 Mirror clone; the existing `PlayerController.StoryMeterGainFactor` is now `StoryCombatRules.MeterGainFactor(AppliesStoryMeterGainScale, StoryUltimateBuildRateMultiplier)`, read by `UltimateMeter`'s two gain paths through `InfluenceMeterRules.GainFromDamageDealt` / `GainFromDamageTaken` and by `RallyUncreditedMeter`. The campaign hero earns 0.35 meter per HP dealt and 0.0875 per HP taken (echo included as it drains); with Mozart's node 0.385 / 0.09625. Flat grants (Shield of Orléans +5, the MeterBoost orb, the Level 0 lesson's direct set), the durable restore and every spend are unscaled; the cap stays 100. Fighter, the sim, `FighterMeterRules`, the CPU and every Fighter hash are untouched. **Measured** (same build, the new diagnostic `--story-meter-scale=off` against the default `on`; Levels 5, 7 and 11, Joan and Mozart; slotless bot runs, so no lane): Normal (`cd5off` → `cd5on`, `play`/`spam`/`basics`, 18 runs each): `play` median clean bar fill 12.0 → 34.6 s (2.9×), Ultimates 6.5 → 2.83 per run, Defy procs 0.67 → 0.17 per run, `gain/min` 223 → 106 (0.47×); `spam` bar fill 19.9 → 51.4 s (2.6×); `basics` 21.5 → 71.1 s (3.3×). Easy `play` (`cd5offE` → `cd5onE`, 6 runs each): bar fill 14.1 → 42.3 s (3.0×), Ultimates 4.83 → 2.0 per run, `gain/min` 217 → 110 (0.51×). `gain/min` does not fall to 0.35× because it is a rate of observed rises per tracked minute: `spam` and `basics` never spend the bar and sit at the cap for 66–78 % of their frames with the scale off (36–38 % with it on), so their `gain/min` is cap-limited and barely moved (63.9 → 63.9, 53.2 → 49.6); and the `play` bot, casting fewer Ultimates (whose damage earns nothing, D03h), deals more meter-earning damage. The exact per-HP rate is pinned by the unit suites, not by the bots. In the tree at measurement: groups A (D1/D1a/D2/D10 and the freeze buffer) and B (D3/D4/D8) and the C1/C3 telemetry; not groups D or E. Output kept in `absolum_research/flow/C2-story-meter-scale.batches.txt`. **After every combat-flow group** (2026-10-12, `absolum_research/flow/bots_after.md`, scale on, seeds 0–3 pooled, against the pre-A, pre-D5 `bots_before.md`): Normal `play` clean bar fill 14.6 → 38.2 s (2.6×), Ultimates 7.08 → 2.75 per run, Defy procs 0.54 → 0.46 per run; Easy `play` 14.0 → 39.5 s (2.8×), Ultimates 4.92 → 1.79 per run. The fill rises a little less than a pure ×0.35 would (2.86×) because D1, D2 and D4 raise the meter-earning damage. Suites: `StoryMeterGainScaleTests` (8), `InfluenceMeterSourceScanTests.TheStoryMeterScaleLivesInStoryCombatRulesAndNeverReachesTheSimOrTheSharedRulebook`, `MirrorParadoxTests.TheCloneIsExemptFromTheStoryMeterScaleAndKeepsItsMirroredLane`; in-place expectation changes in `StoryRallyTests`, `StoryEnvironmentalDamageTests`, `StoryDefenceOrderTests`, `PlaceholderZoneTests`, `StoryFsmM04TransitionTests`, `PuzzleEnvironmentToolkitTests`, `Level09ContentTests` and `StoryMeterGainFactorTests` (a campaign hero's gain now reads its own `StoryMeterGainFactor`; a fill through a gain became a direct set). | User (design amendment) | (1) The GDD master (`design-godot-v7.md`) amended at the lines cited and re-synced to the mirror (this file does not edit the mirror). (2) The measured row above recorded — done, 2026-10-11. (3) A hands-on session confirms the bar time reads right. |
| **PROVISIONAL-STORY-METER-SCALE-RULES** — the details D5 left open | D5 gives only the scale, the three gain sources, the Mozart multiplication, the Mirror exemption and the unchanged flat grants and spends. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (spec C §1.1, §2 and the orchestrator's default C):* (1) **The campaign hero** is any build on `CharacterFactory`'s `applyStoryProgression && applyLegacyUnlockLocks` branch: the hub, Level 0, Level 1, Levels 2–15, heroes rebuilt by an Anchor Snap, a Collapse resume or an ordinary load, and slotless dev or bot launches. No D5 scale: the Mirror clone on every tier, the Calibration Drills, the Holodeck, the Test Arena and Fighter proxies. (2) **One flat value on every difficulty** (difficulty already scales mob HP and enemy damage, which feed meter as HP removed). (3) **The Mozart lane** multiplies all three gains on the campaign hero (0.385 per HP dealt). A non-campaign build keeps its lane with no scale; only the Hard Mirror clone can carry one (F20 mirrors purchased perks), so a Hard clone of a Mozart save earns 1.1 per HP, as `StoryMeterGainFactorTests` and the new Mirror case pin (orchestrator default C: "the 0.35 scale applies only to the campaign hero"). (4) **The uncredited Rally record** (F10 attempt state, `StoryPlayerResourceTimers.RallyUncreditedMeter`) is stored in the hero's scaled units and settled unscaled on load; a record written before this change settles once at the old basis. These are development saves only, so no save derivation is declared. (5) **Flat sources stay flat:** Shield of Orléans +5, the MeterBoost pickup, the Level 0 lesson's direct set and the durable restore; spends are unchanged. (6) **No rounding and no HUD change:** the meter stays a float, and the `UltimateReady` cue, the gold ring and the Defy seal fire on the same thresholds, later. (7) **The Mirror CPU's read of the hero's meter** (`TargetInfluenceRaw`) is unchanged; only the Hard shield-orb heuristic reads it, and Level 13 spawns no orbs. (8) **The Eraser's Siphon Snare** is refused at a hero meter ≤ 0.0001 (`EnemyAbilityExecutor.IsSiphonEligible`) and its tether breaks on `MeterEmpty` (`SiphonTetherChannel`): at ×0.35 it is committed less often (never at a fresh level start, where the meter is 0) and breaks sooner, while each point it drains costs about 2.9× as much dealt damage to win back; its 10/s rate and 30-point cap are unchanged, and like the Extractor's −20 it weighs about 2.9× more against bar fill. (9) **Diagnostics:** the playtest runner's `--story-meter-scale=on\|off` (default on) clears the flag on every new hero instance when off, so a slotless bot runs at the pre-D5 rate for a same-build A/B; the report carries `story_meter_scale`, the combat summary `meter_scale`, `compare_runs.py`'s "meter and presses" table a `scale` column and the batch CSV a `meter_scale` column. (10) **Orchestrator defaults (`DECISIONS.md` addendum C):** the `play` bot's Ultimate policy is unchanged (it taps every other frame with a full bar), and no slotted chained-run bot option was built (bot runs stay slotless, so `meter.level_start` is 0 by construction). | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a playtest that leaves them alone. |

## Confirmed open deviations — combat flow (spacing and walls), 2026-10-11

User-directed Story spacing rules from `DECISIONS.md` D6 and D9, built from spec D (spacing and walls) in two sub-passes. **Sub-pass (i), D6b** (the Story aerial string reach) was built on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`), on top of the reach-geometry step's one grounded string table (`FIX-STRING-BOX-ONE-TABLE`) and its Story helper `PlayerController.ResolveGroundStringBoxPixels`. It is **Story-only**: nothing under `scripts/FighterSim/` was touched, and no component, snapshot field, save field, schema version, protocol or translation key changed; no Fighter hash moves. Suite: the new `StoryAerialStringReachTests` (2). It closes the reach-geometry step's `VERIFY-STORY-AERIAL-STRING-BOXES` (above). **Sub-pass (ii), D6a / D6c / D9** (the finisher carry, mob melee range, the wall bounce) was built on the same working tree on 2026-10-11, after group C's telemetry (C1, C3) and the `bots-before` batches, with the user's post-spec addendum applied: **elites also get the shorter finisher carry** and **thrown standard mobs wall-bounce like launched ones**. It is Story-only in the same sense (no file under `scripts/FighterSim/` changed; the one `BasicComboRules` addition, `WidestStringReachWidthPercent`, is a read-only int nothing in the sim reads; no component, snapshot, save, protocol, hash or translation key moved). Suites: `StoryEnemyCombatRulesTests` +3 (pure), the new `StoryFinisherCarryTests` (6) and `StoryWallBounceTests` (6), `EnemyControllerTests` +6 and `EnemyRosterContentTests` +1, so +22: the spec's +20 with the addendum's elite-carry and thrown-bounce cases as two more. Four `EnemyControllerTests` cases changed in place for D6c (no count change): the stand-off shuffle reads `StandOffResumePixels`, the pressure-exit target moved 60 → 50 px, and the stand-off halt and cadence cases assert `StandOffEngagePixels` / `CommitRangePixels`.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-STORY-AERIAL-REACH** — the Story aerial string boxes take the grounded reach, front-only (D6b) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D6b):** "Story air attacks. Lengthen the Story aerial string boxes to match the sim's aerial reach, which reuses the grounded boxes: far edge 1.6 / 1.7 / 2.1 u × width%. Make them front-only (no reach behind the hero). Keep their vertical coverage. Put the table in `BasicComboRules` beside the grounded table. Close the ledger row the bug batch added." This supersedes `design-godot.md` `:1089` (F09's "shorter reach" for aerials, now true in neither mode) and `:1099`–`:1101` (aerial boxes placed from a per-frame `Marker2D`), and satisfies `:1082` (aerial hitboxes authored in `BasicComboRules`) for the vertical span and `:1375` (front-only). | `PlayerController.ResolveAerialStringBoxPixels`: an aerial string hit's **x-span is its grounded twin's**, from `ResolveGroundStringBoxPixels` unchanged (the profile's width % and the Story AttackRange minor scale it exactly as they scale the grounded box); its **vertical span is Story's pre-D6b aerial span**, now authored in `BasicComboRules.AerialStringBoxCentreYUnits` (centre −46 / −50 / −12 px from the feet, Y down) and `AerialStringBoxHeightUnits` (64 / 82 / 104 px, × `ReachHeightPercent` × the minor about the centre), so template coverage stays hit 1 −78 … −14, hit 2 −91 … −9, hit 3 −64 … +40 px. `OnAttackActiveStarted` reads the facing at active start, as for the grounded box; `AerialComboHitboxSizes` / `AerialComboHitboxOffsets`, the `_aerialHitboxMarker` field, its swing-start write in `StartComboHit` and the factory's `AerialHitboxMarker` node are deleted. Aerial far edges h1 / h2 / h3, before → after (hit-3 near edge): Lincoln (85 %) 79.9 / 70.6 / 54.4 → 81.6 / 86.7 / 107.1 px (−20.4 → +15.3); Joan (90) 84.6 / 74.7 / 57.6 → 86.4 / 91.8 / 113.4 (−21.6 → +16.2); Tesla, Tubman (100) 94 / 83 / 64 → 96 / 102 / 126 (−24 → +18); Mozart, Shakespeare (105) 98.7 / 87.2 / 67.2 → 100.8 / 107.1 / 132.3 (−25.2 → +18.9); Einstein (110) 103.4 / 91.3 / 70.4 → 105.6 / 112.2 / 138.6 (−26.4 → +19.8); Leonardo (115) 108.1 / 95.5 / 73.6 → 110.4 / 117.3 / 144.9 (−27.6 → +20.7); Cleopatra (120) 112.8 / 99.6 / 76.8 → 115.2 / 122.4 / 151.2 (−28.8 → +21.6). With a minor both edges move with the helper (Leonardo +8 %: hit 1 far 110.4 → 119.2, near 27.6 → 29.8). Template aerial pivot reach against a Standard mob (23 px hurtbox half-width) 117 / 106 / 87 → 119 / 125 / 149 px, equal to the grounded string. The Level 13 Mirror clone's Story aerial strings lengthen the same way (116 / 122 / 146 px against the hero's 20 px half-width, template), matching the sim reach its Fighter CPU table assumes; no CPU code changed. Unchanged: the aerial frame data, the payloads (damage, hitstun, knockback, launch), the directional boxes, M1 line of sight (a longer box casts a longer ray, so expect more refusals at steps) and the cleave (a wider aerial box cleaves more mobs). `StoryAerialStringReachTests` pins it in real swings (both cases fail on the pre-D6b code). | User | A visual check that the aerial poses read at the new reach (the art-to-box gap widens most on aerial hit 3), and a hands-on air pass. |
| **USER-FINISHER-CARRY** — a string finisher lands its mob about one step outside the hero's reach (D6a) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D6a):** "Walk-back after finishers.
