Lincoln stays the longest carry. Measure before and after." **Addendum (user, 2026-10-11):** "Elites also get the shorter finisher carry (D6a)." Orchestrator default: Lincoln leads in final landing distance (spec D's heavy clause), and fast melee mobs landing one step out and walking back are accepted. *Definitions:* **land** = the mob's first floor contact after the finisher (a flyer: its stun's expiry), measured pivot to pivot (group C's `carry` / `land_gap` tick); **one step** = 0.5 u (30 px); **hit-1 reach** = the attacker's live grounded hit-1 far edge (`PlayerController.StoryStringSpanPixels(0)`, width % and the AttackRange minor included) + the mob's hurtbox half-width (Standard 23, Elite 29 px). Supersedes `S1-STUNNED-KNOCKBACK-FRICTION`'s single 600 px/s² air drag for finisher flights, and V7.4's pressure exit at stun expiry, which for a finisher flight runs at touchdown. | `EnemyController.ArmLaunch` (from `OnHurtboxHit`, after the stun step, only when the hit wrote knockback and left the mob stunned): a Basic `combo_3` hit authored to launch, on a **Standard or Elite** `EnemyController`, whose `SourceActorId` resolves to a `PlayerController`, arms a **finisher flight**: target = own hit-1 reach + 30 px, or for an authored finisher force (`BasicAttackKnockback` × the profile's finisher tenths / 10) ≥ 18 (only Lincoln's 22.5) at least the widest profile's hit-1 reach (template 96 px × `BasicComboRules.WidestStringReachWidthPercent` 120 % + the half-width) + 30 + 15 px; carry = max(0, target − the gap at the finisher); deceleration `v²/(2(carry + v/120))` from the launch speed the hit wrote (`StoryEnemyCombatRules.FinisherCarryDeceleration`), applied as `max(S1, it)` in `ApplyStunnedKnockbackFriction`, with S1's edge cancel after it. A ground mob still airborne when its stun expires keeps flying with its **AI suspended** (no chase step, commit, band, facing or blink; the getup armor still arms) and its pressure exit deferred to touchdown; a 120-frame post-stun cap backs it. The flight ends on touchdown, a stun expiry while grounded or flying, any knockback write (`TryApplyKnockback` clears it; the hit re-arms), an Armored Recovery trip, death, a grab or a throw, the rewind freeze and the pool and rewind resets. Not affected: bosses, Lincoln's launching hit 2, the Up-Attack and Down-Air, Specials, Ultimates, throws, and any payload with no resolvable hero. **Measured** (`StoryFinisherCarryTests`, real strings in real physics frames, Normal, full-HP aggroed `chrono_slasher` at pushbox contact; pivot-to-pivot landing and its offset from the hero's own hit-1 reach, **before → after**): Einstein 102.0 (−26.6) → 158.6 (+30.0); Joan 165.7 (+56.3) → 139.4 (+30.0); Leonardo 82.4 (−51.0) → 159.9 (+26.5); **Lincoln 361.8 (+257.2) → 183.5 (+78.9)**; Cleopatra 61.5 (−76.7) → 157.8 (+19.6, S1-limited); Tesla 82.4 (−36.6) → 147.0 (+28.0); Shakespeare 102.0 (−21.8) → 153.8 (+30.0); Mozart 71.8 (−52.0) → 153.9 (+30.1); Tubman 107.5 (−11.5) → 149.1 (+30.1). Before, every aggroed slasher steered back mid-air after its stun; after, none does. Touchdown 61–76 frames after the finisher connects, against an armor end of 67–69. Einstein tip-range string on a passive slasher 353.0 (+224.4) → 158.7 (+30.1); close string on a `laser_rifle_infantry` (ranged primary) 233.7 (+105.1) → 158.0 (+29.4). **Elites** (`chrono_guard_elite`, resistance zeroed): Joan 135.6 (+20.2) → 134.5 (+19.1, S1-limited); Lincoln 196.7 (+86.1, steered back) → 182.8 (+72.2, no steering). Lincoln lands farthest from a close string (184 px; next Leonardo 160), but his finisher-only displacement (81 px) is not the longest, because his launching hit 2 has already carried the mob to 102 px (spec D §7.1). Bot "before" (`bots_before.md`, pooled, `play`): re-approach after a finisher median / p90 80 / 166 frames on Normal, 84 / 156 on Easy; Standard finisher carry / land gap median 189 / 149 px (Normal), 217 / 114 px (Easy). **After** (2026-10-12, `absolum_research/flow/bots_after.md`, every combat-flow group in the tree, seeds 0–3 pooled, the landing measured from where the hero stood at the finisher): Normal standards land 256 → 116 px from Joan (her target 116) and 269 → 131 px from Mozart (target 131), elites alike (Joan 205 → 116, Mozart 198 → 130), with 81–90 % of landings within ±30 px of the target (2–13 % before); the Standard finisher carry median is 189 → 53 px (`play`); the re-approach median / p90 is 80 / 166 → 55 / 123 frames on Normal `play` (batch ranges 64–93 → 54–58) and 84 / 156 → 82 / 146 on Easy `play` (within the batch spread). **Side effect:** the bots' next swing after a finisher connects less often (Normal `play` 94.6 → 87.8 %, `confirm` policy 93.5 → 79.7 %), because they re-swing about 47 frames after the connect while the mob touches down 60–75 frames after it, one step out; those whiffs are grounded hit 1s at a median gap of 30–31 px. A player who swings on rhythm may feel the same (spec D §7.2–7.3). | User | A hands-on pass confirms "about one step" on close and tip-range strings; group C's Easy and Normal `play` re-approach medians are re-measured after (done 2026-10-12, above); a ruling on which "carry" Lincoln must lead (landing distance, implemented, or finisher-only displacement). |
| **USER-MOB-MELEE-RANGE** — melee mobs commit and hold inside their own reach (D6c) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D6c):** "Mobs stay in range. Cap a melee mob's commit and stand-off distance at its own melee hitbox reach minus a margin, so it stops swinging at air. Applies to MeleeStrike and ChargeDash primaries only. Ranged and AreaPulse attacks are unchanged." | `EnemyController`: when the attack the mob will throw next (`PreviewNextAttack()`, a side-effect-free preview of `SelectNextAttack`, both now reading one `ResolveNextAttack`) is its own MeleeStrike / ChargeDash primary (authored, or the legacy synthesized 40 px melee), it **commits** only when the target is horizontally within `C = min(AttackRangePixels, pivot reach − 10)` (pivot reach = `\|HitboxOffset.X\| + HitboxSize.X/2` + a ChargeDash's travel + the hero's 20 px hurtbox half-width) **and** its box's height band overlaps the hero's 64 px hurtbox; while it **can** commit it never holds (walks in to C); while it **cannot** (cooldown running, or the hero above or below its box) it holds in a band on the horizontal gap engaged at `max(0.85 C, own pushbox half + 18 + 4 + 4.5 v/60)` and released at that × 1.10 / 0.85, so it rests outside pushbox contact and inside its reach. The V7.4 pressure exit and the Armored Recovery answer use the same test: out of reach, the pressure exit falls through to Chase and the armored answer advances armored in Chase and swings the moment the target is in reach, still inside its armored window, ignoring the primary's cooldown and with no reaction delay, as the in-reach answer does (with no target it still commits as before). Every other attack (projectile primaries, AreaPulse, every elite or secondary ability), the engage blink, the reactive Phase Skip and `BossController` keep `AttackRangePixels`. Roster (spec D §1.3 table): the 16 melee primaries now commit at 60–77 px (e.g. `chrono_slasher` 90 → 60, `unbound_eraser` 180 → 77) and rest at 41–52 px. Bot "before" (`bots_before.md`, Normal): mob melee whiffs 64.1 % (`play`), 98 % of them out of reach; **after** (2026-10-12, `bots_after.md`, pooled): 11.7 % (`play`), 18.0 % (`basics`), 25.0 % (`spam`), Easy `play` 30.2 %; near misses at activation (a 1–40 px gap, the stand-off signature) per 100 swings: `chrono_slasher` 39.0 → 2.5, `lance_jab` 19.8 → 8.3, `cyber_cavalry_commander` 28.6 → 4.0; the whiffs left are mostly 41 px or more (the hero left during the wind-up); Normal mob melee swings fell 9.76 → 5.68 per run while their connects rose 3.50 → 4.69, the pressure rise spec D predicted. Limitations (recorded): the commit is measured against the hero's 40 × 64 hurtbox, so against Cleopatra's 36 px sand decoy it is 2 px generous, inside the margin; a mob does not commit at an airborne hero wholly above its box; a flying mob's diagonal approach can overshoot its band by up to √2×, with the pushbox as the net; nine elites still run the legacy 40 × 40 placeholder primary. | User | Group C3's mob-swing telemetry shows the stand-off whiffs gone (an "after" batch; met at bot level 2026-10-12, above); a hands-on pass. |
| **USER-STORY-WALL-BOUNCE** — a launched or thrown mob bounces off a wall (D9) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D9):** "Trigger: a mob launched into an Environment wall faster than ~300 px/s horizontally. Effect: it bounces back at 40% of that speed; it takes 10% of the launching hit's damage as impact damage, once per launch. Scope: Story only, never Fighter." **Addendum (user, 2026-10-11):** "Thrown standard mobs wall-bounce (D9), just like launched ones." | `EnemyController`: a player-dealt (`IsPlayerDealt`), launching hit that writes knockback on a non-phasing, still-stunned Standard or Elite arms one bounce (`ArmLaunch`), its impact `ScaleIntakeDamage(intake, 0.1)` of the hit's pre-defense Story intake (half away from zero, at least 1); an Ultimate launch's impact also ignores the bubble. `LaunchThrown(velocity, bowling, throwDamage)` arms the throw's own bounce, its impact a tenth of the throw's damage (`PlayerController.ResolveStoryThrow` now passes it). After each `MoveAndSlide` (the stunned / finisher-flight step, and `ProcessThrownFlight`), a latched mob with `IsOnWall()`, a pre-move horizontal speed **> 300 px/s**, a facing wall normal (`normal.x × sign(v) ≤ −0.7`) **and** the existing body-centre wall ray (`HasWallAhead`, so a floor seam or step lip never counts) is set to `−0.4 ×` its pre-move speed (vertical kept, the current deceleration kept) and takes the impact once through the private `TakeDamage` — defenses once there (RadiantBurn, the bubble), frontal reduction never (no origin), no stun, knockback, hitstop, poise, meter, Rally, landed-hit report or Punish multiplier; a lethal impact dies normally. It floats an untinted number and shakes like a non-launching direct hit. The latch clears with the stun's natural expiry (a finisher flight keeps it to touchdown; a throw keeps it to its landing), any knockback write, the bounce itself, and every reset the D6a flight has. Never: the Fighter sim, `BossController`, the Mirror clone, the hero, a phaser, a non-launching shove, a mob whose stun has ended outside a finisher flight. Impact sizes: a string finisher deals 1 HP on a standard or elite (Lincoln 2 on a standard), a Special launch more; spec D §1.4 tabulates the bounce radii (after D6a the slasher's is 52–81 px from a string finisher). | User | A hands-on pass near walls; a ruling on the open items in spec D §7 (impact size, bounce radius). |
| **PROVISIONAL-SPACING-WALLS-RULES** — the details D6 and D9 left open | D6b gives the far edge, front-only, the kept vertical coverage and the table's home only. D6a gives "about one step outside hit-1 reach", the untouched 4.5× finisher, "Lincoln stays the longest carry" and (addendum) elites too; D6c gives "cap a melee mob's commit and stand-off distance at its own melee hitbox reach minus a margin", MeleeStrike / ChargeDash primaries only; D9 gives "~300 px/s", 40 %, 10 % of the launching hit's damage once per launch, Story only, never Fighter, and (addendum) thrown standards too. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (spec D §1.2 and §6).* **(2) D6b:** (a) the aerial **near** edge is the grounded near edge (0.4 / 0.3 / 0.3 u × width % × the AttackRange minor, as the reach-geometry step scales the grounded box), not a near edge pinned at the pivot; (b) the facing is read at active start, so a hero who turns during the aerial startup strikes in front of its new facing (the swing-start latch would otherwise strike behind, breaking front-only); (c) the vertical span keeps the pre-D6b Story pixels exactly, and the minor scales the aerial height about its centre because it scales the grounded height (`PROVISIONAL-REACH-MINOR-SHAPE` (a)); (d) the span is stored in world units as the authored pixels over 60, with the centre measured **Y down from the feet** (Story's convention), unlike the grounded table's Y-up offset from the hurtbox centre, because nothing but Story reads it; (e) `PlayerController.StoryStringSpanPixels(step)` is a read-only seam returning a hero's live (near, far) string span in pixels, for sub-pass (ii). **(1) D6a** (sub-pass (ii), 2026-10-11): (a) the landing target is the attacker's live hit-1 pivot reach + 30 px; (b) the heavy-finisher clause — an authored finisher force ≥ 18 lands the mob at least the widest profile's hit-1 reach + 30 + 15 px — is "Lincoln stays the longest carry", read as the farthest landing from the same gap; (c) the carry is realised as the deceleration `max(S1, v²/(2(D + v/120)))`, which never lengthens a carry S1 already ends sooner (so Cleopatra's light finisher lands at +20, and grounded-first-frame finishers a few px short of target); (d) Standard and, by the user's addendum, Elite mobs, flying standards included (their carry ends at stun expiry); (e) a ground mob's flight, carry and bounce latch outlive the stun until touchdown, with the AI and the pressure exit suspended, behind a 120-frame post-stun cap; (f) no resolvable hero attacker (`SourceActorId` not a `PlayerController`) → S1; Lincoln's launching hit 2, the directional strikes, Specials, Ultimates and throws keep S1. (g) **Orchestrator defaults (`DECISIONS.md` addendum D):** Lincoln leads by final landing distance ((b)), and a fast melee mob that lands one step out and walks straight back is accepted. **(3) D6c:** (a) margin 10 px, measured against the Story hero hurtbox (40 × 64, `StoryCombatRules.HeroHurtbox*Pixels`); (b) the commit test is the horizontal gap ≤ C with the vertical-overlap gate (touching edges do not overlap); the decoy's 36 px hurtbox is measured as the hero's; (c) the stand-off engage is `max(0.85 C, contact + 4 + 4.5 v/60)` with v the unscaled chase speed, released at × 1.10 / 0.85; (d) no hold while the mob can commit; (e) only when the previewed next attack is the melee primary, so an elite whose signature ability is next commits from its authored range and an elite cooldown coming up during the reaction delay after a capped commit fires from the capped distance; (f) a ChargeDash primary's reach adds its dash travel; (g) the engage blink and the elite abilities keep `AttackRange`; (h) an out-of-reach Armored Recovery answer advances armored in Chase with the answer owed (`EnemyController.ArmoredAnswerOwed`) and commits it the moment the target is in reach under the in-reach answer's rules: no cooldown gate, so a trip landing while the primary's cooldown runs still walks in instead of holding at its band, and no reaction delay, so the telegraph opens inside the armored window (2026-10-11 review fix; the first cut routed it through the ordinary commit, which held on a running cooldown and rolled a 30–45-frame reaction delay that outlasted the 0.6 s standard window); the debt lapses with the window, which is not extended, and the ordinary pressure exit and cooldown then apply; death, a stun, a grab, losing the target, the rewind freeze and the pool and rewind resets clear it; with no target it commits as before. **(4) D9:** (a) armed by any player-dealt launch that writes knockback on a stunned, non-phasing `EnemyController`, and (addendum) by every throw of a non-phasing mob; (b) a strict 300 px/s pre-move horizontal speed, a facing-normal test (`\|nx\| ≥ 0.7`) and the body-centre ray confirm; (c) one bounce and one impact per launch or throw; (d) the bounce keeps the vertical speed and the current deceleration; (e) impact = 10 % of the pre-defense intake (for a throw, the throw's damage), rounded half away from zero, minimum 1, then defenses once (an Ultimate launch ignores the bubble); (f) no intake scale, stun, hitstop, poise, meter, Rally or Punish effect; the F9 number (untinted) and the non-launching shake; (g) phasers, bosses, the hero and the Mirror clone excluded. Test seams: `EnemyController.IsInFinisherFlight`, `FinisherCarryDeceleration`, `LastFinisherLandingTargetPixels`, `IsWallBounceArmed`, `LastWallImpactDealt`, `LastWallBouncePreMoveVelocityX`, `PreviewNextAttack()`, `CommitRangePixels`, `PrimaryCommitRangePixels`, `StandOffEngagePixels`, `StandOffResumePixels`, `ArmoredAnswerOwed` (and the internal `SetAttackCooldownForTest`). | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a playtest that leaves them alone. `StoryAerialStringReachTests`, `StoryFinisherCarryTests`, `StoryWallBounceTests`, the D6c cases in `EnemyControllerTests` / `EnemyRosterContentTests` and `StoryEnemyCombatRulesTests` pin today's rules. |

## Confirmed open deviations — level fixes and input (gamepad Time Freeze on L3, dust never expires, a Restoration Font in every level), 2026-10-11

User-directed Story input rule from `DECISIONS.md` D11, built on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`) by spec E's Pass A, on top of the gamepad-defaults step (`VERIFY-GAMEPAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD` (b), `PROVISIONAL-GAMEPAD-OVERRIDE-NO-MIGRATION`). It is **Story-only**: Time Freeze has no `GameplayButtons` bit, so nothing under `scripts/FighterSim/` was touched and no component, snapshot field, input-frame bit, protocol, save field, schema version or translation key changed; no Fighter hash moved. One English value changed (`tutorial_step_time_freeze`). Spec E's later passes (D12, mob dust never expires; D13, a Restoration Font in every level) append their rows here. **Pass B (D12, 2026-10-11)** added the four dust rows after `USER-GAMEPAD-TIME-FREEZE-L3`. It is Story-only too: `ChronalDustPickup`, `LevelManager`, `StoryDropSystem`, `StoryManager` (`LastLevelDustLost`), `LevelRewardDirectory` (the duplicate `LastTierBonusDust` deleted) and the two results call sites (`StoryLevelControllerBase.PresentCompletion`, `Level01Controller`); no save field, schema version, translation key, component or Fighter hash changed. Tests: +5 `[TestCase]` (`StoryDropsAndRewindTests` +2, `StoryLevelControllerBaseTests` +2, `SummonRewardProvenanceTests` +1), 3001 → 3006, all five failing against the pre-D12 behaviour. **The D12 review fix-up (2026-10-11)** re-fixed the results' losses half (a same-attempt reload zeroed the in-memory loss tally, the exit fee was never recorded, and the dust held across a reload was on no line) and added `PROVISIONAL-RESULTS-CARRIED-DUST` after the fixed row: `StoryManager` (`RecordAttemptDustLoss`, `LevelDustCarriedIn` / `LastLevelDustCarriedIn`, the carry in `RestoreAttemptStateFromSave`, the exit fee in `CommitOrderlyExit`), `LevelRewardDirectory.ClearDustLoss`, `LevelResultsPanel` (the `DustCarried` line and a ten-argument `ShowResults`) and both results call sites. One translation key was added (`results_dust_carried`) and one English value changed (`results_dust_losses`); no save field, schema version, component or Fighter hash changed. Tests: +3 `[TestCase]` (`StoryLevelControllerBaseTests` +1, `CompletionOnlyBankingTests` +2), 3006 → 3009, all three failing against the step's code; `CompletionBanksLiveDustPickupsFirstAndNothingPaysAfterIt` now pins the campaign level first, so its post-completion kill resolves against Chicago's ledger and pins the kill guard whatever test ran before it (no count change). **Pass C (D13, 2026-10-11)** added the four Restoration Font rows after `PROVISIONAL-RESULTS-CARRIED-DUST`. It is Story-only too: `RestorationFont` (the `Create` factory, the `restoration_font` group, the `RefusesWhile` predicate, the `ChannelRadial`, the F10 commit/credit/cancel calls and the `EventBus.OnPlayerDied` death hook), `InteractionArea.RefreshPrompt` (now public), `StoryManager` (the font commit writes the wallet; a Timeline Collapse clears a pending heal), `StoryLevelControllerBase` (the `RestorationFontPosition` hook), the fourteen Level 2–15 controllers, `Level01Controller`, `Level00Controller` (the font built at the Combat Trial, plus the named checkpoint and wall constants and the `BeginCombatTrialForTesting` seam) and `PlaytestRunner.RouteInteract`. One English value changed (`font_channel_prompt`); no translation key, save field, schema version, component or Fighter hash changed. Tests: +11 `[TestCase]` (`StoryHealingLoopTests` +7, `SecretCachePlacementTests` +1, `Level01ContentTests` +1, `Level00FlowSceneTests` +1, `Level02ContentTests` +1), 3009 → 3020; the two existing font cases in `StoryHealingLoopTests` gained slot −1 and pending-heal hygiene in place (no count change). **The D13 review fix-up (2026-10-12)** moved three fonts out of a hazard's reach (Florence, Levels 7 and 14), made the hazard clearance a tested rule (the shared non-suite test helper `FontHazardClearance`) and added the attempt-level death cancel in `ChronalRewindManager.OnPlayerDied`: +1 `[TestCase]` (`StoryHealingLoopTests.ADeathCancelsAPendingHealThatNoFontIsTracking`), 3020 → 3021, with four existing font cases tightened in place. The user row `USER-RESTORATION-FONT-EVERY-LEVEL` was added at the finalize step so that D13 has its own user row; the agent-chosen half stays in `PROVISIONAL-FONT-PLACEMENT`.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-GAMEPAD-TIME-FREEZE-L3** — the gamepad Time Freeze default is L3 (D11) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D11):** "Gamepad Time Freeze moves to L3 (`JoyButton.LeftStick` = 7). The bug batch has already moved Movement to LB (9), Special 2 to RB (10), and the Ultimate chord to LB+RB." **Supersedes:** `design-godot.md` :405, the Level 0 calibration lesson ("`gameplay_time_freeze` (**R / Back / Select**)"); :2110, Time Freeze's input line ("**R** / **Back or Select**"); :2290, the Controls table ("`TimeFreeze` … `Back` / `Select`"); and `COMFORT_SETTINGS.md` C01c :104 ("R / Back or Select"). | `project.godot` `gameplay_time_freeze`: the joypad event is `button_index` 7 (was 4, Back, inherited from the retired rewind); the keyboard default, physical keycode 82 (`R`), is unchanged, and nothing else in the gamepad layout moved. Button 7 was bound to nothing, so L3 collides with no authored action; Back (4) and Guide (5) now carry no authored action. The runtime path is unchanged: `TimeFreezeController` polls `Input.IsActionJustPressed(InputManager.Actions.TimeFreeze)` on every device, and a human L3 press freezes the Level 13 Mirror clone as Back did (its input source cannot press Time Freeze). Pinned by `GamepadDefaultBindingTests.TimeFreezeDefaultsToTheLeftStickClickAlone` (the enum value 7, the authored joypad half exactly `[7]`, keyboard `R` kept; fails on the old `[4]`); `GamepadOverrideRepairTests` was rewritten in place for the new defaults (no count change). **No migration** (`PROVISIONAL-GAMEPAD-OVERRIDE-NO-MIGRATION`): a profile with no Time Freeze row inherits L3 at once; a saved Time Freeze row, even from a keyboard-only rebind, keeps what it carried (Back works). The Controls tab labels the bind "Pad Button 7" through `InputBindingService.Describe` (there is no glyph table, a pre-existing C01c gap), and "Reset to default" reads `project.godot`, so it restores L3. The Level 0 objective `tutorial_step_time_freeze` now reads "Press Time Freeze to stop time, then slip past and reach the marker" (was "Press R …", wrong on a pad); it names the action, not a button, because `Describe` would render "Pad Button 7" and would show one device's binding to a player on the other. | User (design amendment) | The GDD master and the C01c contract amended at the four lines cited and re-synced, and one pad session confirming that L3 does not fire by accident during ordinary or hard stick movement (a stray Time Freeze spends its 45 s cooldown). |
| **USER-DUST-NEVER-EXPIRES** — a mob dust pickup is collected at its timer (D12) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D12):** "A mob dust pickup that reaches its 10-second expiry is auto-collected into the wallet instead of vanishing. Its claim is committed, as for a normal collection. The authored 720 / 1,000 totals become reachable." **Supersedes** `design-godot.md` :891 (the pool table's "10s idle expiration" for Chronal Dust). **Narrows** `DUST_ECONOMY.md` :12 ("Missed pickups … can reduce income"): a mob pickup can no longer be missed by waiting. | `ChronalDustPickup._PhysicsProcess` calls `Collect()` when a pickup with `NeverExpires == false` (every kill drop, `Source == Mob`) reaches `AutoCollectSeconds` (10 s; renamed from `ExpirationTime`, value unchanged). Collection is unchanged: `LevelRewardDirectory.CommitClaim(SourceID)`, `RaiseChronalDustCollected` (wallet and the level's tally), `RaiseDustAwardCollected { Source = Mob }`, the pickup cue and the HUD `+N` float, then the pool. The 150 px magnet and its speed are unchanged. Boss, Extractor and Secret pickups never had a timer. Pinned by `StoryDropsAndRewindTests.AMobPickupThatReachesItsTimerIsCollectedIntoTheWalletWithItsClaimCommitted` (one award of 4, Mob attribution, the claim committed, idempotent afterwards; the old code returned it to the pool unpaid). | User (design amendment) | The master and `DUST_ECONOMY.md` amended at :891 and :12, and a thorough-route playthrough (or a bot batch) showing no mob source left Issued at completion. |
| **PROVISIONAL-DUST-SETTLE-RULES** — the details D12 left open | D12 names the timer collection and the committed claim; nothing else. The 2026-10-11 addendum's orchestrator default (spec E question 1): "Boss, Extractor and Secret pickups still on the ground at the seal are paid automatically." | *Provisional, agent-chosen (spec E §1 D12, revision 3, with the addendum applied):* (1) **Timer and magnet.** The timer, the magnet and the 150 px radius are unchanged. The timer pauses wherever the pickup is frozen (Time Freeze, the death rewind including its pre-hold and playback, the R03 hold, the collapse beat; `story_loot` takes every suspension latch) and under tree pause, and resumes with its remaining time (`StoryDropsAndRewindTests.AMobPickupsTimerPausesUnderTimeFreezeAndResumesWhereItStopped`: 300 live, 900 frozen and 290 live ticks pay nothing, then it pays within 40). A dead hero whose world is not yet suspended may still auto-collect into the in-memory wallet (harmless: Smothered persists before presentation, Restart clears the wallet, Quit reloads from save). (2) **Completion settles every pickup on the ground.** `LevelManager.CompleteLevel` (the one chokepoint the base controller, Florence and the Tutorial all reach) calls `ChronalDustPickup.SettleOutstandingDust(level root)` **before** it sets the new scene-local `LevelManager.IsComplete` and raises `OnLevelComplete`: every live pickup under the level (in the tree, not pool-parked, not already collected) is `Collect()`ed, **mob drops and, per the addendum, boss, Extractor and Secret awards alike**. Their dust therefore counts toward the tier bonus, the deposit and the results lines. **This amends `DUST_ECONOMY.md` :76** ("commits actually collected/retained rewards … Boss defeat still spawns its existing physical pickup and does not auto-credit or deposit it"): the seal now collects whatever is still on the ground. (3) **Nothing pays or issues after completion.** `ChronalDustPickup.Collect()` is idempotent (a `_collected` latch, cleared only by `OnSpawn`) and releases a pickup **unpaid** once `LevelManager.IsLevelCompleteFor(pickup)` (a complete LevelManager in group `level_manager` whose parent, never the tree root, is an ancestor of the node); `StoryDropSystem.SpawnKillDust` issues nothing once its level is complete. Why: `OnLevelComplete` deposits and zeroes the wallet and then re-keys the reward directory (to the next level, or a reset copy of Level 15's own), and a fresh entry does not clear the wallet, so before this a pickup touched or a mob killed under the results overlay paid into the next level's bank or claimed the next level's first source of its type. A kill landed in the same physics flush as the completion issues nothing (only Level 0 can complete inside a flush, and its ledger pays nothing); a pickup spawned after the seal vanishes unpaid, with no cue. No LevelManager (the Test Arena, unit hosts) reads "not complete", so those contexts behave as before. (4) **Pickup hygiene.** `_lifetime` is field-initialized to 10 s and reset with `_timeFrozen` on despawn; `IsCollected` is a public test seam and the settle filter. Pinned by `StoryLevelControllerBaseTests.CompletionBanksLiveDustPickupsFirstAndNothingPaysAfterIt` (a mob drop and an Extractor award both paid before `OnLevelComplete`; a Secret pickup collected afterwards pays nothing; a later kill spawns nothing) and `SummonRewardProvenanceTests.AKillAfterItsLevelCompletedIssuesNoRewardFromTheNextLevelsLedger` (the Chicago ledger's first source is still the next one issued after a post-completion kill). | Unassigned | The user confirms the seal settle of milestone pickups (then the master and `DUST_ECONOMY.md` :76 say so) or rules that the seal must collect nothing, and a playtest that reads a vanishing post-seal pickup as acceptable (or a ruling to settle it too). |
| **VERIFY-PENDING-MOB-PICKUP-RECOVERY** — mob pickups out at a Collapse, Snap, exit or crash are dropped (pre-existing; recorded with D12) | `DUST_ECONOMY.md` :73 ("Restore an uncollected source/pickup consistently without creating two copies"); `design-godot.md` :2122 and `STORY_PERSISTENCE.md` :31 / :108 ("Restore claimed and pending pickups"). The 2026-10-11 addendum's orchestrator default: "Up to 10 s of mob dust lost on a Collapse, exit or crash is accepted and recorded." | A Timeline Collapse (Acts I–II), an Anchor Snap (Act III), a voluntary exit, a crash or a scene teardown releases outstanding **mob** pickups unpaid (`ReleasePooledContent` / `OnDespawn`). Their sources were only Issued in memory, never saved, so `RestoreClaims` makes them re-issuable, and they pay again only if that encounter is fought again, which forward-only rooms often prevent. The F10 API for preserving them exists (`StoryManager.TryIssueReward` / `IsRewardPendingPickup`) but the kill-drop path has never used it. Restart Level is conformant (it clears everything). D12 shrinks the exposure to at most 10 s of kills (a mob pickup now collects itself at its timer) and leaves the rule itself untouched; that ≤ 10 s exposure is accepted provisionally by the orchestrator's default. Of the milestone pickups, a boss pickup has its own restore (`SealReadiness.RestorePendingBossPickup`) and so does a Secret (`SecretCache.RestorePendingAward`); an uncollected Extractor pickup has none (`ChronalExtractor.RestoreDestroyedState` rebuilds the machine broken and pays nothing), an adjacent pre-existing gap this row also covers. | Unassigned | Kill drops (and Extractor awards) issued through the attempt's reward-source record and restored once on resume, or a user ruling that the exposure stands. |
| ~~**RESULTS-DUST-LINES-AFTER-RESET**~~ — the results panel's tier-bonus, losses and carried dust (fixed 2026-10-11, with D12; the losses half re-fixed by the D12 review fix-up the same day) | F05's six-category itemisation adds the tier bonus and subtracts the losses, and its total equals what the level banked. | **Fixed 2026-10-11.** Before, three faults. (a) `StoryLevelControllerBase.PresentCompletion` and Florence's results call read `LevelRewardDirectory.LastTierBonusDust` / `DustLostThisAttempt` **after** `OnLevelComplete` → `AdvanceToNextLevel` → `ClearLevelAttemptState` → `ResetAttempt()` had zeroed both, so both lines read 0 on every level. (b) The loss tally is in memory only, and every fee is followed by a reload of the attempt before the level can complete (the Collapse's hub-portal resume `RestartCollapsedLevel(true)` → `LoadCurrentLevel` → `BeginLevelRun(resume)`, the Anchor Snap's in-place `_forceResumeNextRun` reload, a slot load after Quit to Main Menu); that reload's `RestoreAttemptStateFromSave` → `ClearLevelAttemptState` → `ResetAttempt()` zeroed the tally, so even after (a) was fixed the losses line read 0 in every slotted run, and the voluntary exit fee was never recorded at all. (c) The per-source category lines are the controllers' scene-local tallies, so the dust the attempt held across that reload appeared on no line, and the panel subtracted a fee from dust it never counted (earn 100, Collapse fee 20, earn 50 more, bonus 13: the level banked 143, the panel showed 50 + 13 − 20 = 43). Now: (a) `StoryManager.CompleteLevelRun` freezes `LastLevelDustLost` and the new `LastLevelDustCarriedIn` beside the other "last level" figures (first in `OnLevelComplete`, before the reset; like them they keep their old value when the run timer was not running), and both panels read them and the existing `LastLevelTierBonusDust` (Florence's bonus stays 0: it is untimed); the duplicate `LevelRewardDirectory.LastTierBonusDust` is deleted. (b) `StoryManager` keys the tally to the paying attempt's GUID (`RecordAttemptDustLoss`, used by `ApplyTimelineCollapseDustPenalty` and now also by `CommitOrderlyExit`, so the exit fee is a loss too), and `RestoreAttemptStateFromSave` carries it across its reset when the parked attempt is that attempt; any other attempt's reload starts from zero, and a fee charged to another attempt restarts the tally rather than adding to it (`LevelRewardDirectory.ClearDustLoss`). (c) `BeginLevelRun` captures `LevelDustCarriedIn` = the wallet at the load + the losses already taken from it, and the panel shows it on a new line (`results_dust_carried`, "Earned before resuming: {0}", hidden when 0) and adds it to the total, so categories + carried + bonus − losses equals the bank (agent choices: `PROVISIONAL-RESULTS-CARRIED-DUST`). Pinned by `StoryLevelControllerBaseTests.TheResultsPanelShowsTheTierBonusAndLossesTheCompletionApplied` (both labels through the real completion path; they read 0 before), `StoryLevelControllerBaseTests.AfterACollapseAndTheAnchorResumeTheResultsAddUpToWhatTheLevelBanked` (a slotted fresh entry, a real Collapse, the anchor resume and the real level's completion: losses 20, carried 100, mobs 50, the total label equal to the deposit), `CompletionOnlyBankingTests.AnAnchorSnapAndAnExitFeeBothReachTheResumedAttemptsResults` (an Act III Snap, a Quit to Main Menu fee and `ResumeCampaign`: losses 36, carried 100, the itemisation equal to the bank) and `CompletionOnlyBankingTests.AnotherAttemptNeverInheritsTheLossTally`; the three new cases fail on the code before the fix-up (the tally zeroed by the reload, the exit fee unrecorded, two attempts' fees summed). | Unassigned | Closed. The split the panel shows after a process restart is `PROVISIONAL-RESULTS-CARRIED-DUST` (4). |
| **PROVISIONAL-RESULTS-CARRIED-DUST** — how the results itemise dust held across a reload (D12 review fix-up) | `DUST_ECONOMY.md` :64: "The results screen itemizes required enemies, boss, Extractors, secret/other optional allocations, losses, and the tier bonus", and F05 wants the total to equal what the level banked. Neither says how dust earned before a Collapse resume, an Anchor Snap or a quit-and-resume is shown when the category tallies are per scene load. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (2026-10-11):* (1) **A seventh line.** `LevelResultsPanel` gains `DustCarried` ("Earned before resuming: {0}", key `results_dust_carried`), placed between the optional and losses lines, shown only when non-zero, and added to the total. Its value is `StoryManager.LastLevelDustCarriedIn`: the wallet when the last load began plus the losses already taken from it, i.e. the gross dust the attempt had earned before that load. It is not split back into categories (the per-source tallies are scene-local; re-deriving them from the claimed reward sources was judged too invasive for this fix-up). **This amends `DUST_ECONOMY.md` :64's six categories.** (2) **The loss tally is in memory, keyed by attempt.** No save field was added (no schema change). The tally survives the same attempt's reloads within one process run, including a Quit to Main Menu and a slot load. (3) **The exit fee is a loss.** `CommitOrderlyExit` records the voluntary 20 % fee (the pause menu's Exit, in a level or at the hub) like the Collapse and Snap fee, as `DustLostThisAttempt` had always documented; the English of `results_dust_losses` changed from "Lost to collapse: {0}" to "Lost to collapse or exit: {0}" (key unchanged). (4) **After a process restart** (quit the game and reload, or a crash) the tally is gone: fees paid before the restart are not itemised on the losses line, and the carried line shows the net dust held instead of the gross. The total still equals the bank. A fresh entry carries the wallet too, which production always enters at 0. | Unassigned | The user accepts the seventh line and its wording (then `DUST_ECONOMY.md` :64 says so) or rules another presentation (for example a per-category carry re-derived from the claimed sources); and either accepts (4) or asks for the tally to persist with the attempt record (a save field, for the next package's single bump). |

Orchestrator defaults (`DECISIONS.md` addendum E): keep the Tutorial font, one font per level, no refusal during boss fights. | Built 2026-10-11 by spec E's Pass C and its 2026-10-12 review fix-up: one font in every campaign level 0–15 (Level 0's only from the Combat Trial on). The positions, the placement and hazard rule, the use rules and the F10 wiring are agent choices, recorded in `PROVISIONAL-FONT-PLACEMENT`. | User | The hand-play of `PROVISIONAL-FONT-PLACEMENT`; the user's rulings on its open questions (the Tutorial font, second fonts for the act finales, a boss-fight refusal). |
| **PROVISIONAL-FONT-PLACEMENT** — a Restoration Font in every campaign level (D13; placement and use rules agent-chosen) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D13):** "Place the designed, already-implemented `RestorationFont` once in each campaign level, as the design calls for (`design-godot.md` ~2131-2132)." Design :2131-2132 (Story Mode Healing Loop): one font per level, "authored between the mid checkpoint and the boss (act finales may author two)"; hold Interact 1.5 s, damage interrupts and refunds, the heal lands over 2 s; Easy 2 × 50 % / Normal 1 × 50 % / Hard 1 × 25 %; the spent state survives rewinds and Collapse. :2135 describes a rhythm, not a use rule. Addendum orchestrator defaults: keep the Tutorial font, one font per level, no refusal during boss fights. | **Before:** `RestorationFont` was complete but nothing placed one. **Now (built 2026-10-11 by spec E's Pass C, uncommitted on `d2e52e0`):** every campaign level 0–15 authors exactly one, built by the new `RestorationFont.Create(fontID, position)` (a placeholder cracked-hourglass body, the gold `Glow` that dims 1.0 → 0.15 with uses, a `RadialProgress` `ChannelRadial` written by the font only, and an `InteractionArea` 110 × 150 with its prompt, the `WardenBeacon` pattern), ID `{DialoguePrefix}.restoration_font`. Levels 2–15 through the new `StoryLevelControllerBase.RestorationFontPosition` override (built right after the secret cache; `PlacedRestorationFont`, `AuthoredRestorationFontPosition`); Florence through `Level01Controller.RestorationFontPosition` (9720, 900; the workshop, past `florence_checkpoint_1`, midway between the second spike run and the scene's `SteamGeyser`); the Tutorial through `Level00Controller.RestorationFontPosition` (4300, 900), **built only when the Combat Trial starts** (never during the home-era hold, the calibration, the mobility gates or the Skip path; agent choice, since the design rule has no meaning in Level 0). Positions (x, floor top): L2 (9180, 900), L3 (8760, 1400), L4 (8200, 900), L5 (9100, 800), L6 (8920, 900), L7 (8700, 880), L8 (8660, 1620), L9 (8150, 1200), L10 (8760, 1120), L11 (9400, 900), L12 (9400, 1900), L13 (6850, 1900), L14 (9850, 1700), L15 (9560, 1400). **Fix-up (2026-10-12, after review):** three of spec E's positions stood in a hazard's reach, because the spec's table missed the scene-authored hazards and treated Level 14's laser lane as a line. Florence's first font (9900, 900) stood inside the scene's `SteamGeyser` (a `CyclicHazardTemplate` at (9920, 890), column x 9860–9980, y 670–890, 15 damage and a (240, −180) knockback every 4 s), so the channel was interrupted every cycle. Level 7's (8800, 880) had `FlagshipFireA`'s column (x 8840–8960, 14 damage every ~4 s) 40 px inside its channel circle. Level 14's (9480, 1700) had Security Core lane 1 (`SecurityCoreBeam1`, column x 9540–9660), which arms in Phases 2–3, inside its circle, and there is no boss-fight refusal; west of the lane the Beacon's 200 px leaves no room, so it moved east, between lanes 1 and 2. That puts it 350 px from the arena edge, inside Archive Prime's 600 px engagement band, as the Level 2 font is inside its boss's band (damage interrupts and refunds). **Rule** (pinned for 2–15 by `SecretCachePlacementTests`, for Florence by `Level01ContentTests`, for the Tutorial by `Level00FlowSceneTests`): standing on a surface whose top is in [y − 2, y + 16]; x ≥ the Middle-role checkpoint + 110; the whole channel circle outside the boss reveal (x ≤ the arena's west edge − (90 + 30); for the Mirror also ≥ its reveal radius + 120); ≥ 110 px from every checkpoint, ≥ 320 px from every Extractor or Resonance Hold node, ≥ 200 px from every other Interact target (Warden Beacons, the secret cache, sealing anchors, Puzzle Reset Stations); and, since the fix-up, the hero's body (40 × 64) anywhere in the 90 px channel circle stays 20 px clear of every damaging hazard's shape: each `StoryCyclicHazard`, armed **or dormant at load** (a phase-armed laser lane included), each `ChronalRiftZone` and each `Spikes_*` strip (the shared test helper `FontHazardClearance`; Florence's case loads the real scene, so its scene-authored geyser is checked; both cases fail on the old Florence and Level 7 positions, the Levels 2–15 case on the old Level 14 one). Preference, applied by the table and not tested: the stretch after the PreBoss anchor, else just before it (Levels 4, 6, 9 and 13 — so on the timed Levels 4, 6 and 9 a timer Collapse can land mid-restore; it now cancels the remainder, see the F10 wiring), never in an escape, flood, searchlight or low-gravity zone. **Exceptions:** Level 12 is low gravity end to end (its font stands on the landing pad, dampers 0.48); Level 13's whole pre-arena route drifts, so its font stands grounded on the shelf. **Spawn proximity (recorded, not tested):** the Level 12 (50 px), 13 (inside wave 3) and 15 (60 px) fonts stand on wave spawn points, and Level 14's stands 300 px from its nearest wave-4 ground spawn (70 px before the fix-up); each wave spawns at a trigger west of its font, so all four are post-wave heals, contested until the wave is cleared. Act finales (5, 12, 15) author one each. **Use rules:** `CanInteract` refuses while the font's own restore runs (on Easy the second channel used to start 1.5 s into the 2 s restore and overwrite it, losing about a quarter of the first heal) and while the new `RefusesWhile` predicate says so — wired to `LevelComplete` (base), `_levelComplete` (Florence, Tutorial), because completion re-keys the use registry to the next level, so a spent font would read fresh and commit into the next attempt; a channel running when it turns true is interrupted with a refund, a paid restore completes; there is **no** boss-fight refusal (a pinned boss can still reach the Level 2 font through its 540 px band; damage interrupts and refunds). **F10 wiring:** the channel now commits through `StoryManager.CommitFontChannelCompletion` (use + pending heal together; it used to call the bare `RecordFontUse`), each paid tick calls `CreditPendingHealing`, and the channeler's death (`EventBus.OnPlayerDied`, delivered even while a rewind's freeze latch holds the font, T01a) interrupts a channel with a refund or cancels a running restore without one (`CancelPendingHealingForRecovery`); before, a hero who died mid-restore and was revived kept receiving the remainder after the thaw. Since the fix-up the death recovery itself also cancels the attempt's pending heal, without refund (`ChronalRewindManager.OnPlayerDied`, the story hero only), so a remainder that a reload restored, which no font is channelling, is cancelled too (`StoryHealingLoopTests.ADeathCancelsAPendingHealThatNoFontIsTracking`). A Timeline Collapse (Acts I–II, `BeginTimelineCollapse`) now clears the pending heal too, before its save, as `BeginAnchorSnap` already did (STORY_PERSISTENCE :77: the Collapse replaces HP, so pending healing is cancelled without refunding the use); a timer Collapse has no death to cancel it, and the collapse save used to persist an active pending heal (`StoryHealingLoopTests.ATimerCollapseCancelsAPendingFontHealWithoutRefundingTheUse`). `InteractionArea.RefreshPrompt` is public and the font refreshes its prompt when a use ends, so the Easy prompt returns without leaving the area. `font_channel_prompt` reads "Hold Interact to channel the wellspring" (was "Hold E …"; key unchanged). `PlaytestRunner.RouteInteract`'s blind-press branch excludes fonts, but that branch finds no area at all today: `FindChildren`'s type filter matches only native and global classes, and `InteractionArea` is neither (pre-existing, left unchanged, because making the branch live changes what the bots press). The fonts are live Collapse Tremor safe zones. Pinned also by `StoryHealingLoopTests` (+7, then +1 in the fix-up) and `Level02ContentTests.TheOrleansFontIsUsableWhereItStandsAndRefusesOnceTheLevelIsComplete` (real physics frames on the real scene). | Unassigned | A hand-play on Easy and Hard confirming each font is found and usable before its boss, including the four post-wave fonts and the two low-gravity ones; and user rulings on the Tutorial font (kept by the addendum's default), second fonts for the act finales (none by default) and a boss-fight refusal (none by default). |
| **VERIFY-FONT-PENDING-HEAL-RESUME** — a pending Font heal is not applied on reload | `STORY_PERSISTENCE.md` :66 / :77: pending healing carries the "remaining live duration", and "repeated loads resume only the uncredited remainder", applied "over its saved duration". | The commit, credit and cancel are wired (D13, above), and a Collapse, an Anchor Snap or a death recovery clears the record (the death since the 2026-10-12 fix-up, at the attempt level in `ChronalRewindManager.OnPlayerDied`: before it, only the channelling font cancelled on a death, so a remainder restored by a reload, which has no channeler, survived a later death and was re-persisted at every checkpoint until a Collapse, a Snap, a font commit or completion). `RestoreAttemptRecord` restores the uncredited remainder into the attempt (`ACompletedFontChannelPersistsItsUseAndItsUncreditedRemainderTogether` pins 18), but **nothing applies it to the hero on load**, so a quit mid-restore loses the remainder while the use stays spent. `CreditPendingHealing` never decrements `RemainingSeconds`. ## The 2026-10-11 user rulings — index, scope decisions and the after-measurement

The combat-flow follow-up — the user's goal, in their words: "more synergy between standard attacks and specials. I want the game to flow better in combat" — was ruled on 2026-10-11 in `DECISIONS.md` (outside this repository, `D:/Projects/absolum_research/DECISIONS.md`, with its addendum) and built on `main`'s working tree the same day (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`). The five sections above hold its rows; this one indexes them, records the rulings that changed no game rule, and records the one finding of the bot after-measurement that needs a ruling. **Every user ruling has a user row:** D1 + D1a → `USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL` (and the amended `USER-ABILITY-LOCKOUT`); D2 (bosses included, addendum) → `USER-PER-HIT-MOMENTUM`; D3 (every basic variant, addendum) → `USER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL`; D4 (no count rule, addendum) → `USER-FIRST-SPECIAL-UNTAXED`; D5 → `USER-STORY-METER-SCALE`; D6a (elites too, addendum) → `USER-FINISHER-CARRY`; D6b → `USER-STORY-AERIAL-REACH`; D6c → `USER-MOB-MELEE-RANGE`; D6d → `USER-BOT-COMBAT-TELEMETRY` (below); D7 and "Not doing now" → `USER-COMBAT-FLOW-NOT-NOW` (below); D8 → `USER-PUNISH-WINDOW`; D9 (thrown mobs too, addendum) → `USER-STORY-WALL-BOUNCE`; D10 → `USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`; the freeze-only Special buffer (addendum) → `USER-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`; D11 → `USER-GAMEPAD-TIME-FREEZE-L3`; D12 → `USER-DUST-NEVER-EXPIRES`; D13 → `USER-RESTORATION-FONT-EVERY-LEVEL`. Every agent choice and every orchestrator default sits in that ruling's `PROVISIONAL-*` row (`PROVISIONAL-HIT-CONFIRM-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`, `PROVISIONAL-FIRST-SPECIAL-TAX-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-PUNISH-WINDOW-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-STORY-METER-SCALE-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-SPACING-WALLS-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-DUST-SETTLE-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-RESULTS-CARRIED-DUST`, `PROVISIONAL-FONT-PLACEMENT`). **Design-master lines owed (owner User; the repository mirror `design-godot.md` was deliberately not edited; line numbers are the mirror's as of 2026-10-12):** D1 / D1a and the freeze buffer — :782, :816, :1499, :3912 and the Cooldown System block from :1106 (the 2026-10-08 lockout's own citations in `USER-ABILITY-LOCKOUT` were taken against an older mirror and have drifted); D2 — :1108, :1352 and the snapshot struct :1766–1767, plus `ROLLBACK_STATE_CONTRACT.md`'s "refund counts"; D3 — :783–785 and :1500; D4, D8 and D9 — new Story content beside Enemy Stagger Discipline (no line states them today); D5 — :1268, :1269 and the build-rate reading of :2793; D6b — :1089 and :1099–1101; D10 — :3514, :4916, :4918 and :4919, plus `HUD_CONTRACT.md` (master and the repo copy); D11 — :405, :2110 and :2290, plus `COMFORT_SETTINGS.md` :104; D12 — :891, plus `DUST_ECONOMY.md` :12 (and :76 and :64 if `PROVISIONAL-DUST-SETTLE-RULES` (2) and `PROVISIONAL-RESULTS-CARRIED-DUST` are accepted). D6a, D6c and D13 need no master line (D13 is what :2132 already asks for). **Test baseline:** 2887 → **3021** (+134 `[TestCase]`s; 2882 → 3021 counting the C1 telemetry's +5; the arithmetic is in `AGENTS.md`'s validation section), three consecutive green full runs on 2026-10-12. **Nobody has played any of it, and nobody has looked at the flash, the fonts or the aerial poses.**

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**USER-BOT-COMBAT-TELEMETRY**~~ — the playtest bots report swings, finishers, meter, Ultimates and refused presses (D6d; **met 2026-10-12**) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D6d):** "Telemetry for the bots (`scripts/Diagnostics/`): per swing: connect or whiff, and the distance to the nearest hurtbox; re-approach frames after a finisher; meter at level start; Ultimates cast; refused Special presses." Orchestrator defaults (addendum C): the `play` bot's Ultimate policy is unchanged; no slotted chained-run option. Diagnostics only: no design line covers bot telemetry. | Built by spec C's packages C1 (with its fix-up) and C3 (2026-10-11): `PlaytestCombatStats.cs` (pure) and `PlaytestTelemetry.cs` report `swings` / `swing_log`, `finishers`, `meter`, `ability_presses` (with the refusal conditions and `lockout_events`) and `mob_swings`; `PlaytestWorld`'s "Hero gates" region reads the group-A seams; the runner gained `--special-press=gated\|confirm` (C3) and `--story-meter-scale=on\|off` (C2); `compare_runs.py`, `trace.py` and `run_playtests.ps1` print it. No gameplay, sim, save or localization change. Used for the before (`absolum_research/flow/bots_before.md`, 144 runs) and after (`bots_after.md`, 144 runs) measurements; every after report reads `connect_basis` `d1a`, `orphan_basic_hits` 0 and `meter.level_start` 0. **Known telemetry limits (not game bugs):** a press is resolved on the next tick, so a Special press the freeze-only buffer fires after the freeze is booked as refused under `hitstop` (the after counts under the `gated` policy are an upper bound); a mob swing that strikes Cleopatra's sand decoy reports `TargetIndex` 0 and counts as connected; seed-0 bot runs are not frame-identical across launches on this machine, so a single batch moves a median by roughly ±15 frames (compare pooled seeded batches). Suites: `PlaytestCombatStatsTests` (6). | User | Met: the telemetry exists and the before / after batches ran. Reopen only for the limits above. |
| **USER-COMBAT-FLOW-NOT-NOW** — what the 2026-10-11 rulings deliberately left out | **User rulings 2026-10-11:** "D7 — No heavy attack in this pass." "Not doing now: a heavy button; Special clash; the visible poise break; elite HP cuts; closing `DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`; the per-kit linker/ender pass; an Absolum-style mana pip system; Chronal Spark tokens; any grid expansion." | None of them was built: there is no heavy button or heavy attack, no Special-versus-Special clash rule, no on-screen poise display, no elite HP change (the bots' elite fights got faster — Normal `play` median 2290 → 1442 frames — but the synthesis's "string-only elite fight above ~2× the `play` fight" trigger for HP cuts is not met: 1.73 → 1.54), sim Specials still fire on press (`DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES` stays open, which is why D3 is Story-only), no per-kit linker/ender tuning, no mana pips, no Chronal Spark tokens and no new grid nodes. | User | A new user ruling reopens any item; until then a change that adds one needs that ruling first. |
| **VERIFY-COMBAT-FLOW-BURST-KILLS** — the combat-flow rules made Story standards die in a burst again | The 2026-10-04 complaint behind `PROVISIONAL-STORY-CONFIRM-PRORATION` (S2): string + both Specials was "basically an insta-kill"; that row's acceptance says "string + S1 + S2 should no longer kill a full-HP standard mob in one cycle while a Special thrown from neutral keeps full value". Against it, the user's 2026-10-11 rulings deliberately speed the string → Special route: D1 (a Special straight off a landed hit 1), D4 (the chain's first Special untaxed) and D2 (per-hit Momentum). | **Measured 2026-10-12** (`absolum_research/flow/bots_after.md`, every combat-flow group in the tree, seeds 0–3 pooled, before = pre-A): standard mobs killed within 2 s of the first hit — Normal `play` 5 → 22 %, `spam` 2 → 18 %, Easy `play` 13 → 27 %, and under the `confirm` press policy (a Special the moment a swing lands) 4 → 37 % (`play`) and 2 → 38 % (`spam`); standard median time to kill Normal `play` 282 → 200 frames, `confirm` `play` 271 → 167. Joan is the extreme: `confirm` `play` 208 → 71 frames (1.2 s), 64 % of her standard kills inside 2 s; Mozart moves far less (373 → 243 frames, 3 → 7 %). The string-only `basics` bot is unchanged (317 → 314). Special-majority kills stay tiny (3–5 %), so the string still finishes most mobs. Completions and deaths did not get worse anywhere (Normal `play` 9 → 11 of 24 completions). Not isolated per ruling: everything acts together. | User | A ruling, after a hands-on session on Normal with Joan and one slower kit: accept the burst as the intended flow (and retire the S2 acceptance sentence), or name what should give — for example the first Special's untaxed damage on a mob already in hitstun, S4's ×1.25 basic multiplier, or standard HP. |

## Historical leads — dispositioned by Package 11

These four rows were opened as **verification candidates** when no game build was available to this docs workspace. Package 11 supplied that evidence and closed all four. **The records are retained rather than deleted**, per this file's own rule.

| ID / area | Historical lead and current target | Disposition (2026-09-13, Package 11) |
|---|---|---|
| ~~VERIFY-01~~ — Time mechanics | The report describes manual rewind, Stasis Echo and a 12-second cooldown. F03 now specifies Story-only escape Time Freeze for 5 seconds, 45-second cooldown after thaw, no charges and continued Integrity drain; death rewind remains separate. | **Confirmed and resolved.** The lead was accurate: the build did carry manual rewind, the Stasis Echo and a 12 s cooldown. Package 11 A2 **deleted** all three — `StasisEcho.cs`, `ChronalRewindManager`'s whole manual/scrub region, `IRewindScrubbable.CancelRewindScrub` and their translation rows — and shipped `TimeFreezeController` to the contract: 5.000 s / 300 ticks, no charges, a 45 s cooldown armed at thaw, identical on all difficulties, legal at zero rewind charges, not lockable by Suppression, and with `chronal_extractor` deliberately excluded from the freeze sweep so Integrity keeps draining. `TimeFreezeTests` (21 cases) plus a source gate that fails if the retired verb's name reappears anywhere in `scripts/` or `tests/`. The death rewind is untouched. |
| ~~VERIFY-02~~ — Integrity and recovery | The report includes siphon shares/restoration paths that F01 retired. Current design uses normalized live drain, fixed starting denominator, no time refill, role-based PreBoss lock and F10/F11 recovery. | **Confirmed and resolved, with one measurement gap left open.** Package 11 A3 deleted the entire siphon model (the 600 px engagement, the 10 s grace, the 10-point share cap and every restoration path) and rewrote `TimelineIntegrityRules` around normalized live drain, difficulty multipliers 2.0/1.5/1.2, a fixed starting-Extractor denominator, tier lines 50/20 and collapse at zero on every difficulty; A3 also shipped role-based checkpoints and the F11 minima, and A3b shipped the F10 attempt record with status-driven load routing. **The code conformity is closed; the route measurement is not** — see the open `VERIFY-PAR-SECONDS` above, which is exactly the "measured route gaps recorded separately from code conformity" this row asked for. |
| ~~VERIFY-03~~ — Dust and reward commits | The report names 15-dust Extractors, unapplied tier bonuses and a wallet-direct Mirror award. F05 now defines budgeted finite rewards, physical boss pickups and once-only completion banking. | **Confirmed and resolved, with one content gap left open.** Package 11 A10 replaced the per-enemy rate card with 25 authored `LevelRewardManifest` resources compiled per difficulty by `LevelRewardDirectory`: an unauthored source pays nothing (retiring "every enemy guarantees dust"), a difficulty changes the distribution but never the level total, the tier bonus is applied, all sixteen bosses pay 25 as physical pickups, and the Mirror Paradox's wallet-direct path is gone. Claims persist per attempt through `ClaimedRewardSourceIDs`, so a respawned enemy cannot reissue a collected reward. **Open:** the discoverable half of every optional pool has no physical source — see `VERIFY-SECRET-CACHE` above. N01's pending boss pickup is `DEFER-SEALING-ANCHOR`. **Package 12 W2 (2026-09-27):** "once-only completion banking" is now literally true — H02 moved every deposit into `StoryManager.CommitCompletionTransaction` for all levels and deleted the hub's arrival and Repository deposits; the nine Level 4A manifests now bind `.eraser_debut` to `unbound_eraser`, the enemy the trigger actually spawns (GAP-02, 15-dust collection walk pinned in `RewardManifestTests`); and `SummonMinions` spawns carry `IsSummoned` so a summon can no longer consume a skipped mandatory source (GAP-04). Still open: `VERIFY-SECRET-CACHE`, and the Eraser's interim `ChronalDustDrop` (D15, held). |
| ~~VERIFY-04~~ — Content and onboarding | The report describes deferred Open stages/drills and missing placements; current decisions require launch drills, Open stages before Fighter tuning, and 24 campaign level/boss slots including nine Legacy routes. | **Confirmed and resolved as coverage; unreviewed as experience.** Package 11 A9 re-authored Paris, Vesuvius and Nassau as Open stages with real, reachable pits and floor-segment ledges — before any Fighter balance pass, as V7.6 2.D requires. A11 shipped F18's six Calibration Drills with a standalone main-menu route available on a first boot with no Story save. A12 plus B1/B2/B3 authored all nine Level 4A routes with their own bosses under `resources/Bosses/legacy/`, taking the campaign to 24 non-tutorial boss encounter slots (15 shared + 9 Legacy) and seventeen route slots. A7a placed the Eraser and gave the Chrono-Warden its first spawn sites. **This row's own caution still bites in the other direction:** coverage is proved by test, but **nobody has played a drill, a 4A variant or an Open-stage match**, so nothing here is validated as an experience. Production art for all of it remains Package 10 (`PRODUCTION_SCOPE.md`). |

| `docs/PACKAGE5_CAMPAIGN_PLAN.md` | ~~Missing from this checkout~~ — **corrected 2026-09-13 (Package 11 Phase C): the file IS present in the game repository**, at that exact path, complete with its per-level dossiers, the authored boss stat table (§4.1) and a long §9 deviation log. The "unavailable source" flag was a property of the isolated docs workspace, not of the project. | **No recovery work is needed.** The flag in the design master should be corrected the next time the master is edited. Earlier claims of authored Levels 2–15 are verifiable and were verified: all sixteen campaign scenes exist and are covered by per-level content suites. |
| Game code/resources, scenes, builds and test reports | ~~Not available here~~ — **corrected 2026-09-13.** Package 11 was implemented directly in the game repository against a pinned baseline (`31fed14`, 1638 passing tests, clean tree), with nine reconnaissance dossiers reading the build in full and twenty workstreams verifying their own areas. The confirmed entries in the Package 11 table above carry file identifiers, branch names and test evidence. | Keep pinning a revision on every new entry. The evidence standard this row asked for is now met and should stay met: a resource path alone is still not proof that the game loads or applies the value. |

If an original document is later recovered, retain its repository/archive location and immutable commit/release or content hash plus date. Reconcile it against current accepted rules before using it as current authority. Do not revive old reward budgets, checkpoints, time mechanics or retired content just because they appear in a pinned historical file.

P04 delivers the authority rule and ledger structure. **Package 11 (2026-09-13) delivered the first build verification against it**, closing VERIFY-01 through VERIFY-04 and opening ten confirmed/deferred entries in their place. The listed implementation and content work remains pending as itemized above.

**How to use this file.** Before assuming a shipped number is the target, search it here. Before deferring something, add a row with a stable ID, the intended behaviour and its decision reference, the observed value with its pinned commit and file identifier, an owner (`Unassigned` unless claimed), and acceptance criteria concrete enough that someone else can tell when it is done. Never delete a closed row — disposition it in place.
